**Mains hook:** State power can coerce lawfully; the ethical question is whether authority, necessity and proportionality are recorded and checkable (qualified).
**Back:** *"Coerce only by law, need, and proportion, with reasons written down."*

### Flashcard ETH-1070 — Attitude strength
**Front:** What makes an attitude resistant to persuasion and predictive of behaviour?
**Definition (learn):** Attitudes held with high certainty, personal relevance and frequent rehearsal resist persuasion and predict behaviour better than weakly held ones.
**Breakdown:**
- Relevant to why some officials resist corrupt pressure and others succumb.
- Strength improves but does not guarantee prediction; situational and institutional pressure can still override.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 03 Advanced §2 (analytical; standard attitude-strength concept).
**Links:** ETH-1067, ETH-1071, ETH-1066.
**Trap:** Exam option "strongly held attitudes always predict behaviour" is false.
**Mains hook:** Build attitude strength through repeated, personally relevant practice (case work, integrity drills), then reinforce by institutional rules (qualified).
**Back:** *"Strong, personal, rehearsed attitudes resist; still not a guarantee."*

### Flashcard ETH-1071 — How attitudes form
**Front:** Name the three routes by which attitudes form, per the owner.
**Definition (learn):** Direct experience; social learning (family, peers, media); and reinforcement, where repeated positive or negative outcomes attach evaluative charge to an object.
**Breakdown:**
- Direct experience: a field posting that shows citizen hardship.
- Social learning: group norms and media.
- Reinforcement: repeated rewards or penalties.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 §3 (theory-grounded, analytical application).
**Links:** ETH-1033 (value channels), ETH-1070, ETH-1072.
**Trap:** Attitudes form through experience and learning, not only through reasoning.
**Mains hook:** Reform of attitudes needs control of the experiences and reinforcers that officials actually encounter (qualified).
**Back:** *"Experience, company, reward: how attitudes are built."*

### Flashcard ETH-1072 — Social media ethical dilemmas (2025 GS-IV Q1(a))
**Front:** What specific ethical dilemmas does the owner expect for the 2025 social-media question?
**Definition (learn):** Social media's dilemmas in the "present digital age": privacy and data harvesting; misinformation and deepfakes; algorithmic amplification and echo chambers; attention-capture design; anonymity and accountability for harm; and the state's own use of the same channel.
**Breakdown:**
- Anchor one to a dated Indian instrument: IT Rules due-diligence obligations, or DPDP Act, 2023 for consent (owner Advanced §3).
- Cross-link: owner 13 and Science-and-Technology owner 12 (DPDP and cybersecurity).
- Core test: transparency of intent and respect for audience autonomy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q1(a) (✅ owner 03). DPDP Act, 2023 (✅ owner 03 Advanced §3; not fetched). IT Rules due-diligence reference (⚠️ owner-named; check exact year and rule before citing).
**Links:** ETH-1065, ETH-1068, ETH-1080 (PYQ map), ETH-1081 (Advanced file 13 cross-link).
**Trap:** A generic "social media has pros and cons" answer scores low; name a specific dilemma.
**Mains hook:** Algorithmic persuasion changes the audience variable at scale; the ethical test is intent and autonomy, not the technology itself (qualified).
**Back:** *"Name the dilemma: privacy, fake news, echo chamber, addiction, anonymity, state power."*

### Flashcard ETH-1073 — Moral attitude vs political attitude (syllabus clause)
**Front:** Contrast moral and political attitudes on definition, object, scope, source and the civil-servant obligation.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Moral attitude:** stance toward right/wrong, just/unjust, honest/dishonest, grounded in ethical reasoning and applied generally.
- **Political attitude:** stance toward a party, ideology, policy programme or contest for power, grounded in preference and affiliation.
**Breakdown:**
- Object: conduct and rightness of acts vs a political actor or outcome.
- Scope: portable across contexts vs context-bound.
- Obligation: retain and act on moral attitude; bracket political attitude in official conduct.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Syllabus clause "moral and political attitudes" (✅ owner 03 §11). Table entries ⚠️ owner analysis.
**Links:** ETH-1074 (Conduct Rules), ETH-1075 (limits), ETH-1076 (permanent executive), ETH-1060.
**Trap:** Students say civil servants should have "no attitudes". Moral attitude must be retained; only political partisanship is bracketed.
**Mains hook:** A civil servant retains a moral attitude and brackets a political attitude so the same officer can serve any elected government (qualified: policy awareness is not partisanship).
**Back:** *"Keep the moral compass, park the party flag."*

### Flashcard ETH-1074 — Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964: Rules 5 and 7
**Front:** What do Rules 5 and 7 of the Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964 restrict, as the owner presents them?
**Definition (learn):** Rule 5 restricts a government servant's participation in politics and demonstrations. Rule 7 restricts canvassing in elections (owner's reading).
**Breakdown:**
- Operationalises the expectation of political neutrality and non-partisanship (owner cites Article 309-linked service rules).
- Restricts conduct, not private belief.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conduct Rules, 1964 (✅ owner 03 §11). ⚠️ Exact wording of Rule 5 and Rule 7 (including the title of Rule 7, which in my memory is about canvassing of outside influence) is not fetched; re-check the official text before quoting sub-rules.
**Links:** ETH-1073, ETH-1075, `04` owner (non-partisanship), `09` owner (Nolan principles).
**Trap:** Over-claiming a duty of political "blankness" of mind is not in the Rules.
**Mains hook:** Cite the Rules for the conduct duty and the constitutional-neutrality principle; note that the duty limits conduct, not belief (qualified).
**Back:** *"Rule 5 keeps you out of politics on duty; Rule 7 keeps you off the canvass."*

### Flashcard ETH-1075 — Limits of non-partisanship: what it does not require
**Front:** What three things does non-partisanship NOT require of a civil servant?
**Definition (learn):**
1. Policy awareness is not partisanship: understanding priorities, briefing ministers candidly, implementing lawful policy faithfully.
2. Private political belief is not misconduct: Rules restrict public activity, canvassing and organised association, not private opinion or the vote.
3. Moral objection to a lawful policy is not "political attitude", provided it is framed in law, rules and public interest.
**Breakdown:**
- Partisanship arises only when the personal party or leader preference, not the law and the record, visibly drives a discretionary decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 03 §11 (analytical limits).
**Links:** ETH-1073, ETH-1074, ETH-1076.
**Trap:** "Officers must have no political opinion at all" is an over-reading.
**Mains hook:** The line is behavioural: test whether personal preference drove the decision (qualified).
**Back:** *"Policy yes, party no, private vote fine, objection by law."*

### Flashcard ETH-1076 — Permanent executive and the constancy of moral attitude
**Front:** Why does the "permanent executive" convention make moral attitude the constant and political attitude irrelevant to duty?
**Definition (learn):** The same civil servant serves successive, differently aligned elected governments. Moral attitude (impartial, rule-bound judgment) is the constant; political attitude is made irrelevant to official duty.
**Breakdown:**
- Institutional design: neutrality keeps political attitude from deciding who receives a benefit, licence or posting.
- Moral attitude makes neutrality trustworthy rather than mechanical.
- Limit: the line blurs when implementation is contested as partisan; the test is personal preference vs lawful policy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 §11 evidence units.
**Links:** ETH-1073, ETH-1075, `09` owner (Nolan objectivity and selflessness).
**Trap:** Thinking neutrality means blindly obeying any government. Moral objection through lawful channels remains.
**Mains hook:** Neutrality plus moral integrity lets officials serve any government; the failure mode is partisan discretion, not policy disagreement (qualified).
**Back:** *"Governments change, the moral compass does not."*

### Flashcard ETH-1077 — Dissonance and rationalisation risk in office (RTI example)
**Front:** How can cognitive dissonance produce ethical rationalisation in an official?
**Definition (learn):** An official who values transparency but is directed to withhold a routine file under an RTI exemption may convince themselves the exemption is broader than it is.
**Breakdown:**
- Drift path: rationalisation → settled misconduct if unchecked.
- Opposite path: principled objection and escalation (see file 10) can also result.
- Counter-measures: ethics review, peer accountability, audit trails (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 Advanced §3 and §10 evidence unit 3. RTI Act, 2005 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-1064, ETH-1067, ETH-1015 (RTI).
**Trap:** Theory does not determine direction; dissonance can resolve either way.
**Mains hook:** Build checks that interrupt rationalisation early, such as a written reasoning note for each withholding (qualified).
**Back:** *"Excusing the exemption is the first step to excusing the breach."*

### Flashcard ETH-1078 — Trusted messenger persuasion (vaccination and local leaders)
**Front:** Why does a credible local messenger usually shift attitudes faster than a top-down circular?
**Definition (learn):** Source credibility is a Hovland-Yale variable; a trusted local voice in the local language carries more persuasive weight.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner examples: Pulse Polio and COVID-19 vaccination campaigns using respected figures or ASHA workers in vernacular language (⚠️ owner examples, not fetched).
- Limit: credibility can be undone by a single visible institutional failure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 03 §10 unit 1.
**Links:** ETH-1065, ETH-1033.
**Trap:** Credibility is fragile; one failure can reverse it.
**Mains hook:** Use trusted messengers for public-health persuasion, but keep the message truthful and the institution accountable (qualified).
**Back:** *"Trusted voice, local tongue: credibility moves people, and breaks easily."*

### Flashcard ETH-1079 — Directive decoding for attitude questions
**Front:** Match the directives Examine, Discuss and Suggest to the structural move the owner expects.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Examine:** ABC model → component targeted → persuasion lever → Indian example → limitation.
- **Discuss:** phenomenon → mechanism → counter-consideration → reasoned position.
- **Suggest:** diagnose precisely → name the lever (source, message, channel) → one concrete measure → its limitation.
**Breakdown:** 10-mark answer ≈ 150 words: define via ABC, identify component, apply lever, Indian example, gap as limitation, conclusion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 §12.
**Links:** ETH-1025, ETH-1061, ETH-1065.
**Trap:** A "Suggest" answer without a limitation is weaker than the owner's model.
**Mains hook:** The directive determines whether attitude theory is the body of the answer or a supporting frame.
**Back:** *"Examine maps, Discuss weighs, Suggest acts and names the cost."*

### Flashcard ETH-1080 — PYQ map for the Attitude owner (2020–2025)
**Front:** Which GS-IV questions route to the Attitude owner?
**Definition (learn):**
- 2020 Q4(a): distinction between laws and rules and the role of ethics in formulating them; Q4(b): positive attitude as an essential characteristic under stress.
- 2021 Q4(a): how to build a suitable attitude for a public servant; Q4(b): whether emotional intelligence helps overcome a crisis of conscience without compromising ethical stand.
- 2022 Q2: positive vs negative mindset; ethics in human actions (also routed in file 02 area).
- 2025 Q1(a): social media ethical dilemmas; 2025 Q3(b): William James.
**Breakdown:** 10 + 10 marks, 150 words each (✅ owner 03 ledgers).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 PYQ ledgers.
**Links:** ETH-1072, ETH-1041, ETH-1055, ETH-1131 (EI PYQ anchors, file 05).
**Trap:** The 2022 Q2 item appears in both owners; answer per its stem.
**Mains hook:** Map each 2020–2025 demand to a named mechanism before writing.
**Back:** *"Twenty: laws and attitude. Twenty-one: build attitude, EI. Twenty-five: social media."*

### Flashcard ETH-1081 — Training design: affective and cognitive engagement (Mission Karmayogi)
**Front:** How should civil-service attitude training be designed, per the owner?
**Definition (learn):** Training targets both cognitive and affective components through simulation and case-based learning; institutional rules close the residual gap.
**Breakdown:**
- Foundation-course and Mission Karmayogi behavioural modules (owner §10 unit 4).
- Periodic refresh for long-serving officials (owner §10 unit 6, William James).
- Limit: repeated training without workload and incentive reform becomes box-ticking.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 §10, Advanced §4. Mission Karmayogi dates in ETH-1054.
**Links:** ETH-1062, ETH-1054, ETH-1082.
**Trap:** Lecture-only design misses the affective component.
**Mains hook:** Attitude training needs simulation and periodic refresh, plus structural reform to make values stick (qualified).
**Back:** *"Simulate, refresh, reform: training that changes conduct."*

### Flashcard ETH-1082 — Binding external constraint closes the attitude-behaviour gap
**Front:** Why does the owner say a binding external constraint is needed beyond attitude change?
**Definition (learn):** Persuasion cannot guarantee behaviour; legal mandates (RTI, whistle-blower protection) and codes close the gap that attitude leaves open.
**Breakdown:**
- Persuasion is slower and less certain than rules but more durable once internalised (advanced §6).
- Over-reliance on constraint without attitude change yields compliance that reverts when monitoring relaxes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 03 Advanced §3 and §6.
**Links:** ETH-1067, ETH-1081, ETH-1015, `16` Codes of Ethics owner.
**Trap:** Choosing between "awareness" and "regulation" is a false binary; the answer uses both with a stated trade-off.
**Mains hook:** Campaigns for attitude change plus binding rules; the trade-off is speed and certainty against durability (qualified).
**Back:** *"Persuade for the heart, bind for the hand."*

---

### GROUP — 04 Aptitude and Foundational Values for Civil Service

### Flashcard ETH-1083 — Aptitude and foundational values: core theme
**Front:** What is the core theme of the Aptitude and Foundational Values owner, and what is its answer thesis?
**Definition (learn):** Aptitude is the disposition to perform public duties competently and ethically. Foundational values (integrity, impartiality and non-partisanship, objectivity, dedication, empathy, tolerance, compassion) are each testable and are exam-credible only as operational design choices.
**Breakdown:**
- Syllabus phrase (verbatim, ✅ owner 04 §5).
- Values tested in combination (impartiality with compassion in a welfare dilemma).
- Operational tests and institutional safeguards (owner Advanced §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** UPSC GS-IV syllabus wording (✅ owner 04). ARC 4th Report Ch.2 Nolan discussion (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** ETH-1084 (aptitude), ETH-1085 (syllabus list), ETH-1104 (PYQ map), ETH-1099 (enabler vs regulator).
**Trap:** Treating each value as an isolated adjective. Mains questions test combinations.
**Mains hook:** Foundational values are institutional design commitments, not personal adjectives; each needs a pressure test and a safeguard (qualified).
**Back:** *"Values are design commitments: test them, then safeguard them."*

### Flashcard ETH-1084 — Aptitude vs attitude vs value
**Front:** How does aptitude differ from attitude and value?
**Definition (learn):** Aptitude = capacity to perform a role or task well. Attitude = evaluative stance toward a specific object. Value = general standard of what is worth pursuing.
**Breakdown:**
- Aptitude is trainable: competency-based training and case method treat it as cultivable (⚠️ owner Advanced §2).
- The three are different objects of assessment (owner Advanced §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 04 §2 and Advanced §2 (analytical).
**Links:** ETH-1063 (four-way distinction), ETH-1060, ETH-1032.
**Trap:** Students write "aptitude is attitude"; the owner keeps them apart.
**Mains hook:** Aptitude determines whether a good attitude becomes competent performance (qualified).
**Back:** *"Aptitude performs, attitude judges, value guides."*

### Flashcard ETH-1085 — GS-IV syllabus list of foundational values (verbatim)
**Front:** Recite the GS-IV syllabus list of aptitude and foundational values.
**Definition (learn):** "Aptitude and foundational values for Civil Service, integrity, impartiality and non-partisanship, objectivity, dedication to public service, empathy, tolerance and compassion towards the weaker-sections."
**Breakdown:**
- Seven named values: integrity; impartiality and non-partisanship; objectivity; dedication to public service; empathy; tolerance; compassion towards the weaker sections.
- The list overlaps with, but is not identical to, the Nolan seven (ETH-1096).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §5 (official syllabus phrase).
**Links:** ETH-1086–ETH-1092, ETH-1096.
**Trap:** Counting "impartiality" and "non-partisanship" as separate items on exam sheets is harmless, but the syllabus pairs them.
**Mains hook:** Use the syllabus list as the checklist for values answers.
**Back:** *"Integrity, impartial and non-partisan, objective, dedicated, empathic, tolerant, compassionate."*

### Flashcard ETH-1086 — Integrity vs honesty (Nolan; ARC 2.2.5)
**Front:** How does the owner distinguish integrity from honesty?
**Definition (learn):** Integrity = consistency of values across situations, including unobserved ones, and freedom from obligations that could compromise duty. Honesty = truthful communication in a given instance.
**Breakdown:**
- ARC 2.2.5 (via Nolan) treats integrity, honesty and openness as separate principles.
- Advanced: integrity is tested when discretion, secrecy or personal gain are present together.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2 and Advanced §2 (ARC 2.2.5).
**Links:** ETH-1087, ETH-1085, ETH-1095.
**Trap:** Honest in one statement is not integrity; integrity is a pattern.
**Mains hook:** Integrity cannot be legislated directly; the state legislates its proxies (ETH-1087) (qualified).
**Back:** *"Honesty is a true statement; integrity is a true pattern."*

### Flashcard ETH-1087 — Integrity proxies: how the state makes integrity observable
**Front:** Which proxies does the owner name for making integrity partly observable?
**Definition (learn):** Asset declarations, conflict-of-interest disclosure, cooling-off periods, and random transfers.
**Breakdown:**
- Converts an unobservable trait into partly observable behaviour (owner Advanced §3).
- Also includes audit trails and random inspection (owner Advanced §1 architecture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner Advanced §3 (analytical); instrument-level details not fetched.
**Links:** ETH-1086, ETH-1095, `14` and `21` owners.
**Trap:** Proxies detect behaviour, not virtue. A clean declaration does not prove integrity.
**Mains hook:** Proxies are necessary but imperfect; they must be paired with a culture of integrity (qualified).
**Back:** *"Declare, disclose, cool off, shuffle: integrity's proxies."*

### Flashcard ETH-1088 — Trap: impartiality is not uniform treatment
**Front:** Does impartiality require identical treatment of every citizen?
**Definition (learn):** No. Impartiality means deciding on transparent, rule-based criteria without personal bias. Needs-based support for weaker sections is impartial when criteria are transparent and applied to all similarly placed persons.
**Breakdown:**
- Compassion-driven discretion, if not rule-bound, looks like favouritism; the safeguard is transparent criteria (owner Advanced §6).
- Impartiality vs equality of treatment (owner Advanced §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §2, §8.
**Links:** ETH-1101 (boundary case), ETH-1100, ETH-1089.
**Trap:** The exam option "same rule for all regardless of need" is an over-reading of impartiality.
**Mains hook:** Impartiality reconciles with compassion through published eligibility rules (qualified).
**Back:** *"Same rules for similar cases; not same treatment for unlike needs."*

### Flashcard ETH-1089 — Objectivity: evidence- and rule-based judgment
**Front:** What does objectivity mean in the owner's operational table?
**Definition (learn):** Judgment based on evidence and rules, free of preconception. Test: evidence vs preconception. Safeguard: reasoned orders and an appeal mechanism.
**Breakdown:**
- Nolan's objectivity principle (ETH-1096).
- Operational test in the owner's architecture: evidence vs preconception (owner Advanced §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §1 (architecture, analytical).
**Links:** ETH-1088, ETH-1096, ETH-1100.
**Trap:** Objectivity is not "no discretion". It is discretion reasoned on evidence.
**Mains hook:** Reasoned orders make objectivity checkable; without them it is a claim (qualified).
**Back:** *"Evidence over preconception, reasons on paper."*

### Flashcard ETH-1090 — Dedication to public service: beyond minimum compliance
**Front:** How does the owner define dedication to public service, and how is it tested?
**Definition (learn):** Motivation to serve the public interest beyond the minimum rules require, visible in responsiveness, initiative and proactive problem-solving.
**Breakdown:**
- Operational test: minimum compliance vs proactive service.
- Safeguard: outcome-linked appraisal (owner, Mission Karmayogi competencies).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 04 §2 and Advanced §1. Linked PYQ: 2025 GS-IV Q5(a).
**Links:** ETH-1099, ETH-1104, ETH-1034 (nishkama karma).
**Trap:** Dedication is not overwork or sacrifice of rules; it is proactive lawful service.
**Mains hook:** Dedication is measured by outcomes enabled, not only rules enforced (qualified).
**Back:** *"Go beyond the rule, never around it."*

### Flashcard ETH-1091 — Empathy vs sympathy vs compassion
**Front:** Distinguish empathy, sympathy and compassion, and state which is administratively assessable.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Empathy:** accurately grasping another's situation and feelings from their frame of reference.
- **Sympathy:** feeling for someone from outside, often with an implicit giver–receiver hierarchy.
- **Compassion:** empathy plus a motivated commitment to relieve suffering, which issues in action or service design.
**Breakdown:** Only compassion, in this sense, is administratively assessable. GS-IV answers must convert felt concern into a procedural change.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 04 §2 (conceptual definitions, analytical).
**Links:** ETH-1100 (compassion as service design), ETH-1062 (affective), ETH-1005.
**Trap:** Using the three terms as synonyms is the owner's named weakness in casual speech.
**Mains hook:** Compassion becomes credible when it changes a process such as a form, a route or a timing (qualified).
**Back:** *"Feel with, feel for, then act: only the act is assessable."*

### Flashcard ETH-1092 — Tolerance vs passive non-interference
**Front:** How does tolerance differ from passive non-interference in the owner's definition?
**Definition (learn):** Respect for and acceptance of diversity of belief, culture and viewpoint, distinct from mere passive non-interference.
**Breakdown:**
- Operational test: diversity of belief or community.
- Constitutional anchor cited by owner: Article 51A(e) (fundamental duty to promote harmony and the spirit of common brotherhood) and secular conduct. ⚠️ Article text not fetched here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §1 (Article 51A(e) reference); ⚠️ wording unverified.
**Links:** ETH-1085, ETH-1028 (constitutional morality), `Polity` owner (Part IVA).
**Trap:** Tolerance is more than "not interfering". It involves active respect.
**Mains hook:** Tolerance in administration means equal service regardless of community, not only the absence of visible bias (qualified).
**Back:** *"Tolerance is respect in action, not silence."*

### Flashcard ETH-1093 — Non-partisanship: serve the government of the day
**Front:** How does the owner define non-partisanship?
**Definition (learn):** A civil servant's duty to serve the government of the day loyally and professionally, regardless of personal political preference, and without party considerations shaping advice or action.
**Breakdown:**
- Foundational value (owner §2, ⚠️ analytical definition).
- Anchored in the Conduct Rules (see ETH-1074) and the permanent-executive convention (ETH-1076).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 04 §2 definition; Conduct Rules 1964 per owner 03 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-1074, ETH-1075, ETH-1076, ETH-1102 (trap).
**Trap:** Non-partisanship does not mean having no political view (ETH-1075).
**Mains hook:** Non-partisanship is sustained by tenure and transfer safeguards, not only personal virtue (qualified).
**Back:** *"Serve the government of the day, not the party of the day."*

### Flashcard ETH-1094 — Warren Buffett: integrity, intelligence and energy (2018 GS-IV Q3(b))
**Front:** What is the Buffett hiring line, what does it teach, and what is its attribution status?
**Definition (learn):** Hire for three qualities: integrity, intelligence and energy; "and if they don't have the first, the other two will kill you" (widely attributed).
**Breakdown:**
- Intelligence and energy are force-multipliers; without integrity, they amplify concealment and favouritism.
- Integrity is the hardest to test at entry; hence safeguards throughout career.
- Attribution: Buffett introduces it as "somebody once said"; traceable published form is a 1994 *Omaha World-Herald* report of his 1993 Columbia Business School remarks (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 GS-IV Q3(b) (✅ owner 04 §9 ledger). 1993 remarks, 1994 report (✅ owner 04 §9; not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-1095, ETH-1057 (attribution trap), file 09 owner (conflict-of-interest taxonomy).
**Trap:** Presenting it as a coined Buffett aphorism. Describe as widely repeated and endorsed.
**Mains hook:** Integrity is first because the other two amplify whatever integrity provides (qualified: the teaching is a judgement, not a measured law).
**Back:** *"Integrity first, or the other two kill you."*

### Flashcard ETH-1095 — Integrity is structurally hardest to test at entry
**Front:** Why does the owner say integrity is the hardest of the three Buffett qualities to test at recruitment?
**Definition (learn):** Competitive examinations test intelligence; probation and performance review test energy; integrity cannot be tested objectively at entry.
**Breakdown:**
- Hence continuous safeguards: asset declarations, vigilance clearance, 360-degree feedback under Mission Karmayogi (owner §9, ⚠️ 360-degree linkage not verified).
- Cross-link to file 09 conflict-of-interest taxonomy (actual, potential, apparent) (owner §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 04 §9 analytical claim.
**Links:** ETH-1094, ETH-1087, ETH-1054.
**Trap:** Assuming a clean entry exam certifies integrity for a whole career.
**Mains hook:** Integrity is a career-long monitoring problem, not a one-time test (qualified).
**Back:** *"Exams test the head and the legs; integrity is tested in service."*

### Flashcard ETH-1096 — Nolan Committee's seven principles of public life
**Front:** List the Nolan seven principles of public life and say how they relate to the GS-IV syllabus list.
**Definition (learn):** Selflessness, integrity, objectivity, accountability, openness, honesty, leadership.
**Breakdown:**
- Overlaps substantially with the GS-IV syllabus list but is not identical (owner §5 and Advanced §7).
- Exact seven and detail in file 09 owner.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §5 (list as stated); Nolan Committee, first report 1995 (⚠️ year from memory, not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-1085, ETH-1089, ETH-1086, `09` owner.
**Trap:** Students confuse the Nolan seven with the GS-IV syllabus list; the Nolan list has accountability, openness and leadership which the syllabus list does not name directly.
**Mains hook:** Use Nolan as a checklist for conduct codes; use the syllabus list for the foundational-values answer (qualified).
**Back:** *"Selfless, integrity, objective, accountable, open, honest, leading."*

### Flashcard ETH-1097 — The transfer industry: the threat to non-partisanship (ARC 9.4–9.13)
**Front:** What does the ARC treat as the principal structural threat to non-partisanship, and what does the Wade quotation say?
**Definition (learn):** The arbitrary transfer power of politicians, which the ARC describes through Robert Wade's study of Andhra Pradesh (ARC 9.4): "the transfer is the politicians' basic weapon of control over the bureaucracy and thus the lever for surplus-extraction from the clients of the bureaucracy."
**Breakdown:**
- ARC 9.4–9.13 document the "transfer industry" (✅ owner 04 Advanced §7).
- Fifth Pay Commission recommended minimum three-to-five-year tenure, two-to-three years for sensitive posts (✅ owner 04 Advanced §7 as cited).
- Remedy: Civil Services Board-type mechanisms (⚠️ owner, ARC and NCRWC; see ETH-1098).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 9.4 (✅ owner). Fifth Pay Commission tenure figures (✅ owner citation; not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-1093, ETH-1098, ETH-1102, `21` owner.
**Trap:** Blaming only individual character for partisan conduct when the ARC treats it as structural.
**Mains hook:** Non-partisanship needs tenure and transfer protection; no training substitutes for it (qualified).
**Back:** *"Transfers are the leash; tenure is the collar's key."*

### Flashcard ETH-1098 — Civil Services Board-type mechanisms
**Front:** What role do Civil Services Board-type mechanisms play in protecting non-partisanship?
**Definition (learn):** Bodies that insulate routine transfer and posting decisions from unmediated political discretion. Recommended by the ARC and the National Commission to Review the Working of the Constitution (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Routine postings through a mediated board.
- Link to ETH-1097 transfer leverage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner Advanced §4 (ARC and NCRWC recommendations as cited; not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-1097, `11` and `21` owners.
**Trap:** A Civil Services Board removes political input only partly; the owner frames it as insulation, not abolition of political direction.
**Mains hook:** Institutional insulation converts non-partisanship from a personal virtue into a designed system (qualified).
**Back:** *"Board the transfer, insulate the post."*

### Flashcard ETH-1099 — Enabler not regulator: calibrated facilitation (2025 GS-IV Q5(b))
**Front:** What does the 2025 Q5(b) phrase demand, and how does the owner calibrate it?
**Definition (learn):** The civil servant as "enabler and active facilitator of growth rather than a regulator" for holistic development, with specific measures.
**Breakdown:**
- Calibrated design: pre-clearance single-window facilitation for legitimate activity, plus post-facto, risk-based regulatory scrutiny (owner Advanced §3, §10; single-window cross-link file 17).
- Blanket ex-ante gatekeeping slows all applicants equally regardless of risk.
- Limit: without risk-based information systems, facilitation defaults into under-regulation by omission.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q5(b) (✅ owner 04 PYQ ledger).
**Links:** ETH-1103 (trap), ETH-1104 (PYQ map), `17` owner.
**Trap:** "Enabler" is not "deregulation".
**Mains hook:** Argue for sequencing and calibration: enable where risk is low, regulate where safety or equity requires it (qualified).
**Back:** *"Enable the low-risk, check the high-risk, measure both."*

### Flashcard ETH-1100 — Compassion as service design: doorstep pension and proof-of-life
**Front:** Give two examples the owner uses to show compassion becoming an administrative process.
**Definition (learn):** Compassion is credible only when it changes a process.
**Breakdown:**
- Doorstep pension verification for elderly or disabled applicants (basic §4).
- Video-call proof-of-life instead of in-person appearance for pension beneficiaries (advanced §3).
- Trade-off: doorstep and remote schemes raise their own verification and fraud risks, which must be managed separately (basic §11 unit 2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner examples (illustrative, not scheme-verified).
**Links:** ETH-1091, ETH-1088, ETH-1101.
**Trap:** Good intention without a changed process earns no marks.
**Mains hook:** Compassion as design, with verification controls to protect the same beneficiaries (qualified).
**Back:** *"Bring the office to the old, not the old to the office, and verify it."*

### Flashcard ETH-1101 — Boundary case: needs-based facilitation vs nepotism
**Front:** When does needs-based facilitation become nepotism?
**Definition (learn):** Affirmative, needs-based facilitation (fee waiver, doorstep service) is compassionate and impartial if criteria are transparent, rule-based and universally applied. The same facilitation given informally to a politically favoured person is nepotism.
**Breakdown:**
- Test: published criteria and consistent application.
- Tax officer example: identical audit-selection criteria for connected and unconnected assessees (owner Advanced §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner Advanced §5 (analytical boundary case).
**Links:** ETH-1088, ETH-1100, ETH-1102.
**Trap:** Compassion and favouritism look alike in form; criteria distinguish them.
**Mains hook:** The safeguard is transparent eligibility, not the absence of discretion (qualified).
**Back:** *"Same published rule, different need: compassion. Secret rule, same favour: nepotism."*

### Flashcard ETH-1102 — Trap: non-partisanship is purely individual character
**Front:** Is non-partisanship a matter of individual character alone?
**Definition (learn):** No. ARC evidence shows it is structurally undermined by the discretionary transfer power of the political executive absent tenure safeguards.
**Breakdown:**
- Institutional design issue as much as personal (owner Advanced §8).
- No amount of training fully substitutes for tenure protection (owner Advanced §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §8 and §6.
**Links:** ETH-1097, ETH-1098, ETH-1093.
**Trap:** Exam options that locate the problem only in the official's character are incomplete.
**Mains hook:** Pair individual training with tenure and transfer safeguards (qualified).
**Back:** *"Character helps; tenure protects."*

### Flashcard ETH-1103 — Trap: enabler means deregulation
**Front:** Does "enabler not regulator" mean removing regulation?
**Definition (learn):** No. It means calibrated, risk-based sequencing of facilitation and regulation, not the removal of oversight.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner's regulator-vs-enabler tension is a design trade-off (owner Advanced §2).
- Pure facilitation without backstops risks regulatory capture in safety or equity sectors (owner basic §11 unit 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §8.
**Links:** ETH-1099, ETH-1104.
**Trap:** Answers that call for "less government" in every sector.
**Mains hook:** Specify which sectors need regulation and which need facilitation (qualified).
**Back:** *"Enable where risk is low; never switch the safety off."*

### Flashcard ETH-1104 — PYQ map for file 04 (2018–2025 GS-IV)
**Front:** Which GS-IV questions route to the Aptitude owner?
**Definition (learn):**
- 2018 Q1(a): three basic values universal in civil services; Q1(b): code of ethics vs code of conduct.
- 2018 Q3(a): actual vs potential conflict of interest; Q3(b): Buffett quotation.
- 2019 Q1(a): basic principles of public life; Q1(b): meaning of public servant; 2019 Q7: rescue officer case (20 marks).
- 2021 Q1(a): five ethical traits on a performance matrix; Q1(b): ten essential values; 2021 Q5(a): refugees and non-refoulement; Q5(b): impartiality and non-partisanship.
- 2024 Q6(a): gender identity and equal opportunity; Q6(b): Mission Karmayogi.
- 2025 Q4(a): reason and critical thinking in welfare schemes; Q4(b): Mahavir; Q5(a): devotion to duty; Q5(b): enabler.
**Breakdown:** 10 + 10 marks (150 words each) except 2019 Q7 (20 marks, 250 words) (✅ owner 04 ledgers).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 PYQ ledgers.
**Links:** ETH-1094, ETH-1105, ETH-1099, ETH-1034, ETH-1037.
**Trap:** The 2024 Q6 and 2025 Q5 items are also routed to other owners; answer per stem.
**Mains hook:** Map each demand to a named value and mechanism before writing.
**Back:** *"Eighteen values and codes; nineteen rescue; twenty-one traits and refugees; twenty-five enabler."*

### Flashcard ETH-1105 — 2019 GS-IV Q7 rescue-officer case (20 marks)
**Front:** Summarise the 2019 GS-IV Q7 case and the qualities it tests.
**Definition (learn):** A rescue officer in a severe natural calamity faces assault by an angry crowd; some team members plead to call off operations. The question asks the response and the qualities required of a public servant.
**Breakdown:**
- Case-study format, 20 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 04 ledger).
- Use the full eight-element case architecture in file 22 (owner routing).
- Tests: courage, composure, decisive action, dedication, and the ethical balance between safety and duty.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 GS-IV Q7 (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** ETH-1104, ETH-1091, ETH-1053 (Dalai Lama on sacrifice), `22` owner.
**Trap:** Answering with a single option. The owner requires at least three realistic options.
**Mains hook:** Decide on safety and duty together; propose options, steelman the objection, then decide (qualified).
**Back:** *"Crowd angry, team split: options first, safety and duty decided together."*

### Flashcard ETH-1106 — Code of ethics vs code of conduct (2018 GS-IV Q1(b))
**Front:** Distinguish a code of ethics from a code of conduct, as the 2018 Q1(b) demand asks.
**Definition (learn):** A code of ethics states aspirational values and principles to guide judgement. A code of conduct sets specific, enforceable rules for behaviour.
**Breakdown:**
- Ethics: values, principles, reasoned judgment (owner's distinction; ⚠️ general definition).
- Conduct: rules, prohibitions, sanctions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 GS-IV Q1(b) (✅ owner 04 ledger). Detailed owner in file 16.
**Links:** ETH-1074 (Conduct Rules 1964), `16` owner.
**Trap:** Calling a code of conduct "ethics" loses the distinction the question tests.
**Mains hook:** Good governance needs both: principles to guide, rules to enforce (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ethics guides the judgement; conduct binds the act."*

### Flashcard ETH-1107 — Trap: compassion is administratively empty sentiment
**Front:** Is compassion a private emotional sentiment with no administrative content?
**Definition (learn):** No. In GS-IV it must be shown as translated into service design or decision. A felt sentiment alone earns no marks.
**Breakdown:**
- Compassion = empathy plus commitment to relieve suffering (ETH-1091).
- Service-design example: ETH-1100.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §6.
**Links:** ETH-1091, ETH-1100, ETH-1062.
**Trap:** Answers that end with "be kind to citizens" lose the mark.
**Mains hook:** Convert empathy into a process change (qualified).
**Back:** *"No process, no compassion on the page."*

### Flashcard ETH-1108 — Limit: competency platforms measure uptake, not conduct
**Front:** What limitation does the owner raise about Mission Karmayogi platform metrics?
**Definition (learn):** Course counts, users and completions measure training uptake, not verified behavioural change in the field.
**Breakdown:**
- Official scale per owner (as reported to Parliament, 6 February 2026): over 1.49 crore users, 4,342 courses, 7.26 crore completions (⚠️ dated; re-check).
- Need field-level indicators and appraisal linkage (DoPT order required).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4 and §11 unit 4 (dated figures).
**Links:** ETH-1054, ETH-1081, ETH-1095.
**Trap:** Citing completion counts as proof of value internalisation.
**Mains hook:** Measure conduct, not only completions, to show values training works (qualified).
**Back:** *"Completions count courses; conduct counts values."*

### Flashcard ETH-1109 — Impartiality under political transition (returning officer)
**Front:** What example does the owner give for non-partisanship tested under pressure?
**Definition (learn):** A returning officer applies identical scrutiny to all candidates regardless of ruling-party affiliation during election duty.
**Breakdown:**
- Operationalises impartiality under real pressure (owner basic §11 unit 3).
- Limit: formal even-handedness does not correct pre-existing structural disadvantage; necessary, not sufficient.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner illustrative example (not an event report).
**Links:** ETH-1093, ETH-1088, ETH-1076.
**Trap:** Treating equal scrutiny as full fairness.
**Mains hook:** Pair equal process with substantive measures where disadvantage is structural (qualified).
**Back:** *"Same scrutiny for all, but equal scrutiny is not equal opportunity."*

### Flashcard ETH-1110 — Tax officer and impartiality: identical criteria, either direction
**Front:** What does the owner's tax-officer example teach about impartiality?
**Definition (learn):** Selecting or sparing assessees on non-merit grounds violates impartiality regardless of the political direction of the bias.
**Breakdown:**
- Test: identical audit-selection criteria for politically connected and unconnected assessees (owner Advanced §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner analytical example.
**Links:** ETH-1088, ETH-1101, ETH-1109.
**Trap:** A bias "in the right direction" is still bias.
**Mains hook:** Impartiality is direction-neutral; audit selection must be rule-based and recorded (qualified).
**Back:** *"Bias either way is still bias."*

### Flashcard ETH-1111 — PYQ application: 2025 and 2024 analytical demands
**Front:** What specific measures does the owner say examiners reward for the 2025 Q5(b) and 2024 Q6(a) demands?
**Definition (learn):**
- 2025 Q5(b): specific enabling measures such as single-window clearance, outcome-based KPIs, risk-based inspection.
- 2024 Q6(a): gender-specific workplace measures (safety, flexible postings, harassment redress) connected to impartiality and probity.
**Breakdown:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §10.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §10 (named measures as examiner-rewarded; ⚠️ the specific list is owner analysis).
**Links:** ETH-1099, ETH-1104.
**Trap:** A standalone women's-welfare answer misses the probity framework.
**Mains hook:** Tie each measure back to a foundational value (qualified).
**Back:** *"Name the measure, tie it to the value."*

---

### GROUP — 05 Emotional Intelligence in Administration

### Flashcard ETH-1112 — Emotional intelligence in administration: core theme
**Front:** What is the core theme of the EI owner, and what is its answer thesis?
**Definition (learn):** Emotional intelligence is the capacity to perceive, understand, regulate and use emotions (one's own and others') to guide thought and action. In administration it is the psychological infrastructure that makes composed, empathetic and ethically sound decisions possible under pressure.
**Breakdown:**
- Goleman's five components: self-awareness, self-regulation, motivation, empathy, social skill.
- EI mediates between stress or pressure and ethical decision quality.
- Limit: EI is necessary, not sufficient; it is ethically neutral.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Salovey and Mayer (1990); Mayer-Salovey four-branch model (1997); Goleman (1995; HBR 1998) (✅ owner 05 §5 and Advanced §1).
**Links:** ETH-1113 to ETH-1121, ETH-1120 (dark side), ETH-1132 (empathy institutionalised), ETH-1126 (DC case).
**Trap:** EI is not "suppress emotion" (ETH-1128).
**Mains hook:** EI improves execution under stress, but it must be joined to integrity and institutional support to serve citizens rather than manipulate them (qualified).
**Back:** *"Feel, name, regulate, use: EI carries decisions through stress."*

### Flashcard ETH-1113 — Salovey and Mayer's 1990 definition of EI
**Front:** Give the 1990 Salovey and Mayer definition of emotional intelligence.
**Definition (learn):** "The subset of social intelligence that involves the ability to monitor one's own and others' feelings and emotions, to discriminate among them and to use this information to guide one's thinking and actions."
**Breakdown:**
- Monitor, discriminate, use: three verbs to remember.
- Counts as an ability, not a personality trait (ability model).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990 (✅ owner 05 §2 and Advanced §2).
**Links:** ETH-1114 (1997 revision), ETH-1116 (trait model), ETH-1129.
**Trap:** The four-branch model is 1997, not the 1990 paper (ETH-1114).
**Mains hook:** Define EI with the three verbs, then show each in an administrative moment (qualified).
**Back:** *"Monitor, discriminate, use: EI in three verbs."*

### Flashcard ETH-1114 — Mayer-Salovey four-branch ability model (1997)
**Front:** Name the four branches of the 1997 Mayer-Salovey ability model.
**Definition (learn):** (1) perceiving emotion; (2) using emotion to facilitate thought; (3) understanding emotion; (4) managing emotion.
**Breakdown:**
- Branches are hierarchical in the literature (⚠️ standard description, not in owner).
- Advanced decomposition of the DC case uses the branches (ETH-1126).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1997 four-branch revision (✅ owner 05 §5 and Advanced §2).
**Links:** ETH-1113, ETH-1115, ETH-1126.
**Trap:** Saying the four branches were in the 1990 paper.
**Mains hook:** Use the branches to label each decision point in a case (qualified: not a diagnostic tool).
**Back:** *"Perceive, use, understand, manage: the four rungs of ability EI."*

### Flashcard ETH-1115 — Goleman's five components and their administrative translation
**Front:** List Goleman's five EI components and the administrative translation the owner gives each.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Self-awareness:** recognising own bias or stress before deciding.
- **Self-regulation:** composure under provocation or crisis.
- **Motivation:** sustained public-service drive beyond incentive.
- **Empathy:** reading citizens' and colleagues' unstated needs.
- **Social skill:** managing teams, stakeholders and conflict.
**Breakdown:** Goleman's 1995 book popularised the field; 1998 HBR essay set out the five workplace competencies (✅ owner 05 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Goleman (1995; HBR 1998) (✅ owner 05 §5).
**Links:** ETH-1112, ETH-1116, ETH-1117, ETH-1127.
**Trap:** Mixing up the Salovey-Mayer branches with Goleman's five competencies.
**Mains hook:** Name the component engaged at each decision point before describing the emotion (owner §8).
**Back:** *"Self-aware, self-regulate, motivated, empathic, socially skilled."*

### Flashcard ETH-1116 — Bar-On's trait model (EQ-i) vs ability tests (MSCEIT)
**Front:** How does the trait model (Bar-On EQ-i) differ from ability tests?
**Definition (learn):** The trait model treats EI as self-perceived emotional and social competencies measured by self-report. Bar-On's EQ-i technical manual is dated 1997 (owner). The MSCEIT is an ability test.
**Breakdown:**
- Trait: useful for training design; contested as a predictor of actual behaviour.
- Ability: performance-based tests.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Bar-On EQ-i (1997) (✅ owner 05 Advanced header). MSCEIT as ability test (⚠️ owner Advanced §2).
**Links:** ETH-1117, ETH-1113.
**Trap:** "All EI models measure the same construct identically" is a named trap.
**Mains hook:** Choose the model by purpose: ability for measurement claims, trait for training, mixed for workplace competency (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ability tests what you can do; trait asks what you say you do."*

### Flashcard ETH-1117 — Comparison: ability vs mixed/competency vs trait models
**Front:** Compare the three EI models by origin, method and focus.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Ability (Salovey-Mayer):** genuine cognitive ability; performance-based.
- **Mixed/competency (Goleman):** ability blended with personality and motivation; five workplace competencies.
- **Trait (Bar-On EQ-i):** constellation of self-perceived competencies; self-report.
**Breakdown:** Combined in the owner's architecture as three entry points into one applied use: crisis leadership, stakeholder management, dilemma resolution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 Advanced §1, §7.
**Links:** ETH-1113, ETH-1115, ETH-1116.
**Trap:** The model differs in method, not only in label.
**Mains hook:** Use Goleman for administrative competency design, Salovey-Mayer for theory, and note the self-report limit (qualified).
**Back:** *"Ability, competency, trait: three doors to the same room."*

### Flashcard ETH-1118 — IQ vs EQ: complementary, EQ decisive at execution
**Front:** How does the owner state the relation between IQ and EQ?
**Definition (learn):** IQ measures cognitive and analytical ability; EQ measures the capacity to manage emotional responses and interpersonal dynamics. Both matter; EQ is decisive under high-stress, high-stakes situations.
**Breakdown:**
- Two officers with identical cognitive ability can produce different crisis outcomes depending on EQ (owner §10 unit).
- EQ cannot replace technical or legal competence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 05 §2 and §10 (analytical claim).
**Links:** ETH-1123 (2023 Q4(a)), ETH-1129 (trap), ETH-1126.
**Trap:** High IQ does not imply high EQ.
**Mains hook:** Jointly necessary; EQ matters most at the point of execution under stress (qualified).
**Back:** *"IQ decides what; EQ decides whether it gets done under fire."*

### Flashcard ETH-1119 — Emotional labour (Hochschild, 1983)
**Front:** Define emotional labour and state its administrative cost.
**Definition (learn):** The effort required to manage one's displayed emotions to meet professional role demands, from *The Managed Heart* (1983).
**Breakdown:**
- Example: a public-facing officer staying courteous under provocation.
- Cost: burnout risk if sustained without institutional support (owner Advanced §2).
- Justifies staff rotation and grievance channels for staff (owner §12 unit 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Hochschild, *The Managed Heart* (1983) (✅ owner 05 §2 and Advanced §2).
**Links:** ETH-1127 (institutional supports), ETH-1062 (affective component).
**Trap:** Natural empathy does not make emotional labour cost-free.
**Mains hook:** Staff wellbeing is part of ethical administration, but not an excuse for poor service (qualified).
**Back:** *"Managed smiles cost; sustained, they burn."*

### Flashcard ETH-1120 — EI's dark side: manipulation without integrity
**Front:** Why is EI ethically neutral, and what is the boundary case?
**Definition (learn):** EI is ethically neutral; high social skill and empathic accuracy can manipulate stakeholders as readily as serve them.
**Breakdown:**
- Example: "I understand your urgency, so paying extra will speed this up" — accurate empathy used to extract a bribe (owner Advanced §3).
- Boundary case: outwardly composed officer who manipulates colleagues privately (owner Advanced §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner Advanced §2, §3, §5 (analytical).
**Links:** ETH-1121, ETH-1129, ETH-1012 (collusive/coercive), `14` owner.
**Trap:** "High EQ guarantees ethics" is a named trap.
**Mains hook:** Assess EI jointly with integrity and probity; a Mains answer must name the safeguard against manipulation (qualified).
**Back:** *"Empathy can serve or sell; integrity chooses which."*

### Flashcard ETH-1121 — EI mediates between pressure and ethical decision quality (mechanism)
**Front:** How does EI connect stress to ethical decision quality, per the owner's mechanism?
**Definition (learn):** Self-awareness recognises stress or bias; self-regulation converts awareness into composed conduct; motivation sustains conduct without external reward; empathy reads unstated needs; social skill coordinates and communicates unpopular decisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Stress → self-awareness → self-regulation → ethical decision.
- Motivation links to dedication (file 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §3 (mechanism, analytical application).
**Links:** ETH-1115, ETH-1083, ETH-1090.
**Trap:** Describing emotions narratively without naming the component.
**Mains hook:** Label each component and its effect, then show the decision it enables (qualified).
**Back:** *"Aware, composed, driven, attuned, persuasive: EI's five steps to a fair decision."*

### Flashcard ETH-1122 — Moral intuition vs moral reasoning (2023 GS-IV Q4(b))
**Front:** How does the owner distinguish moral intuition and moral reasoning, and what role does EQ play?
**Definition (learn):**
- **Moral intuition:** fast, felt, affect-driven "this feels wrong" judgment drawing on empathy and self-awareness.
- **Moral reasoning:** slower, deliberate application of a theory or rule to test and correct intuition.
- EQ is the regulatory bridge between intuition and reasoning, not a third channel.
**Breakdown:** Full definitional treatment in file 08 (owner); philosophical grounding in file 10.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-IV Q4(b) (✅ owner 05 §10 ledger). Distinction ⚠️ owner analysis.
**Links:** ETH-1123, `08` and `10` owners.
**Trap:** Treating intuition as always wrong or always right.
**Mains hook:** Use self-awareness to check intuition with reasoning before acting (qualified).
**Back:** *"Intuition feels, reasoning checks; EQ is the bridge."*

### Flashcard ETH-1123 — EQ as key to success over cognitive ability (2023 GS-IV Q4(a))
**Front:** What is the claim in 2023 Q4(a), and what is the defensible position?
**Definition (learn):** The claim that EQ is key to success over and above cognitive ability.
**Breakdown:**
- Claim: IQ predicts task-competence; EQ predicts how competence is deployed under pressure.
- Evidence: two officers with equal cognitive ability diverge in a law-and-order crisis by regulation of panic, empathy and de-escalation.
- Limit: EQ cannot replace legal competence; the defensible position is joint necessity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-IV Q4(a) (✅ owner 05 §10 ledger). Analysis ⚠️ owner.
**Links:** ETH-1118, ETH-1122, ETH-1120.
**Trap:** Answering "EQ alone is key" loses the joint-necessity qualifier.
**Mains hook:** Argue joint necessity and EQ's disproportionate role at execution, with a legal-competence caveat (qualified).
**Back:** *"Skill plans, EQ delivers under strain, neither suffices alone."*

### Flashcard ETH-1124 — Wisdom in administration and the travesty of justice (2022 GS-IV Q1(a))
**Front:** What does the owner mean by "wisdom in administration," and how does it avoid a travesty of justice?
**Definition (learn):** Practical judgment (Aristotelian *phronesis*, file 07/08) applied under uncertainty and incomplete information, so a rule is applied with contextual sensitivity rather than mechanically.
**Breakdown:**
- Travesty of justice: following the letter of a rule while defeating its purpose (e.g., denying a genuine welfare claimant on a minor documentation technicality).
- Risk is EQ-linked: a low-empathy official applies rules mechanically without seeing the human cost.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-IV Q1(a) (✅ owner 05 §11 ledger). *Phronesis* definition ⚠️ owner analysis.
**Links:** ETH-1125, ETH-1006 (legality vs propriety), ETH-1132.
**Trap:** Wisdom does not create exemptions where the rule works.
**Mains hook:** Wisdom corrects a rule that misfires; it is not licence to waive a rule that works (qualified).
**Back:** *"Apply the rule's purpose, not only its words."*

### Flashcard ETH-1125 — Empathy and compassion as vital attributes (2022 GS-IV Q1(b))
**Front:** Why does the owner call empathy and compassion "vital," and what trigger role do they play?
**Definition (learn):** They are the input that alerts a procedurally correct official to a case that requires wisdom.
**Breakdown:**
- Without empathy, "wisdom in administration" has no trigger to activate.
- Full definition of empathy and compassion in file 04 (ETH-1091).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-IV Q1(b) (✅ owner 05 §11 ledger).
**Links:** ETH-1124, ETH-1091, ETH-1100.
**Trap:** Empathy is not the same as lenience.
**Mains hook:** Empathy triggers wisdom; wisdom decides; institutions verify (qualified).
**Back:** *"Empathy raises the flag; wisdom lowers the rule where it misfires."*

### Flashcard ETH-1126 — DC "Vijay": 2025 GS-IV Q7 as an EI stress test
**Front:** How does the owner decompose the 2025 GS-IV Q7 DC case using the ability model?
**Definition (learn):** A DC leads disaster relief in a hill-district cloudburst (more than 200 deaths, about 5,000 injured, road and telecom disruption) while his mother dies during the crisis and family pressure mounts to travel.
**Breakdown:**
- Perceive: his own grief without denial.
- Use: channel urgency into relief rather than paralysis.
- Understand: competing claims of family and duty.
- Manage: a reasoned, communicated choice (delegate command, attend last rites briefly, or explain transparently to family).
- Structured, transparent balancing, not a single heroic choice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q7 (✅ owner 05 Advanced §3). Casualty figures are owner-stated for the case (case fiction; ⚠️ not an external event).
**Links:** ETH-1114 (branches), ETH-1127 (institutional protocol), ETH-1105 (2019 rescue case), `22` owner.
**Trap:** A narrative of emotions without component labels scores low.
**Mains hook:** Structured delegation and transparent communication show EI operating through institutions (qualified).
**Back:** *"Grieve, lead, delegate, explain: the DC's four moves."*

### Flashcard ETH-1127 — Institutional supports for EI: succession, debrief, rotation
**Front:** What institutional supports does the owner say sustain EI in high-stress posts?
**Definition (learn):** Succession and delegation protocols during crises; peer-support and debrief after trauma; rotation policies to prevent chronic burnout.
**Breakdown:**
- Applicable to disaster management, law and order, frontline health (owner Advanced §3).
- Crisis training can convert a personal dilemma into a foreseen protocol (owner Advanced §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner Advanced §3–§4 (analytical; NDMA reference is owner-named, not verified).
**Links:** ETH-1126, ETH-1119, `21` owner.
**Trap:** Individual resilience cannot indefinitely substitute for systemic preparedness.
**Mains hook:** Design a pre-agreed protocol so EI is not a personal burden (qualified).
**Back:** *"Plan the succession, debrief the trauma, rotate the strain."*

### Flashcard ETH-1128 — Trap: EI means suppressing emotion
**Front:** Does emotional intelligence mean suppressing all emotion?
**Definition (learn):** No. It means recognising and regulating emotion appropriately; suppression without awareness is not EI.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner's trap (basic §6).
- Overemphasis on composure can suppress legitimate whistle-blowing (advanced §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 basic §6 and Advanced §6.
**Links:** ETH-1115, ETH-1120, ETH-1130.
**Trap:** "Stay calm and don't rock the boat" is a misuse of EI.
**Mains hook:** Pair composure with courage and integrity (qualified).
**Back:** *"Regulate, don't erase."*

### Flashcard ETH-1129 — Trap: high IQ means high EQ
**Front:** Does a high IQ automatically imply a high EQ?
**Definition (learn):** No. The two are conceptually and empirically distinct.
**Breakdown:**
- An analytically brilliant officer can make ethically poor decisions under emotional pressure.
- Links to the IQ/EQ complementarity card (ETH-1118).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 basic §6.
**Links:** ETH-1118, ETH-1113, ETH-1120.
**Trap:** Exam option "brilliant officers are emotionally intelligent" is false.
**Mains hook:** Assess EQ separately, not as a by-product of ability (qualified).
**Back:** *"Clever is not kind; measure both."*

### Flashcard ETH-1130 — Trap: empathy means bending rules
**Front:** Does empathy in administration mean bending rules for anyone who appears distressed?
**Definition (learn):** No. Empathy informs how a rule-bound decision is communicated and delivered, not whether the rule is waived arbitrarily.
**Breakdown:**
- Governing rule: empathy must be institutionalised, not personalised (ETH-1132).
- Remedy for need: route the person to an entitlement, not suspend the rule.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 basic §6 and Advanced §11A.
**Links:** ETH-1132, ETH-1133, ETH-1128.
**Trap:** A request that sounds sympathetic may still be a favour.
**Mains hook:** Compassion is realised through lawful flexibility, never through private suspension (qualified).
**Back:** *"Empathy changes the delivery, not the rule."*

### Flashcard ETH-1131 — PYQ anchors: components and learnability (2013 Q4; 2020 Q2(b))
**Front:** What do the 2013 Q4 and 2020 Q2(b) questions ask about EI?
**Definition (learn):**
- 2013 GS-IV Q4: what EI is, how it can be developed, and how it helps ethical decisions. Use definition, development, mechanism.
- 2020 GS-IV Q2(b): main components of EI, whether they can be learned. Use Goleman's five components, then trainability through reflection, feedback and practice.
**Breakdown:** ✅ owner 05 §7 and Advanced §10. Advanced: separate emotional awareness from ethical judgment (2013).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2013 GS-IV Q4; 2020 GS-IV Q2(b) (✅ owner 05).
**Links:** ETH-1115, ETH-1114, ETH-1118.
**Trap:** Training improves skills without making every trainee equally proficient (advanced §10).
**Mains hook:** Show learnability as a graded skill, not a binary (qualified).
**Back:** *"Thirteen defines, twenty components: learn them by practice."*

### Flashcard ETH-1132 — Empathy institutionalised, not personalised (governing rule)
**Front:** What is the governing rule the owner gives for empathy in public decisions?
**Definition (learn):** Empathy is the perception that a person's situation deserves a response. It is not an authorisation to supply that response through a personal exception.
**Breakdown:**
- A rule bent for one person is arbitrary distribution of advantage; its cost falls invisibly on everyone with equal or stronger claims.
- Compassion is a provision for a category, not an exception for a person (owner 11A boundary).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 05 §11A (analytical governing rule).
**Links:** ETH-1133, ETH-1134, ETH-1130, ETH-1136.
**Trap:** "I was only being kind" is not a defence against favour.
**Mains hook:** Institutionalise empathy in a category-wide provision (qualified).
**Back:** *"Provide for the class, never suspend for the person."*

### Flashcard ETH-1133 — Three-question test: stranger, lawful route, recordable reason
**Front:** What three questions must a favour-like request pass?
**Definition (learn):**
1. Would I do this for a stranger with identical facts? (universalisability; Kant, file 07).
2. Is there a lawful route serving the same need? (substitution, not suppression).
3. Can I record the reason and have it read publicly? (reasonable-observer test, file 09).
**Breakdown:** All three must be passed. A case passing all three is lawful flexibility applied to a class.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 05 Advanced §11A test (analytical).
**Links:** ETH-1132, ETH-1134, ETH-1135.
**Trap:** Passing two of three is failing.
**Mains hook:** Use the test as a checklist for any discretionary exception (qualified).
**Back:** *"Stranger, statute, sentence on file."*

### Flashcard ETH-1134 — Sympathy, empathy, compassion and favour (boundary table)
**Front:** Distinguish sympathy, empathy, compassion and favour as the owner does.
**Definition (learn):**
- **Sympathy:** feeling for another's distress; motivating but undirected.
- **Empathy:** understanding another's situation from their standpoint; the diagnostic input.
- **Compassion:** empathy plus motivation to relieve suffering; action-generating.
- **Favour:** satisfying the need through a personal exception to a general rule; an abuse of discretion.
**Breakdown:** The boundary is crossed at favour, not at compassion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 05 Advanced §11A table.
**Links:** ETH-1091, ETH-1132, ETH-1133.
**Trap:** Compassion is not favour merely because it is personal.
**Mains hook:** Concede the empathy; reject the favour; supply the lawful route (qualified).
**Back:** *"Feel for, understand, act for the class; never favour the one."*

### Flashcard ETH-1135 — 2026 GS-IV Q6(a) fact pattern: three defects
**Front:** What three defects does the owner find in the DM-PA-teacher transfer case (2026 GS-IV Q6(a))?
**Definition (learn):** A DM stops a schoolteacher's transfer on the request of his Personal Assistant, whose daughter has a learning disability and receives private tuition from the teacher. The question asks for empathy versus compliance with rules.
**Breakdown:**
1. Extraneous consideration and abuse of discretion: private tuition is not a relevant transfer criterion.
2. Conflict of interest: the PA is the DM's own subordinate; the conflict is at least apparent (reasonable-observer test, file 09).
3. The underlying premise is a breach: RTE Act, 2009, Section 28 prohibits private tuition by teachers (owner; statutory text not fetched; applicability depends on school category).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q6(a) (✅ owner 05 §11A and §2026 pointer). RTE §28 (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ text not fetched here).
**Links:** ETH-1136 (lawful route), ETH-1137, ETH-1138, ETH-1139 (trap), `09` and `21` owners.
**Trap:** Missing any of the three defects prevents a top-band answer.
**Mains hook:** Name the defects, then the lawful route; a refusal without a route is incomplete (qualified).
**Back:** *"Irrelevant ground, own staff, prohibited tutor."*

### Flashcard ETH-1136 — Lawful accommodation route for the child's need
**Front:** Which lawful routes meet the child's need in the 2026 case without a personal exception?
**Definition (learn):**
- Specific learning disability is a recognised disability under the Schedule to the RPwD Act, 2016 (✅ owner 05 §11A).
- RPwD Act §16: inclusive education, early detection of specific learning disabilities, reasonable accommodation.
- RPwD Act §31: free education for children with benchmark disability aged 6 to 18 in a neighbourhood or special school of choice.
- Samagra Shiksha inclusive-education components: remedial teaching, resource teachers (⚠️ scheme-based).
- Exam accommodations under §16 and board guidelines (⚠️).
- If the school genuinely loses its only subject teacher: a transparent, criteria-based, recorded transfer-policy exception applicable to all similar teachers through the board route, consistent with *T.S.R. Subramanian v. Union of India* (2013) (owner citation; ⚠️ directions not fetched).
**Breakdown:** Routing the person to an entitlement discharges compassion without suspending the rule.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** RPwD Act, 2016 §16, §31 (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ not fetched). *T.S.R. Subramanian* (2013) (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-1135, ETH-1132, ETH-1097 (transfer), `21` owner.
**Trap:** The exception was never necessary for the child; the answer must name the statutory route.
**Mains hook:** Every element of the need has a lawful universal answer (qualified: school disruption must be proved on the school's record).
**Back:** *"Sixteen and thirty-one: the child's rights, not the teacher's favour."*

### Flashcard ETH-1137 — Conduct rules frame: AIS (Conduct) Rules 1968 Rule 3 and CCS (Conduct) Rules 1964 Rule 3(1)
**Front:** What general obligations do the owner's conduct-rule citations impose?
**Definition (learn):** Every member of the service must maintain absolute integrity and devotion to duty, and do nothing unbecoming of a member of the service.
**Breakdown:**
- AIS (Conduct) Rules, 1968, Rule 3 and CCS (Conduct) Rules, 1964, Rule 3(1) (✅ owner 05 §11A; ⚠️ exact text not fetched).
- A decision the officer cannot record truthfully has failed the conduct standard.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As cited by owner (⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-1074 (CCS Rules 1964 Rules 5, 7), ETH-1135.
**Trap:** Citing a rule number for a favour without showing the conduct breach.
**Mains hook:** Conduct rules are the frame; the recordability test is the operational check (qualified).
**Back:** *"Absolute integrity, devotion, nothing unbecoming."*

### Flashcard ETH-1138 — PYQ map for the EI owner (2013–2026)
**Front:** Which GS-IV questions route to the EI owner?
**Definition (learn):**
- 2013 Q4: EI definition, development, ethical decisions.
- 2019 Q5(b): EI as ability to make emotions work for you (2019 Q5(a) is probity).
- 2020 Q2(b): components and learnability.
- 2021 Q4(b): whether EI helps overcome a crisis of conscience without compromising ethical stand.
- 2022 Q1(a)–(b): wisdom and travesty; empathy and compassion.
- 2023 Q4(a)–(b): EQ over cognitive ability; moral intuition vs reasoning.
- 2025 Q7: DC Vijay case.
- 2026 Q6(a): DM and PA transfer case.
**Breakdown:** 10 + 10 marks (150 words each) except 2025 Q7 (case study, 20 marks) (✅ owner 05 ledgers and pointer).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 ledgers. 2026 routing ledger is separate (owner pointer).
**Links:** ETH-1131, ETH-1123, ETH-1124, ETH-1126, ETH-1135.
**Trap:** 2019 Q5(a) probity and 2019 Q5(b) EI are one question with two parts.
**Mains hook:** Map each demand to a named EI component or to a rule before writing.
**Back:** *"Thirteen, nineteen, twenty, twenty-one, twenty-two, twenty-three, twenty-five, twenty-six."*

### Flashcard ETH-1139 — Trap: missing the conflict and the prohibited premise in the DM case
**Front:** Why does the DM-PA case fail if the answer only balances empathy and rules?
**Definition (learn):** The question contains a conflict of interest (own PA) and a statutory breach (RTE §28), which convert it from a compassion question into a conflict-of-interest question.
**Breakdown:**
- Trap 1: a balanced "empathy is important but rules matter too" essay.
- Trap 2: missing that the PA is the DM's own staff.
- Trap 3: missing that the tuition is itself prohibited (sympathetic fact is not mitigating).
- Trap 4: refusing without naming the lawful route.
- Trap 5: treating empathy as a weakness to suppress.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §11A traps.
**Links:** ETH-1135, ETH-1136, ETH-1128.
**Trap:** Five named traps, each of which caps the score.
**Mains hook:** Name the defects and the lawful route; empathy identified the need, but the remedy was wrong (qualified).
**Back:** *"Own staff, prohibited tuition, no route named: three misses."*

---

### GROUP — 06 Indian Moral Thinkers and Philosophers

### Flashcard ETH-1140 — Indian moral thinkers: core theme (realist and idealist registers)
**Front:** What is the core theme of the Indian Moral Thinkers owner, and what is its answer thesis?
**Definition (learn):** Indian moral thought spans structural realism (Kautilya) to spiritual service idealism (Vivekananda, Basava, Nanak), and Gandhi's ends-means and trusteeship idealism. A governance ethic needs both: controls that assume temptation and service that cultivates duty.
**Breakdown:**
- Realist register: Kautilya; design controls, reward and rotation.
- Idealist register: Gandhi, Vivekananda, Basava, Guru Nanak; service as duty, trust, dignity.
- Buddha's Middle Path as the synthesising procedure (advanced §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4th Report Annexure-I(2) and Box 2.8 (✅ owner 06). Standard primary sources for Buddha, Vivekananda, Basava and Guru Nanak (✅ owner 06 header).
**Links:** ETH-1141 (Kautilya), ETH-1143 (Buddha), ETH-1145 (Gandhi), ETH-1150 (Vivekananda), ETH-1151 (Basava), ETH-1152 (Nanak), ETH-1153 (Ambedkar).
**Trap:** Presenting Kautilya and Gandhi as the same ethic in different eras; they are structurally opposite registers (advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** Indian moral thought is not monolithic; durable governance ethics holds control-based and service-based approaches together (qualified).
**Back:** *"Control with Kautilya, conscience with Gandhi, and proportion from the Buddha."*

### Flashcard ETH-1141 — Kautilya: Arthashastra, rajadharma and structural theory of corruption
**Front:** Summarise Kautilya's contribution to administrative ethics: work, central claim, application.
**Definition (learn):** Kautilya's *Arthashastra* frames the ruler's duty of protection and welfare (rajadharma). Misuse of the treasury is a breach of that duty, and corruption is opportunity-driven, not only a private moral lapse.
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *Arthashastra* (classical Indian source quoted by the ARC).
- Central claim: assume opportunity-driven temptation is structural; design controls.
- Application: rotation, reward and disgorgement; permanence for those who increase the king's wealth "in just ways" (ETH-1046).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC Annexure-I(2), Kangle 2.9.32–34 (✅ owner 06 §3); rajadharma (✅ owner 06 §2).
**Links:** ETH-1045, ETH-1046, ETH-1047, ETH-1165 (trap), ETH-1140.
**Trap:** Calling the *Arthashastra* purely realpolitik with no ethical content is a named error (ETH-1165).
**Mains hook:** Kautilya gives the design logic for anti-corruption controls, but his remedy pairs vigilance with incentives; a purely surveillance-based regime demoralises honest officials (qualified).
**Back:** *"Rajadharma: the ruler guards the treasury, and design beats hope."*

### Flashcard ETH-1142 — Vivekananda: Practical Vedanta and "Daridra Narayana"
**Front:** Explain Vivekananda's Practical Vedanta and the "Daridra Narayana" idea.
**Definition (learn):** Practical Vedanta applies Advaita's non-dual metaphysics to ethical service. Serving the deprived is serving the divine; the sacred and the secular merge in public service motivation.
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *Complete Works*, Vol. III, "The Common Bases of Hinduism" (Lahore, 1897) (✅ owner 02, 06).
- Application: converts welfare from a bureaucratic obligation into a devotional and civic duty (⚠️ owner §3 translation).
- Dignity model: service is not charity (ETH-1164).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 06 §2 and Advanced §2, §7.
**Links:** ETH-1037 (selective learning), ETH-1038 (law vs morality), ETH-1164, ETH-1100 (compassion as design).
**Trap:** Daridra Narayana is not a licence for unbounded charity; it is a dignity-based service register.
**Mains hook:** Use Vivekananda for the motivational dimension of service, paired with procedural safeguards (qualified).
**Back:** *"Serve the poor as the divine: dignity first, never charity's superiority."*

### Flashcard ETH-1143 — Buddha: Middle Path, Four Noble Truths and Eightfold Path (administrative reading)
**Front:** State the Buddhist doctrines the owner uses and their administrative reading.
**Definition (learn):** Madhyama Pratipada (the Middle Path) is the balance between self-indulgence and self-mortification. The Four Noble Truths and the Eightfold Path (Right Action, Right Livelihood, Right Mindfulness among its limbs) give a route to balanced conduct.
**Breakdown:**
- Administrative reading: balance, proportionality and non-extremism (owner §2).
- Right Action → lawful conduct; Right Livelihood → honest occupation; Right Mindfulness → self-aware decision-making (⚠️ owner §3 mapping).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 06 §2 (Middle Path as a term); mapping ⚠️ owner analysis; full doctrine in `Philosophy/paper-1/indian/Buddhism.md` (owner cross-link).
**Links:** ETH-1144 (decision procedure), ETH-1158 (trap), ETH-1140.
**Trap:** Middle Path does not mean avoid firm decisions (ETH-1163 trap list; advanced §8).
**Mains hook:** The Middle Path is a proportionality test for decisions, not a preference for compromise (qualified).
**Back:** *"Neither indulge nor starve: the path between is the proportionate decision."*

### Flashcard ETH-1144 — Buddha's Middle Path as a decision procedure
**Front:** How does the advanced owner convert the Middle Path into a decision procedure?
**Definition (learn):** Before acting, test whether the contemplated response is an over-reaction (excessive, punitive, populist crackdown) or an under-reaction (complacent inaction), then correct toward proportionality.
**Breakdown:**
- Maps onto the case-study proportionality check (file 22).
- Example: a land-dispute mediation where the DM avoids both populism and paralytic risk-aversion (basic §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner advanced §3 (analytical application).
**Links:** ETH-1143, ETH-1166, `22` owner.
**Trap:** Proportionality is not midpoint compromise; the test is fit to the facts.
**Mains hook:** Use the procedure to evaluate vigilance design: is it over-suspicious or under-controlled? (qualified).
**Back:** *"Test the response for excess and for neglect; then fit it to the facts."*

### Flashcard ETH-1145 — Gandhi: Sarvodaya, Trusteeship, Satyagraha and Seven Social Sins
**Front:** Which Gandhian doctrines does the owner use, and what do they teach administrators?
**Definition (learn):**
- **Sarvodaya:** welfare of all.
- **Trusteeship:** wealth and power held in trust for society, not as personal property.
- **Satyagraha:** non-violent resistance.
- **Seven Social Sins:** ARC Box 2.8 (sourced to *Young India*, 1925) (ETH-1035).
**Breakdown:** Trusteeship converts office into a fiduciary holding (owner §2, §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 06 §2, §5; *Young India* and *Harijan* as the owner's publication references (✅ owner 06 §10B). Other works ⚠️ not in owner.
**Links:** ETH-1035, ETH-1146, ETH-1147, ETH-1148.
**Trap:** Trusteeship is not a wealth-redistribution proposal (ETH-1149 trap).
**Mains hook:** Gandhi supplies the internal disposition; institutions supply the proof (qualified).
**Back:** *"Sarvodaya, trust, satyagraha: the trustee's three words."*

### Flashcard ETH-1146 — Gandhi's ends-means unity: impure means corrupt good ends
**Front:** What is Gandhi's ends-means position, and how does it differ from consequentialism?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi held that impure means corrupt even a good end; the means must be as ethical as the end.
**Breakdown:**
- Contrast with consequentialism, which can justify a bad means for a good end (owner advanced §2; file 08).
- A strict deontological-virtue hybrid (⚠️ owner analytical classification).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner advanced §2 (classification).
**Links:** ETH-1145, ETH-1035 (politics without principles), `08` owner.
**Trap:** Saying Gandhi is a consequentialist because satyagraha aims at social change.
**Mains hook:** The ends-means test is a check on investigative or enforcement shortcuts that promise good outcomes (qualified).
**Back:** *"Good ends cannot be bought with foul means."*

### Flashcard ETH-1147 — Gandhi's Practical Trusteeship Formula (Harijan, 1952)
**Front:** What does the Practical Trusteeship Formula state?
**Definition (learn):** Trusteeship transforms, not abolishes, the existing order. The trustee has no right of ownership, only a right to an honourable livelihood "no better than that enjoyed by millions of others". The State regulates trusteeship in the public interest, with a statutory minimum and a limited maximum income.
**Breakdown:**
