- Each element attacks a named mechanism.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis.

**Links:** ETH-3027, ETH-3047, ETH-3049, ETH-3051.

**Trap:** "DPDP compliance settles the matter" and "ban AI in policing". The first ignores equality; the second is unimplementable.

**Mains hook:** Recommend a conditional continuation with the architecture, not continue or scrap. Qualified conclusion: keep the technology only with contestability and sunset.

**Back:** *"Six steps, four phases, one rule: score may point, never accuse."*

### Flashcard ETH-3063 — Forest Rights Act 2006, section 4(5): no eviction before recognition

**Front:** State FRA s.4(5) as quoted in the owner and identify the Gram Sabha's role in the Act.

**Definition (learn):** The Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act 2006, s.4(5) (owner 13 verbatim): "Save as otherwise provided, no member of a forest dwelling Scheduled Tribe or other traditional forest dweller shall be evicted or removed from forest land under his occupation till the recognition and verification procedure is complete." The Gram Sabha is the initiating authority for determining rights (s.6) and has protective functions (s.5). The Act was enacted in 2006 and came into force on 31 December 2007 (✅ Wikipedia; the Wikipedia article gives both 18 and 29 December 2006 as passage dates; the year is 2006 either way). The s.4(5) text was not verified against the Act in this build (⚠️).

**Breakdown:**
- Bar: no eviction before recognition and verification.
- Gram Sabha: initiating authority (s.6); protective duties (s.5).
- Individual and community rights under s.3(1).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2006 (enacted); 31 December 2007 (in force) (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ETH-3064, ETH-3065, ETH-3066.

**Trap:** "The FRA gives an absolute veto." It bars eviction before recognition and vests decision competence in the Gram Sabha.

**Mains hook:** Argue the specific provision. Qualified conclusion: displacement before recognition is unlawful, not merely unfair.

**Back:** *"No eviction until the verification is done; the Gram Sabha starts."*

### Flashcard ETH-3064 — The Niyamgiri case and Gram Sabha decision competence

**Front:** What did the Supreme Court hold in the Niyamgiri case, and why does it matter for FPIC?

**Definition (learn):** *Orissa Mining Corporation Ltd v. Ministry of Environment & Forest* ((2013) 6 SCC 476, decided 18 April 2013; owner 13, ⚠️ not verified in this build). The Court held that the Gram Sabhas of affected villages were the competent forum to decide the community's religious, cultural and community forest-rights claims over the Niyamgiri hills, and directed that their decision go to the Ministry for the final decision. All consulted Gram Sabhas rejected the proposal (owner). It converts consultation into a decision-making competence in the community.

**Breakdown:**
- Forum: Gram Sabha.
- Claim: religious and cultural rights to a sacred hill.
- Outcome: rejection by all consulted Gram Sabhas (owner).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (2013) 6 SCC 476; 18 April 2013 (⚠️ owner-only).

**Links:** ETH-3041, ETH-3063, ETH-3066.

**Trap:** Describing Niyamgiri as a general veto. It located competence in Gram Sabhas on specified claims.

**Mains hook:** Use Niyamgiri to show "recognitional injustice" is legally cognisable. Qualified conclusion: consent is a competence, not a formality.

**Back:** *"The Gram Sabha decides; the hill stays."*

### Flashcard ETH-3065 — PESA consultation versus RFCTLARR consent

**Front:** Distinguish PESA's land-acquisition provision from RFCTLARR's consent thresholds.

**Definition (learn):** PESA (Panchayats (Extension to the Scheduled Areas) Act 1996) mandates consultation with the Gram Sabha or Panchayat before land acquisition in Scheduled Areas, which is weaker than consent. RFCTLARR Act 2013 requires prior consent of at least 80% of affected families for private-company acquisition and 70% for PPP projects, carried out alongside the Social Impact Assessment, and Gram Sabha consent in Scheduled Areas (⚠️ owner 13; numbers not verified). (✅ Wikipedia for PESA's 1996 enactment as a Scheduled Areas Act.)

**Breakdown:**
- PESA: consultation.
- RFCTLARR: consent thresholds 80% (private) and 70% (PPP).
- SIA: mandatory public hearing.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PESA 1996; RFCTLARR 2013; 80% and 70% (⚠️ owner-only, written as statutory thresholds, not observed practice).

**Links:** ETH-3041, ETH-3063, ETH-3066.

**Trap:** Confusing PESA consultation with RFCTLARR consent.

**Mains hook:** Be honest about PESA's weaker standard. Qualified conclusion: consultation is a floor; consent is the standard for acquisition.

**Back:** *"PESA asks, RFCTLARR counts: eighty for private, seventy for PPP."*

### Flashcard ETH-3066 — Seven-part ethical challenge taxonomy and evaluation grid for displacement

**Front:** Name the seven ethical challenges in displacement and the grid's key tests.

**Definition (learn):** (1) Consent and agency. (2) Distributive injustice. (3) Procedural injustice. (4) Recognitional injustice (community valuation not registered). (5) Cultural and epistemic loss. (6) Intergenerational and ecological harm. (7) Conservation-induced displacement. The evaluation grid tests necessity; prior recognition; Gram Sabha consent competence; completeness of valuation (common property, minor forest produce, sacred sites); restoration of livelihood; sequencing (rehabilitation completed before dispossession); benefit-sharing; grievance and reversibility. (⚠️ owner 13 analysis.)

**Breakdown:**
- Seven kinds of wrong, not one list of hardships.
- Eight-test grid.
- Sequencing is the most common failure: "R&R will follow".

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis.

**Links:** ETH-3063, ETH-3064, ETH-3065, ETH-3041.

**Trap:** Treating compensation as the whole ethical question. It addresses distribution only, not consent, recognition or procedure.

**Mains hook:** Deploy three or four heads, then the grid. Qualified conclusion: displacement is survivable only when the community co-authors the decision and co-benefits.

**Back:** *"Consent, distribution, procedure, recognition, culture, future, conservation: and rehabilitation first."*

### Flashcard ETH-3067 — Clausewitz's "war as continuation of policy": attribution and the ethics gap

**Front:** Where does the line "war is the continuation of policy by other means" come from, and why should an ethics answer not treat it as a complete ethical position?

**Definition (learn):** The line is a paraphrase of Carl von Clausewitz's *Vom Kriege* (On War). Clausewitz died in 1831 with the work unfinished; his widow Marie edited and published it in 1832 (✅ Wikipedia). The Howard-Paret translation (Princeton, 1984) renders the phrase as "War is the continuation of policy with other means" (✅ Wikipedia). It is a descriptive strategic claim, not a normative theory of when war is justified. Pair it with the Just War framework (ETH-3019 to ETH-3023).

**Breakdown:**
- Source: *Vom Kriege*, published 1832.
- Translation caution: "policy" versus "politics" varies by translator.
- Ethical gap: descriptive versus normative.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Death 1831; publication 1832 (✅ Wikipedia). Owner 13 uses 2025 GS-IV Q2(a) as the paper context.

**Links:** ETH-3019, ETH-3020, ETH-3025.

**Trap:** Presenting the phrase as a verbatim English canonical quotation with one fixed wording.

**Mains hook:** Quote with attribution, then supply the normative framework. Qualified conclusion: strategy describes war; ethics asks whether and how it may be fought.

**Back:** *"Clausewitz describes war; the Just War asks whether it is just."*

### GROUP — 14 Probity: Concept and Philosophical Basis of Governance

### Flashcard ETH-3068 — Probity: the public-office standard of upright, impartial, public-purpose conduct

**Front:** Define probity and state why it is an institutional as well as a personal standard.

**Definition (learn):** Probity is upright, impartial and public-purpose conduct in entrusted public office. As an analytical test it must be capable of reasoned public justification and verification. It overlaps with honesty, integrity and propriety but does not reduce to any of them. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis; definition from the 2nd ARC framing.)

**Breakdown:**
- Public office is a trust, not personal property.
- Probity requires visibility: documented reasons, audit trails, disclosure.
- Not reducible to paperwork alone.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 grounded in 2nd ARC 4th Report (2007) and the Nolan principles (para 2.2.5). (✅ owner 14)

**Links:** ETH-3069, ETH-3071, ETH-3072.

**Trap:** "Probity is purely a personal virtue." It is defined by institutional verifiability.

**Mains hook:** Define, then show the verification mechanism. Qualified conclusion: probity is engineered into institutions as well as hoped for in officers.

**Back:** *"Upright, impartial, public, and provable."*

### Flashcard ETH-3069 — Probity, honesty, integrity, propriety and accountability: overlap and function

**Front:** Distinguish probity from honesty, integrity, propriety and accountability, and state why a rigid hierarchy is wrong.

**Definition (learn):** Honesty: truthfulness, non-deception, candid disclosure of relevant private interests. Integrity: consistent adherence to ethical principle and freedom from improper influence (ARC/Nolan integrity reproduces freedom from outside obligations that might influence duty). Propriety: appropriateness and fairness of conduct, even when lawful. Accountability: answerability to a competent forum that can review and secure correction. Probity integrates these in public office. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis; Nolan integrity wording ✅ Wikipedia partial.)

**Breakdown:**
- Honesty asks: is it truthful?
- Integrity asks: is it independent?
- Propriety asks: is it appropriate to the purpose of the power?
- Accountability asks: who can review and correct?
- Probity asks: is the whole conduct upright and verifiable?

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner table.

**Links:** ETH-3068, ETH-3071, ETH-3073.

**Trap:** Treating probity, integrity and honesty as interchangeable or as a nested hierarchy. Transparency alone does not complete accountability.

**Mains hook:** Use an overlap-and-function comparison, with an Indian administrative example. Qualified conclusion: each term asks a different question.

**Back:** *"Honest says true, integral says independent, proper says fitting, accountable says answerable."*

### Flashcard ETH-3070 — UNESCAP's eight characteristics of good governance

**Front:** Name UNESCAP's eight characteristics of good governance as quoted in ARC Annexure-I(1).

**Definition (learn):** Good governance "has 8 major characteristics. It is participatory, consensus oriented, accountable, transparent, responsive, effective and efficient, equitable and inclusive and follows the rule of law." (✅ owner 14, quoting ARC Annexure-I(1), National Colloquium 2006. The UNESCAP Wikipedia page was not reached in this build.)

**Breakdown:**
- Participatory; consensus oriented; accountable; transparent; responsive.
- Effective and efficient; equitable and inclusive; follows the rule of law.
- Probity is one part of a systemic set of characteristics.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eight characteristics (✅ owner 14).

**Links:** ETH-3068, ETH-3069, ETH-3075.

**Trap:** "Good governance is achieved once officials are honest." Systemic characteristics are required beyond personal probity.

**Mains hook:** Link individual probity to the eight characteristics. Qualified conclusion: probity is necessary but not sufficient for good governance.

**Back:** *"Eight doors: participate, agree, answer, show, respond, deliver, include, obey the rule."*

### Flashcard ETH-3071 — Nolan's Seven Principles of Public Life

**Front:** What are the seven principles, when were they first published, and what does the integrity principle say?

**Definition (learn):** The Nolan Committee (Committee on Standards in Public Life) published its first report in 1995 with seven principles: selflessness, integrity, objectivity, accountability, openness, honesty and leadership (✅ Wikipedia). The integrity principle: "Holders of public office must avoid placing themselves under any obligation to people or organisations" that might influence them, must not take decisions to secure financial or material gain for themselves, relatives or friends, and must declare and resolve interests and relationships (✅ Wikipedia, partial quotation). The 2nd ARC cites the Nolan integrity principle (owner 14).

**Breakdown:**
- Seven principles.
- Integrity: freedom from obligations; no self-interest decisions; declare interests.
- Owner 09 covers Nolan's integrity principle in depth.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1995 (first report). (✅ Wikipedia)

**Links:** ETH-3069, ETH-3068, ETH-3072.

**Trap:** Using "integrity" in the Nolan sense as its full ordinary meaning. Owner 14 notes the ARC formulation is narrower.

**Mains hook:** Map each principle onto a probity failure. Qualified conclusion: the Nolan principles convert private virtue into public conduct rules.

**Back:** *"Selfless, honest, open, objective, accountable, leading, and of integrity."*

### Flashcard ETH-3072 — Public office as trust: the philosophical anchor

**Front:** Explain the public-office-as-trust framing and its philosophical sources.

**Definition (learn):** Public authority and money are entrusted for public purpose, not personal entitlement. It is a fiduciary or trusteeship analogy, drawing on Gandhi's trusteeship (owner 06), Locke's trust theory (⚠️ owner 14; not verified in this build), deontological duty (Kant) and virtue ethics (Aristotle's phronesis). It is an analogy, not a settled Indian doctrine for public office. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis.)

**Breakdown:**
- Deontology: duty owed to the public trust regardless of detection.
- Virtue: habituated public-office excellence.
- Trusteeship: Gandhian stewardship of resources.
- Constitutional morality (ETH-3081) extends it institutionally.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis; Kant's universalisability and humanity formula (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ETH-3073, ETH-3076, ETH-3080.

**Trap:** Presenting the public-trust analogy as a judicial holding about all public office.

**Mains hook:** Ground the philosophy in one named theory. Qualified conclusion: the trust framing makes probity publicly demonstrable.

**Back:** *"Office is an entrustment; the entrusted must show the books."*

### Flashcard ETH-3073 — The public-trust doctrine and *M.C. Mehta v. Kamal Nath*

**Front:** What is the public-trust doctrine as applied in Indian jurisprudence, and what year does *M.C. Mehta v. Kamal Nath* carry?

**Definition (learn):** The doctrine holds that certain public goods and natural resources are held in trust for present and future citizens and cannot be handed over for private commercial ownership. *M.C. Mehta v. Kamal Nath* is the Indian authority, held to be "part of the law of the land". (✅ Wikipedia: decided 13 December 1996, reported as (1997) 1 SCC 388.) Owner 14 gives the year as 1997. ⚠️ Conflict: the decision date is 1996 and the report year is 1997; cite both precisely.

**Breakdown:**
- Natural resources: State as trustee.
- Extension to public office generally: analogy, not settled law (owner 14).
- Roman origin: *res communes omnium* (✅ Wikipedia).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 December 1996; (1997) 1 SCC 388 (✅ Wikipedia). Owner's "1997" flagged.

**Links:** ETH-3039, ETH-3072, ETH-3074.

**Trap:** "The public-trust doctrine is settled Indian law for public office." It is settled for natural resources.

**Mains hook:** Keep the natural-resource doctrine distinct from the office analogy. Qualified conclusion: the doctrine supports, but does not prove, public-office trusteeship.

**Back:** *"Nineteen-ninety-six decided, ninety-seven reported: natural resources are held, not owned."*

### Flashcard ETH-3074 — Section 75A of the Representation of the People Act 1951: parliamentary asset declaration

**Front:** What does s.75A require, when was it inserted, and what is its reach?

**Definition (learn):** Section 75A (inserted by the Representation of the People (Third Amendment) Act 2002, owner 14) requires every elected candidate for a House of Parliament to file asset and liability details within 90 days of taking oath. The Members of Lok Sabha and Members of Rajya Sabha (Declaration of Assets and Liabilities) Rules 2004 prescribe the form. Owner 14 notes ARC 2.5.6.1 wrongly adds "or the Legislature of the State"; the enacted text covers Parliament only. ⚠️ The s.75A provision was not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia page for the Act in this build.

**Breakdown:**
- Who: elected candidates for a House of Parliament.
- When: within 90 days of taking oath.
- Form: 2004 Rules.
- Reach: not State legislators.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2002 insertion; 90 days; 2004 Rules (⚠️ owner-only; the Act's Wikipedia page did not mention s.75A).

**Links:** ETH-3075, ETH-3076, ETH-3073.

**Trap:** Saying s.75A covers State legislators.

**Mains hook:** Show how private finances become a verifiable record. Qualified conclusion: disclosure is a probity mechanism, not a guarantee of honesty.

**Back:** *"Ninety days after oath, for Parliament only, on the 2004 form."*

### Flashcard ETH-3075 — The Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act 2013: dates and the current section 44

**Front:** When was the Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act passed and brought into force, and what does its s.44 say now?

**Definition (learn):** The Act was passed in 2013, received presidential assent on 1 January 2014 and took effect on 16 January 2014 (Act No. 1 of 2014) (✅ Wikipedia). Owner 14 states that s.44, as substituted by a 2016 amendment retrospective from 16 January 2014, reads simply that every public servant shall make a declaration of assets and liabilities "in such form and manner as may be prescribed", replacing the earlier, more elaborate publication regime. ⚠️ Not confirmed by the fetched page; treat the 2016 amendment as owner-sourced and verify.

**Breakdown:**
- 2013 passed; 1 January 2014 assent; 16 January 2014 in force.
- Current s.44: prescribed-form declaration (owner).
- Do not describe a universal public online declaration duty as the current Act's position (owner).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 January 2014; 16 January 2014 (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ETH-3074, ETH-3076, ETH-3077.

**Trap:** Describing the earlier, elaborate publication regime as the present law.

**Mains hook:** Use the Lokpal Act to show probity law evolves. Qualified conclusion: prescribed-form disclosure relies on service-rule returns in practice.

**Back:** *"Passed in thirteen, in force in January fourteen; section forty-four speaks of form."*

### Flashcard ETH-3076 — Annual immovable-property returns under the conduct rules

**Front:** Which conduct rules require annual immovable-property returns for civil servants, and for which groups?

**Definition (learn):** Owner 14 names Rule 18 of the Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules 1964 (Group A and B officers) and Rule 16 of the All India Services (Conduct) Rules 1968 as the primary service-rule probity instruments, converting private financial conduct into an auditable record. (⚠️ owner 14 only; the rule numbers were not verified in this build.)

**Breakdown:**
- CCS (Conduct) Rules 1964: Rule 18 (owner).
- AIS (Conduct) Rules 1968: Rule 16 (owner).
- Purpose: annual disclosure of immovable property.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rule numbers and years (⚠️ owner-only).

**Links:** ETH-3074, ETH-3075, ETH-3078.

**Trap:** Citing these rules as Lokpal Act provisions.

**Mains hook:** Show service-rule probity alongside statutory disclosure. Qualified conclusion: service rules carry most of the current disclosure load.

**Back:** *"Rule eighteen for the central, sixteen for the All-India."*

### Flashcard ETH-3077 — Central Vigilance Commission: founding year and mandate

**Front:** When was the Central Vigilance Commission founded and what is its mandate?

**Definition (learn):** The CVC was founded in 1964. It is an autonomous body that oversees vigilance activity across the central government and advises its agencies on tackling corruption (✅ Wikipedia).

**Breakdown:**
- Founded 1964.
- Autonomous.
- Oversees vigilance; advises agencies.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1964 (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ETH-3078, ETH-3075, ETH-3079.

**Trap:** Describing the CVC as an investigative agency with prosecutorial powers. Its mandate is oversight and advice.

**Mains hook:** Use the CVC to show probity is institutionally supervised. Qualified conclusion: oversight quality depends on independence and follow-through.

**Back:** *"Nineteen-sixty-four: oversee, advise, don't prosecute."*

### Flashcard ETH-3078 — Prevention of Corruption Act 1988

**Front:** When was the Prevention of Corruption Act enacted and what is its purpose?

**Definition (learn):** The Prevention of Corruption Act was signed and commenced on 9 September 1988. It aims to fight bribery and graft in government bodies and state-run enterprises, consolidating and updating earlier anti-corruption law (✅ Wikipedia).

**Breakdown:**
- 9 September 1988: signed and commenced.
- Purpose: bribery and graft in government and state enterprises.
- Consolidates earlier law.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 September 1988 (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ETH-3077, ETH-3076, ETH-3017.

**Trap:** Confusing the 1988 Act with the Lokpal Act 2013.

**Mains hook:** Use the Act as the penal backstop for probity lapses. Qualified conclusion: penal law deters but does not build probity; systems do.

**Back:** *"Eighty-eight, September the ninth: bribes are crimes."*

### Flashcard ETH-3079 — Presumption of regularity versus presumption of suspicion: risk-graded scrutiny

**Front:** State the design tension between presuming official good faith and presuming suspicion, and the risk-graded resolution.

**Definition (learn):** A regime built on suspicion paralyses honest risk-taking; a regime built on presumed regularity under-detects concealed misconduct. The advanced resolution: routine, low-discretion decisions use presumed regularity with light audit; high-discretion, high-value decisions (procurement, land allotment, licensing) require proactive disclosure and pre-decision documentation. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis; links to owner 21.)

**Breakdown:**
- Suspicion-only: paralysis.
- Regularity-only: under-detection.
- Risk-graded: calibrated scrutiny.
- Excess documentation for trivial decisions is a burden, not probity.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis.

**Links:** ETH-3077, ETH-3080, ETH-3068.

**Trap:** "Maximum scrutiny of every decision maximises probity." It can demoralise honest officials.

**Mains hook:** Recommend risk grading. Qualified conclusion: calibrate scrutiny to actual risk, not uniform suspicion or uniform trust.

**Back:** *"Trust the routine, document the discretion."*

### Flashcard ETH-3080 — Procedural versus substantive probity

**Front:** Distinguish procedural from substantive probity and state why both are needed.

**Definition (learn):** Procedural probity concerns whether a decision followed transparent, auditable, rule-bound process. Substantive probity concerns whether the outcome served the public interest. A process can be impeccable while serving a narrow interest, and a substantively good outcome can come through a flawed process. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis.)

**Breakdown:**
- Procedure: records, rules, reasons.
- Substance: public-interest outcome.
- Both necessary.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis.

**Links:** ETH-3079, ETH-3068, ETH-3072.

**Trap:** Assuming a clean file proves a good decision.

**Mains hook:** Show probity needs both rules and results. Qualified conclusion: procedure disciplines discretion; substance tests it.

**Back:** *"Clean file, right outcome: both or neither."*

### Flashcard ETH-3081 — Constitutional morality as systemic probity

**Front:** How does owner 14 extend probity beyond individual conduct to institutions?

**Definition (learn):** Probity includes fidelity to the constitutional restraint of power. An institution can show systemic probity or its absence through design (checks and balances, transparency defaults, audit architecture), independent of any single official's honesty. The owner cross-links constitutional morality to owner 10. (⚠️ owner 14 analysis; constitutional morality's Ambedkar origin not verified in this build.)

**Breakdown:**
- Individual probity versus systemic probity.
- Design features: checks, transparency, audit.
- Constitutional restraint of power.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only.

**Links:** ETH-3072, ETH-3079, ETH-3080.

**Trap:** Locating probity only in officers.

**Mains hook:** Argue that systems carry probity. Qualified conclusion: institutional design is the stronger lever than exhortation.

**Back:** *"Institutions can be upright, or not, without any single officer."*

### Flashcard ETH-3082 — PYQ route map for probity (2014-2025)

**Front:** Which GS-IV PYQs test probity, and what is each one's demand?

**Definition (learn):** 2014 Q4 (meaning of probity, difficulties, overcoming them). 2018 Q11 (case: charitable hospital tax irregularities; proportionate enforcement). 2019 Q5(a) (meaning of probity and measures). 2021 Q6(b) (integrity as an empowering value; distinguish from probity). 2022 Q5(b)(iii) (short note on probity in public life). 2023 Q5(b) (probity essential for effective governance and development). 2024 Q6(a) (high standards of probity for women public servants). 2025 Q1(b) (constitutional morality; owner 14 isolates it). (✅ owner 14 PYQ ledgers.)

**Breakdown:**
- Define; diagnose; prescribe (2014 pattern).
- Link probity to outcomes (2023 pattern).
- Keep 2019 and 2023 separate; they are different questions.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 14).

**Links:** ETH-3068, ETH-3079, ETH-3081.

**Trap:** Merging 2019 Q5(a) and 2023 Q5(b). They are two distinct questions often quoted together.

**Mains hook:** Use the three-part structure: definition, obstacles, measures. Qualified conclusion: probity is verifiable rectitude sustained by systems.

**Back:** *"Fourteen defines, nineteen measures, twenty-three links, twenty-four gendered."*

### Flashcard ETH-3083 — Deontology, virtue and public trust as probity's philosophical roots

**Front:** Name the three philosophical sources that owner 14 draws on for probity and what each contributes.

**Definition (learn):** Deontology (Kant): probity is owed as a duty regardless of detection; Kant's universal-law formulation asks whether a maxim can be willed universally, and the humanity formulation says never treat persons solely as means (✅ Wikipedia). Virtue ethics (Aristotle): probity as a habituated practised excellence of public character, with phronesis (practical wisdom) as the faculty of judging particular cases (⚠️ owner 14; Wikipedia check not completed). Public trust (Locke and Gandhi's trusteeship): office as entrustment (⚠️ owner 14; Locke and Gandhi not verified in this build).

**Breakdown:**
- Deontology: duty regardless of detection.
- Virtue: character built by practice.
- Trust: entrustment for public purpose.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kant formulas (✅ Wikipedia); others owner-only.

**Links:** ETH-3072, ETH-3068, ETH-3081.

**Trap:** Presenting the three as a single theory. Each asks a different question: duty, character, entrustment.

**Mains hook:** Name one classical theory in an answer. Qualified conclusion: the three together justify verifiable probity, not private honesty alone.

**Back:** *"Duty regardless, character by practice, office as entrustment."*

## ETHICS — Part 4: Transparency and RTI, Codes, Citizens' Charters and Public Funds

> One flashcard per examinable item in the Ethics owners 15–18 (basic + advanced), plus one concept card per owner file. IDs: ETH-4001 onward.
> **Verification status:** built from the repository owners. Claims checked against fetched pages are tagged ✅ with the source; owner-only facts are tagged ✅ owner NN; unverified or memory-only details are tagged ⚠️. Owner-vs-source conflicts are flagged inside the card. Time-sensitive facts carry the owner's as-of date (29 August 2026) or are marked.

---

### GROUP — Transparency, RTI and Information Sharing (owner 15)

### Flashcard ETH-4001 — Core theme: transparency enables accountability, but only a forum completes it
**Front:** Explain the causal chain linking information, transparency and accountability, and say what breaks it.
**Definition (learn):** Information reduces the asymmetry between the State and the citizen; visibility permits verification; verification supports answerability; a competent forum converts answerability into correction or consequence.
**Breakdown:**
- Information asymmetry -> visibility -> verification -> answerability -> forum with corrective power -> correction or consequence.
- The chain can break at every stage: a data dump may be visible but unintelligible; an exposé may reveal wrongdoing but produce no remedy.
- Transparency is a condition of institutional visibility; disclosure is one act of release.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level causal chain (✅ owner 15 §1, marked ⚠️ as analysis in the owner).
**Links:** ETH-4002 (term comparison), ETH-4003 (RTI Section 2), ETH-4024 (privacy limit), ETH-4026 (PYQ routes).
**Trap:** Treating "visible" as "accountable". A dashboard that nobody must explain is not accountability.
**Mains hook:** Define transparency, then show it is an institutional condition, not a one-off release. Qualify with the risk of unintelligible or unsearchable publication. Conclude that the corrective forum completes the chain.
**Back:** *"See it, check it, answer for it, correct it — the forum closes the loop."*

### Flashcard ETH-4002 — Six terms that are not synonyms: transparency, disclosure, information sharing, open data, accountability, privacy
**Front:** Distinguish transparency, disclosure, information sharing, open data, accountability and privacy in one table.
**Definition (learn):** Each term names a different design property of public information.
**Breakdown:**
- Transparency = an institutional condition in which exercise of public power is visible to appropriate scrutiny (not one compelled release).
- Disclosure = release of specified information to an applicant or the public (not a complete accountability system).
- Information sharing = purpose-bound movement among authorised actors; may remain restricted, not automatically public.
- Open data = data made available for reuse, normally machine-readable with metadata (not a substitute for reasons or appeal).
- Accountability = an identified actor answers against a standard before a competent forum that can judge and correct.
- Privacy = protection of dignity, autonomy and contextual control over personal information (not a blanket shield for every record touching a public servant).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner comparison table (✅ owner 15 §1).
**Links:** ETH-4001, ETH-4009 (open data vs RTI), ETH-4025 (sharing checklist).
**Trap:** Information sharing between agencies is not public transparency; open data is not RTI.
**Mains hook:** Use the six-term table to show that digital government needs all six kept distinct, otherwise citizens are either over-exposed or under-informed.
**Back:** *"Share with purpose, disclose by law, publish for reuse, answer to a forum, protect the person."*

### Flashcard ETH-4003 — RTI Section 2: "information" and "right to information"
**Front:** What does the RTI Act 2005 (Section 2) include in "information" and "right to information"?
**Definition (learn):** Information is material in any form; the right to information is the statutory right to access it through defined modes.
**Breakdown:**
- Information includes records, documents, memos, e-mails, opinions and advice already recorded, press releases, circulars, orders, logbooks, contracts, reports, papers, samples, models and electronic data.
- It also covers private-body information that a public authority can access under another law.
- Right to information includes inspection of work/records, notes or certified extracts, certified copies and certified samples, and access in recognised electronic modes.
- "Record" is medium-neutral: digitisation changes storage, not the access principle.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner Section 2 architecture (✅ owner 15 §2). Act No. 22 of 2005; assent 15 June 2005; full commencement 12 October 2005 (✅ Wikipedia: Right to Information Act, 2005).
**Links:** ETH-4004 (public authority), ETH-4005 (Section 3), ETH-4006 (existing-record boundary).
**Trap:** A requester cannot demand a new analysis; the right is to information held or controlled, not to research products the office never prepared.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a medium-neutral definition makes RTI future-proof; qualify that access is still only as good as the record system behind it (ETH-4007).
**Back:** *"Any form, any record, inspect or copy — held, not invented."*

### Flashcard ETH-4004 — "Public authority": the four routes and the trap of "any public money"
**Front:** Through which routes is a body a "public authority" under RTI, and why is "receives any public money" an unsafe answer?
**Definition (learn):** A public authority is a body covered by the Act through constitutional, statutory, notification/control or substantial-financing routes.
**Breakdown:**
- Route 1: established or constituted by the Constitution.
- Route 2: established or constituted by a law made by Parliament or a State legislature.
- Route 3: owned, controlled or substantially financed by the government (control/notification route).
- Route 4: non-government bodies substantially financed, directly or indirectly, by government funds.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner (✅ owner 15 §2). The four-route wording is the Act's structure as stated in the owner; the statutory text should be checked before quoting clause numbers.
**Links:** ETH-4003, ETH-4026.
**Trap:** Owner warns: do not write that every private entity receiving any public money is automatically covered. Substantial financing is the test, not any receipt.
**Mains hook:** Use the routes to show RTI's reach follows public power and public money, while noting that over-extension would chill private participation.
**Back:** *"Constituted, created by law, controlled, or substantially funded — not merely paid."*

### Flashcard ETH-4005 — Section 3: rights-holder, no stake required
**Front:** Who holds the right to information under Section 3, and must the applicant prove a personal stake?
**Definition (learn):** Section 3 confers the right on citizens; the applicant ordinarily need not prove a personal stake or special injury.
**Breakdown:**
- Rights-holder: citizen (owner's wording).
- Burden: the applicant need not justify curiosity.
- Ethical shift: the citizen moves from supplicant to rights-holder using a statutory route, and the State must link withholding to law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2 (Section 3 text). Section 3 wording confirmed by owner; ⚠️ Wikipedia fetch did not return the clause text.
**Links:** ETH-4011 (timelines), ETH-4013 (exemptions), ETH-4001.
**Trap:** Section 3 is not a right to all information without exception; the exemption architecture of Section 8 applies.
**Mains hook:** Show the rights-holder logic changes the burden of governance: the State must justify withholding, the citizen need not justify asking.
**Back:** *"Citizen asks, no stake needed, State must justify the no."*

### Flashcard ETH-4006 — Existing-record boundary: RTI supplies what exists, not what should have existed
**Front:** Why can RTI not manufacture a reason for a decision that the file never recorded?
**Definition (learn):** The PIO supplies information held or controlled; the Act does not generally require creation of a new forecast, legal opinion, explanation or research product.
**Breakdown:**
- If the reason already exists on file, it is information and must be given (subject to exemptions).
- If no reason was recorded, RTI cannot manufacture one; the absence itself may reveal an accountability failure.
- Routine sanction, inspection or movement records are existing information.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2 (existing-record boundary); India-centric example in owner: inspection reports, sanction orders and recorded movement of a delayed mutation file.
**Links:** ETH-4003, ETH-4007 (record management), ETH-4001.
**Trap:** Demanding "why governance is inefficient" as an RTI question; the PIO need not answer an abstract question.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a missing reason is itself an accountability signal and a call for record-keeping reform (ETH-4007), not a licence to fabricate after the fact.
**Back:** *"Recorded reasons come out; unrecorded reasons stay unrecorded — and that silence is evidence."*

### Flashcard ETH-4007 — Section 4(1)(a): records must be catalogued, indexed and networked
**Front:** What does Section 4(1)(a) require of public authorities regarding records?
**Definition (learn):** Public authorities must maintain records duly catalogued and indexed in a manner that facilitates RTI, computerise appropriate records within a reasonable time subject to resources, and connect systems through networks where access is facilitated.
**Breakdown:**
- Catalogue and index (retrieval).
- Computerise appropriate records within reasonable time, subject to resources.
- Network systems where access is facilitated.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3 (Section 4(1)(a)).
**Links:** ETH-4008 (proactive publication), ETH-4009 (publication quality), ETH-4006.
**Trap:** Record management is ethical precondition, not back-office housekeeping. Owner's stewardship failures: no retention schedule, missing metadata, scanned image-only files, vendor lock-in, silent correction, excessive collection (✅ owner 15 advanced §2).
**Mains hook:** Link record quality to RTI effectiveness: poor catalogues convert RTI into a cost borne by the citizen; qualify that resources limit the pace.
**Back:** *"Catalogue it, index it, network it — or RTI becomes a treasure hunt."*

### Flashcard ETH-4008 — Section 4(1)(b) and 4(2): proactive publication and suo motu disclosure
**Front:** List the high-yield categories of Section 4(1)(b) and state the Section 4(2) duty.
**Definition (learn):** Section 4(1)(b) prescribes a proactive-publication architecture; Section 4(2) requires a constant endeavour to provide as much information suo motu as possible so that the public has minimum resort to individual applications.
**Breakdown:**
- Section 4(1)(b) categories: organisation and functions; powers and duties; decision procedure and supervision channels; norms; rules and manuals; document categories; consultation arrangements; boards and committees; directory and remuneration; budget and expenditure; subsidy programmes and beneficiaries; concessions/permits; electronic information; citizen facilities; PIO particulars.
- Section 4(2): regular-interval suo motu communication to minimise individual RTI applications.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3.
**Links:** ETH-4007, ETH-4009, ETH-4015.
**Trap:** Counting disclosed categories is not compliance; disclosure must be usable (ETH-4009).
**Mains hook:** Proactive disclosure can reduce backlog, but it can shift cost to citizens if portals are unusable; qualify with a human assistance route.
**Back:** *"Publish the map of power, and fewer people need to file to find the road."*

### Flashcard ETH-4009 — Usable information: the six publication tests (and "uploaded is not transparent")
**Front:** What makes a publication ethically substantive rather than "compliance theatre"?
**Definition (learn):** Publication is ethical only when it is current, findable, intelligible, accessible, reusable and safe.
**Breakdown:**
- Current: obsolete budget or PIO lists mislead.
- Findable: buried files or broken links impose hidden access cost.
- Intelligible: unexplained codes preserve asymmetry.
- Accessible: local-language and disability access affect equal citizenship.
- Reusable: machine-readable data supports analysis, though a speaking order may still be a PDF.
- Safe: aggregation, masking and suppression protect personal or security-sensitive details.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4008, ETH-4010, ETH-4024.
**Trap:** Unsearchable scanned dumps can be compliance theatre. "Maximum publication can still be unethical if data are personal, unsafe, unusable or misleading."
**Mains hook:** Use the six tests to show that the quality of transparency is an ethical variable; qualify that maximum volume is not maximum transparency.
**Back:** *"Current, findable, intelligible, accessible, reusable, safe — the six doors of usable truth."*

### Flashcard ETH-4010 — Request route: Sections 5 and 6 (PIO, written or electronic request, transfer within five days)
**Front:** Explain the operational route from application to transfer under Sections 5 and 6.
**Definition (learn):** Section 5 creates Central/State PIOs and assistants as access points; Section 6 governs the request.
**Breakdown:**
- Request may be written or electronic.
- May be in English, Hindi or the official language of the area.
- Must specify the information sought.
- No reasons or unnecessary personal details required.
- Oral requesters: reasonable assistance must reduce the request to writing.
- Section 6(3): a request concerning another public authority, or more closely connected with it, is transferred as soon as practicable and within five days; applicant informed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §4 (Section 6(3) five-day transfer as stated in owner; ⚠️ not separately fetched).
**Links:** ETH-4011 (timelines), ETH-4005.
**Trap:** Section 6(3) transfer is not refusal and does not start a fresh "no decision" appeal clock by itself; it is a routing duty.
**Mains hook:** Simple filing and assistance counter procedural domination; a technically available right becomes unequal if only legally skilled citizens can use it.
**Back:** *"Write or e-mail, say what you want, not why — misdirected? Transfer in five."*

### Flashcard ETH-4011 — Section 7 timelines: 30 days ordinarily, 48 hours for life or liberty
**Front:** What are the RTI response periods under Section 7 for an ordinary request and for life or liberty?
**Definition (learn):** The ordinary request must be decided as expeditiously as possible and within 30 days; information concerning life or liberty must be supplied within 48 hours where the nexus is established.
**Breakdown:**
- Ordinary request: 30 days.
- Life or liberty: 48 hours (urgent exception, not a label for every important request).
- Connection between information delay and threatened life or liberty must be shown.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia (RTI Act 2005): reply "within thirty days, or within 48 hours in case of the matter involving a petitioner's life and liberty." ✅ owner 15 §4.
**Links:** ETH-4012 (deemed refusal and free supply), ETH-4018 (appeals), ETH-4020 (Section 24 timeline distinct at 45 days).
**Trap:** 48 hours is not the answer for every urgent-feeling request; it needs a life-or-liberty nexus. Section 24 human-rights information has its own 45-day route (ETH-4020).
**Mains hook:** Argue that predictable time limits counter procedural domination; qualify that the 48-hour route must be used narrowly to avoid being diluted.
**Back:** *"Thirty days for most, forty-eight hours when a life hangs on it."*

### Flashcard ETH-4012 — Deemed refusal, free supply after delay, and rejection-notice duties (Section 7)
**Front:** What follows when a PIO misses the statutory period, and what must a rejection notice contain?
**Definition (learn):** Non-decision within time is a deemed refusal, enabling appeal; information delayed beyond the statutory period is supplied free of charge under Section 7(6).
**Breakdown:**
- No decision in time -> deemed refusal -> appeal available.
- Delayed beyond statutory period -> information supplied free of charge (Section 7(6)).
- Below-poverty-line applicant: fee protection under the statutory proviso/rules.
- Sensory disability: assistance must enable access to the record.
- Rejection must communicate reasons, the appeal period and the appellate authority.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §4 (table of Section 7 protections).
**Links:** ETH-4011, ETH-4018, ETH-4019.
**Trap:** Free supply after delay is a fee rule, not a penalty; penalty under Section 20 is a separate, conditional consequence (ETH-4019).
**Mains hook:** Deemed refusal makes delay itself a reviewable decision; qualify that it depends on appeal being accessible in practice.
**Back:** *"Silence is a refusal you can appeal; late means free."*

### Flashcard ETH-4013 — Section 8(1): exemption families and the interest each protects
**Front:** Name the Section 8(1) exemption families and the interest each protects.
**Definition (learn):** RTI is disclosure-oriented, not absolute; Section 8(1) protects specified interests. The exam method is to connect clause, interest and likely harm.
**Breakdown:**
- Sovereignty, integrity, security, strategic/scientific/economic interests, foreign relations, incitement.
- Court-forbidden information or contempt.
- Legislative privilege.
- Commercial confidence, trade secrets, intellectual property (subject to public-interest architecture).
- Fiduciary relationship (must fit the relationship, not mere official possession).
- Foreign-government confidence.
- Safety of a person or a confidential source.
- Investigation, apprehension or prosecution (test is actual impediment).
- Cabinet papers (deliberation protected within statutory conditions; decisions and reasons have their own proviso).
- Personal information (current text: see ETH-4022).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5 (table).
**Links:** ETH-4014 (override and balancing), ETH-4016 (severability), ETH-4022 (personal information).
**Trap:** "Security" must not become an unreviewed slogan; "fiduciary" must fit the relationship; investigation exemption requires actual impediment, not mere connection with a file.
**Mains hook:** Show each exemption as a narrow tool; over-broad use converts secrecy into the default. Qualify that genuine operational and source-safety interests remain protected.
**Back:** *"Eight clauses, each guarding one harm — name the harm, not just the clause."*

### Flashcard ETH-4014 — Section 8(2) public-interest override and the balancing test
**Front:** How does Section 8(2) override exemptions and the Official Secrets Act, and what is the balancing sequence?
**Definition (learn):** Section 8(2) permits access notwithstanding the Official Secrets Act or Section 8(1) exemptions where public interest in disclosure outweighs harm to protected interests.
**Breakdown:**
- Balancing sequence: exact clause -> protected interest -> concrete and likely harm -> specific public benefit of disclosure -> can redaction/aggregation reduce harm? -> reasoned decision + appeal.
- Public-interest factors: exposure of corruption, health/safety, public expenditure, environmental harm, rights violation, integrity of public decision.
- Curiosity, private rivalry or sensational value are weak factors.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5.
**Links:** ETH-4013, ETH-4015, ETH-4016.
**Trap:** Public interest is not whatever attracts curiosity; countervailing harm must also be concrete. Do not use "public interest" as an unreasoned conclusion either way.
**Mains hook:** The balancing test is the constitutional-style proportionality step inside RTI; qualify that the public-interest override is a reasoning duty, not a discretionary favour.
**Back:** *"Harm must be concrete, benefit must be specific, and reasons must be written."*

### Flashcard ETH-4015 — Section 8(3): the twenty-year disclosure rule (not automatic declassification)
**Front:** What does Section 8(3) provide, and what does it not provide?
**Definition (learn):** Section 8(3) creates a twenty-year disclosure rule, subject to continuing statutory exceptions.
**Breakdown:**
- Period: twenty years.
- Not a universal automatic declassification rule: specified clauses survive beyond the period.
- Dispute over the originating date: Central Government determination route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5 (Section 8(3)). ⚠️ Not verified against Wikipedia fetch (page omitted the clause).
**Links:** ETH-4013, ETH-4014, ETH-4026.
**Trap:** Saying "every document becomes public after twenty years" is wrong; the continuing exceptions matter.
**Mains hook:** Argue for harm-based classification review rather than fixed-period secrecy; qualify that long-lived source or security interests still need protection.
**Back:** *"Twenty years opens the door, but not every lock is removed."*

### Flashcard ETH-4016 — Section 10: severability and the 100-page report
**Front:** What is severability under Section 10, and how does it avoid blanket denial?
**Definition (learn):** If exempt information is reasonably separable from the rest, access must be given to the non-exempt part.
**Breakdown:**
- Example: 100-page inspection report; 3 pages reveal a protected source or personal identifiers (redact or withhold); 97 pages contain findings, expenditure and remedial action (disclose).
- Notice must communicate decision, reasons and material findings, decision-maker, fees and review rights.
- Impossible redaction need not be pretended; the authority should explain why.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §6.
**Links:** ETH-4013, ETH-4014, ETH-4017.
**Trap:** Blanket denial where narrower protection works is unethical. Redaction must be marked; silent alteration is falsification (advanced owner 15 §4).
**Mains hook:** Severability is the mechanism that reconciles privacy and accountability; qualify with the risk of mosaic re-identification (advanced).
**Back:** *"Cut the protected lines, keep the public page."*

### Flashcard ETH-4017 — Section 11: third-party notice and the timelines (consultation, not consent)
**Front:** Give the Section 11 third-party timelines and the key trap.
**Definition (learn):** Where information relates to or was supplied by a third party and treated as confidential, the PIO gives written notice, invites representation and then makes the statutory decision.
**Breakdown:**
- Notice to third party within five days of receiving the request.
- Representation opportunity within ten days of notice.
- Decision within forty days of the request on the Section 11 route.
- Decision notice informs the third party of appeal rights.
- Trade or commercial secrets protected by law receive statutory treatment; public interest still relevant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §6 (five/ten/forty days as stated by owner; ⚠️ not separately fetched).
**Links:** ETH-4016, ETH-4013 (commercial confidence), ETH-4018.
**Trap:** Section 11 creates consultation, not consent. A contractor cannot veto disclosure merely by objecting; the PIO must still not ignore legitimate confidence.
**Mains hook:** Use the decision method (identify confidential component -> notice -> test harm -> assess public interest -> sever -> speaking decision -> appeal) to show fairness to all parties.
**Back:** *"Five to notify, ten to reply, forty to decide — objection is heard, not obeyed."*

### Flashcard ETH-4018 — Section 19 appeal ladder: first appeal, second appeal and the burden of proof
**Front:** Lay out the Section 19 appeal ladder and who bears the burden of proof.
**Definition (learn):** A PIO decision or deemed refusal may be appealed first within the public authority and then to the Information Commission (Central or State).
**Breakdown:**
- First appeal within public authority: ordinarily 30 days.
- First appellate decision: ordinarily 30 days, extendable to 45 with written reasons.
- Second appeal to CIC/SIC: ordinarily 90 days.
- Delay may be condoned for sufficient cause.
- Section 19(5): the burden of proving denial was justified lies on the PIO who denied the request.
- Commission decisions are binding within the Act; it can require compliance steps (access, appointing PIO, publication, record changes, training).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (day limits as stated by owner; the Wikipedia fetch described the sequence but gave no day limits — ⚠️ day limits to be checked against the Act text).
**Links:** ETH-4012, ETH-4019, ETH-4021 (Commission independence).
**Trap:** Confusing the first appeal (within the authority) with the second appeal (to the Commission). The burden of proof lies on the PIO, not on the applicant.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a documented appeal-and-penalty route changes the burden of justification; qualify that appellate formality without quality orders does not deliver access.
**Back:** *"First inside, then the Commission; the PIO proves the denial."*

### Flashcard ETH-4019 — Section 20 penalty: ₹250 per day, capped at ₹25,000, conditional and personal
**Front:** What is the Section 20 penalty, and why is it not automatic?
**Definition (learn):** After a reasonable opportunity of being heard, the Information Commission may impose ₹250 per day for specified conduct, capped at ₹25,000.
**Breakdown:**
- Conduct covered: refusal without reasonable cause, delay, malafide denial, knowingly incorrect/incomplete/misleading information, destruction or obstruction.
- PIO may escape by showing reasonable and diligent conduct.
- Persistent or malafide failure may lead to a disciplinary recommendation under service rules.
- Distinguish: access remedy, compensation, personal penalty and disciplinary recommendation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (₹250/day; ₹25,000 cap). ⚠️ The Wikipedia fetch did not return the Section 20 figures; check against the Act text before quoting.
**Links:** ETH-4012, ETH-4018, ETH-4026.
**Trap:** "Penalty is automatic for every delay" is wrong. It is personal, conditional and capped; it is also not a fine on the department.
**Mains hook:** Use the four-way distinction to show accountability is layered; qualify that deterrence depends on Commissions actually imposing penalties.
**Back:** *"Two-fifty a day, twenty-five thousand at most, and only if the PIO cannot show diligence."*

### Flashcard ETH-4020 — Section 24: intelligence and security organisations, with corruption and human-rights exceptions
**Front:** Are intelligence and security organisations outside RTI, and what are the exceptions?
**Definition (learn):** Notified intelligence and security organisations are generally outside the Act, but information concerning allegations of corruption or human-rights violations is not absolutely excluded.
**Breakdown:**
- Human-rights information: requires Information Commission approval and is supplied within 45 days, notwithstanding the ordinary Section 7 period.
- State Governments have a parallel notification architecture.
- Central organisations are listed in the Second Schedule (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia: intelligence and security organisations exempted "except in cases of corruption or human rights violation"; ✅ owner 15 §7 (45 days).
**Links:** ETH-4011, ETH-4013 (security exemption), ETH-4026.
**Trap:** "Security organisation" does not mean "no RTI under any circumstances." The human-rights route is not the ordinary 30-day route.
**Mains hook:** Show how the exception keeps the security exemption narrow; qualify that legitimate source and operational safety must remain protected.
**Back:** *"Security is shielded, but corruption and rights abuse still knock."*

### Flashcard ETH-4021 — RTI (Amendment) Act, 2019: Commission tenure and salary moved to Central rules
**Front:** What did the RTI (Amendment) Act, 2019 change about Information Commissions, and what is the balanced concern?
**Definition (learn):** The Amendment changed Sections 13 and 16 so that tenure, salaries, allowances and other service conditions of the CIC/ICs and State CIC/SICs are prescribed by the Central Government; Section 27 rule-making power was expanded.
**Breakdown:**
- Pre-2019: the Act fixed central and state commissioner tenure at five years (or until age 65, whichever earlier), with salaries tied to specified offices.
- 2019 Rules (not the Amendment text) prescribe a three-year term, subject to age ceiling.
- Independence concern: a watchdog's security of tenure and pay is now set by the executive it reviews; federal concern because State Commissions are covered.
- Counter-position: uniform conditions and administrative rationalisation.
- The change does not prove interference, abolish RTI rights or remove the appeal/penalty architecture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia: Amendment bill sought to amend Sections 13, 16 and 27; passed 25 July 2019; pre-2019 Section 13 term five years or age 65 (✅ Wikipedia). ✅ owner 15 §8 (three-year term in 2019 Rules).
**Links:** ETH-4018, ETH-4019, ETH-4026, ETH-4001.
**Trap:** The Amendment did not itself state "three years"; the three-year term is in the 2019 Rules. It did not abolish the citizen's right.
**Mains hook:** State it as a risk to insulation, not a finding of misconduct. Qualify that independence also depends on transparent merit-based appointment, timely vacancies and resources.
**Back:** *"Tenure moved to rules, the right stayed — insulation, not a verdict."*

### Flashcard ETH-4022 — Section 8(1)(j) after DPDP Act: the substituted "personal information" clause (13 November 2025)
**Front:** What is the current text of RTI Section 8(1)(j), and what is its commencement position as at 29 August 2026?
**Definition (learn):** DPDP Act 2023 Section 44(3) substituted clause (j) with "information which relates to personal information"; MeitY G.S.R. 843(E) commenced Section 44(3) on publication, 13 November 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Former wording (public activity / unwarranted invasion / larger public interest) is no longer clause (j).
- Sections 8(2), 10 and 11 remain; their interaction with the substituted clause needs careful reasoning.
- DPDP phased: immediate tranche includes Section 44(3); one-year tranche (Section 6(9) and 27(1)(d)) commences 13 November 2026; eighteen-month tranche (Sections 3–5, rest of 6, 7–17) commences 13 May 2027.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §9 (📰 as at 29 August 2026); ⚠️ G.S.R. 843(E) text not independently fetched in this build.
**Links:** ETH-4013, ETH-4014, ETH-4024.
**Trap:** Do not say the whole DPDP Act came into force in November 2025. Do not say Section 8(2) disappeared, nor that it automatically restores the old test.
**Mains hook:** Two overstatements to avoid: "privacy defeats every RTI request" and "nothing changed." Qualify with statutory review and pending adjudication.
**Back:** *"Clause (j) now says personal information — and the DPDP is phased, not switched on."*

### Flashcard ETH-4023 — Official Secrets Act 1923 versus RTI: Section 22 overriding effect
**Front:** Does the Official Secrets Act 1923 still apply, and what does RTI Section 22 do about it?
**Definition (learn):** The OSA 1923 remains in force; RTI Section 22 gives the RTI Act overriding effect to the extent of inconsistency with the OSA and other instruments.
**Breakdown:**
- Second ARC First Report recommended repeal and relocation of genuine official-secrets provisions into a national-security law (reform recommendation, not enacted law).
- An OSA label does not end the RTI inquiry; the authority must use RTI's exemption, public-interest, severability and appeal architecture.
- Section 22 does not mean every classified record must be disclosed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §9 (OSA 1923; Section 22; Second ARC First Report).
**Links:** ETH-4014, ETH-4016, ETH-4026 (2019 PYQ).
**Trap:** "Section 22 repealed the OSA" is false. "The OSA automatically bars RTI" is also false.
**Mains hook:** Argue for harm-based classification, RTI adjudication and review; qualify that operational security and source safety can justify withholding.
**Back:** *"OSA stays on the books; RTI's tests overrule its reflexes."*

### Flashcard ETH-4024 — Puttaswamy privacy framework for State information practices
**Front:** What framework should a State information practice apply after *Puttaswamy* (2017)?
**Definition (learn):** In *Justice K.S. Puttaswamy (Retd.) v Union of India* (nine-judge bench, 24 August 2017) the Supreme Court recognised privacy as a fundamental right; for State information practices use legality, legitimate aim, proportionality and procedural safeguards.
**Breakdown:**
- Legality: a valid law or rule.
- Legitimate State aim.
- Rational connection and necessity/proportionality.
- Procedural safeguards, oversight and remedy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §9 (case date and bench). ⚠️ Not fetched in this build; citation details to be confirmed before quoting.
**Links:** ETH-4010, ETH-4022, ETH-4025.
**Trap:** Do not claim a useful administrative objective alone defeats privacy; do not reduce Puttaswamy to consent alone (advanced owner 15 §10).
**Mains hook:** Use the four-step test to show privacy and transparency are reconcilable through legality and proportionality; qualify with contextual privacy.
**Back:** *"Legal, legitimate, proportionate, safeguarded — privacy is not a consent checkbox."*

### Flashcard ETH-4025 — Purpose-bound information sharing: the nine-step checklist
**Front:** What checks must precede sharing government data between departments?
**Definition (learn):** Government possession is not unlimited permission to circulate; sharing needs authority, purpose, necessity, accuracy, access/security, retention, citizen protection, public layer and review.
**Breakdown:**
- Authority -> purpose (no vague "future use") -> necessity (field-by-field) -> accuracy/provenance -> access/security (role-based, logs) -> retention (no immortal shadow copies) -> citizen protection (notice, human review, correction) -> public layer (safe aggregates) -> review/sunset.
- Example: linking welfare and health databases may identify duplicates; a match is an investigation lead, not automatic cancellation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §10 (checklist) and advanced §9 (sunset/reauthorise).
**Links:** ETH-4002, ETH-4024, ETH-4022.
**Trap:** Interoperability can become surveillance by accretion; "anonymised" is a claim requiring testing, not a magic label.
**Mains hook:** Transparency paradox: make public power visible, keep citizen data purpose-limited. Qualify that urgent lawful sharing may proceed with later review.
**Back:** *"Authority, purpose, need, check, lock, delete, tell, publish, review."*

### Flashcard ETH-4026 — Owner 15 PYQ routes: 2018 Q2(b), 2019 Q4(b) and cross-routes
**Front:** Which PYQs anchor Topic 15, and what does each demand?
**Definition (learn):** Direct ownership: 2018 Q2(b) and 2019 Q4(b). Cross-routes: 2018 public interest; 2018 Snowden disclosure; 2019 institutional ethics systems; 2021 social audit; 2024 ethics code and transparency; 2025 constitutional accountability and public-fund accountability.
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Q2(b): RTI redefines accountability, not empowerment alone.
- 2019 Q4(b): OSA as an obstacle to RTI implementation. Thesis: OSA can obstruct through broad wording and secrecy culture; Section 22 prevents it being an automatic trump card; the remedy is harm-based classification, RTI adjudication and review.
- Official local GS-IV papers control exact question text (✅ owner 15 advanced §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §10 and advanced §10.
**Links:** ETH-4001, ETH-4014, ETH-4023, ETH-4019.
**Trap:** Routing ledgers establish ownership, not permission to invent or merge demands.
**Mains hook:** For 2019 Q4(b), write: OSA obstacle (culture + overbreadth) -> Section 22 -> Sections 8, 8(2), 10 -> proportionate protection and review.
**Back:** *"Accountability needs RTI, and secrecy needs a reason, not a habit."*

---

### GROUP — Codes of Ethics and Codes of Conduct (owner 16)

### Flashcard ETH-4027 — Core theme: an ethics system needs both a compass and guardrails
**Front:** Why do public institutions need both a Code of Ethics and a Code of Conduct?
**Definition (learn):** Entrusted public office carries authority, discretion, money, information and coercive power, so role obligations are stricter than ordinary private life. Values (Code of Ethics) and specific conduct rules (Code of Conduct) answer different needs and together produce ethical competence plus fair accountability.
**Breakdown:**
- Public-action/private-interest interface: conduct acceptable in private life may need disclosure, restriction or abstention in office (Second ARC).
- Values without operational rules become rhetoric; rules without values encourage literal, evasive or fear-driven compliance.
- Output: ethical competence + fair accountability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §1 (Second ARC, Fourth Report, *Ethics in Governance*, 2007, ch. 2).
**Links:** ETH-4028 (comparison), ETH-4029 (enforceability), ETH-4048 (competence), ETH-4055 (PYQ routes).
**Trap:** Treating codes as alternatives. The answer is complementarity, not choosing one.
**Mains hook:** Define both, then show the compass-guardrail logic; qualify that a detailed rulebook can teach "everything not prohibited is acceptable" (ETH-4051).
**Back:** *"Values point the way, rules fix the lines — a code needs both."*

### Flashcard ETH-4028 — Code of Ethics versus Code of Conduct: the controlling distinction
**Front:** Compare a Code of Ethics and a Code of Conduct on form, question, content, enforcement, strength and weakness.
**Definition (learn):** A Code of Ethics states broad guiding values and principles; a Code of Conduct states specific expected or prohibited behaviour, precisely and unambiguously.
**Breakdown:**
- Core form: broad values vs specific behaviour.
- Main question: "what should a worthy public servant aspire to be?" vs "who must do or avoid what, in which circumstances, through what procedure?"
- Typical content: integrity, selflessness, impartiality, empathy, openness, accountability, service vs gifts, political activity, official information, outside employment, property returns, conflict disclosure.
- Enforcement: not automatically sanctionable as aspiration vs enforceable when issued under competent authority.
- Strength: guides novel dilemmas vs gives notice, consistency and a basis for discipline.
- Weakness: symbolic or vague vs mechanical, loophole-driven or minimum compliance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §2; ARC para 2.2.6 describes Code of Ethics as broad guiding principles (✅ owner 16).
**Links:** ETH-4027, ETH-4029, ETH-4034.
**Trap:** "Code of Ethics has no sanction; Code of Conduct has sanction" is a useful first contrast but an unsafe absolute.
**Mains hook:** Use the table to distinguish, then add the incorporation qualification (ETH-4029).
**Back:** *"Ethics aspires, conduct commands — but a valid command can carry an ethic."*

### Flashcard ETH-4029 — Aspirational language versus validly incorporated standards: the enforceability test
**Front:** When does a value in a code become enforceable, and why is "ethics can never be enforced" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** An aspirational Code of Ethics is not legally self-executing; a standard validly incorporated into an enforceable regime can become disciplinary misconduct when breached.
**Breakdown:**
- Enforceability requires: (1) competent legal or institutional source; (2) sufficiently clear standard; (3) identified authority and procedure; (4) evidence and impartial finding; (5) proportionate consequence; (6) review or appeal where provided.
- Accurate distinction: aspirational language by itself vs a standard validly incorporated into an enforceable regime.
- Integrity or conflict duties in a competent rule can ground discipline.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §2 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4028, ETH-4050 (due process), ETH-4053 (Article 311), ETH-4054.
**Trap:** Two errors: "values can never be enforced" and "values are automatically penal."
**Mains hook:** Write the incorporation-and-due-process qualification into any "distinguish" answer; conclude that aspiration guides judgment, rules discipline.
**Back:** *"Aspiration binds no one until a valid rule adopts it — then due process decides."*

### Flashcard ETH-4030 — The Nolan (Seven Principles of Public Life) and its origin
**Front:** Name the seven Nolan principles of public life and state the founding facts of the Nolan Committee.
**Definition (learn):** The seven principles are the UK standard for public office: Selflessness, Integrity, Objectivity, Accountability, Openness, Honesty and Leadership.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Selflessness: holders should act "solely in terms of the public interest."
- 2. Integrity: avoid obligations that could improperly sway; no decisions for financial or material gain to self, family or friends; declare and resolve interests.
- 3. Objectivity: impartial, fair, on merit, using best evidence.
- 4. Accountability: answer to the public and accept scrutiny.
- 5. Openness: transparent action; withhold only for clear and lawful reasons.
- 6. Honesty: "should be truthful."
- 7. Leadership: model principles, respect others, challenge poor behaviour.
- Committee established by PM John Major in 1994; first report 1995.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia (Nolan principles). Principle text partly paraphrased by the source.
**Links:** ETH-4027, ETH-4031 (translation), ETH-4037 (neutrality), ETH-4041 (gifts).
**Trap:** The Nolan principles are aspirational; the Wikipedia source does not characterise them as legally enforceable. Owner 09 (cross-topic) treats them as an aspirational floor linked to a Code of Conduct.
**Mains hook:** Use the seven principles as the shared floor, then show how each becomes a rule (ETH-4031); qualify that they carry no direct sanction without a linked code.
**Back:** *"Selfless, Integrity, Objective, Accountable, Open, Honest, Leading — the seven doors of public trust."*

### Flashcard ETH-4031 — From value to rule: the translation chain and rule-design checklist
**Front:** Show how a public-service value becomes an enforceable rule, and list the rule-design checks.
**Definition (learn):** A value is translated into role-specific meaning, foreseeable risk, a specific rule with exception and procedure, then record, advice, monitoring, remedy and periodic learning.
**Breakdown:**
- Chain: value -> role meaning -> foreseeable risk -> rule + exception + procedure -> record + advice + monitoring + remedy -> learning.
- Translation matrix: Integrity (risk: benefit distorts duty; conduct: refuse/report gifts, disclose interests; mechanism: gift register, approval, audit); Impartiality (recusal, reassignment, reasons); Political neutrality (role separation, written directions); Openness (disclosure, appeal, audit trail); Accountability (inquiry, corrective order, reporting); Service (accessible channel, grievance remedy); Stewardship (procurement control, utilisation audit).
- Checklist: define coverage; define risk and required/prohibited conduct; state exceptions and approval authority; distinguish prior permission, prior intimation and later reporting; set valuation, record and time rules; state who advises, investigates and decides; specify consequences through competent instrument; preserve notice, hearing, reasons, review; include anti-circumvention; review periodically.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §3.
**Links:** ETH-4030, ETH-4032, ETH-4040, ETH-4049.
**Trap:** A disclosure form that is never reviewed does not institutionalise integrity; a register revealing unnecessary family data may violate privacy without improving control.
**Mains hook:** Argue that translation must preserve the purpose of the value; qualify that disclosure is not always better.
**Back:** *"Value, role, risk, rule, record, review — the ladder from word to deed."*

### Flashcard ETH-4032 — Six tests for a translated rule (advanced)
**Front:** What six tests should every translated rule pass?
**Definition (learn):** A rule should be authoritative, understandable, fit the risk, proportionate, administrable and reviewable.
**Breakdown:**
- Authority: who may issue and enforce it?
- Notice: can the covered person understand the duty?
- Fit: does it address the identified risk?
- Proportionality: is the burden no greater than needed?
- Administrability: can disclosures and approvals be processed?
- Reviewability: are reasons and remedies available?
- Excessive family-data collection can cause privacy harm and false positives.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 advanced §4 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4031, ETH-4041, ETH-4050.
**Trap:** More disclosure is not automatically better; role- and risk-sensitive disclosure is the test.
**Mains hook:** Use the six tests to critique an over-broad gift or asset rule; qualify that under-inclusive rules also fail.
**Back:** *"Authority, notice, fit, proportion, workability, review — six tests, one rule."*

### Flashcard ETH-4033 — India's architecture: what exists and what is missing
**Front:** What code architecture already exists in India, and what precise gap remains?
**Definition (learn):** India has enforceable conduct rules for civil servants, a Ministers' Code of Conduct and legislative ethics arrangements; the gap is the comprehensive, coherent civil-service Code of Ethics recommended by the Second ARC, which has not been implemented as a distinct national layer.
**Breakdown:**
- Civil servants: Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964; All India Services (Conduct) Rules, 1968.
- Ministers: a ministerial Code of Conduct, examined and extended by the Second ARC's recommendations.
- Legislators: House ethics arrangements (Rajya Sabha Committee on Ethics, ETH-4045).
- Suggested formulation: enforceable conduct rules with ethical duties exist; comprehensive ARC-recommended Code of Ethics remains unimplemented.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §4; DoPT updated CCS (Conduct) Rules page (✅ owner 16 §17; live as verified in owner).
**Links:** ETH-4034, ETH-4035, ETH-4044, ETH-4045.
**Trap:** "India has no ethics in its service rules" and "India has no Code of Conduct for public servants" are both false.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose the gap precisely: a missing distinct national ethics layer above existing conduct rules, not a total absence of conduct control.
**Back:** *"Conduct rules exist; the ethics layer above them is still missing."*

### Flashcard ETH-4034 — The direct code-gap PYQ: 2024 Q5(a) (and the 2025 numbering correction)
**Front:** Which GS-IV question directly asks about the missing Code of Ethics, and how is 2025 numbered?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-IV Q5(a) states that a Code of Conduct is already in operation whereas a Code of Ethics is not yet put in place. In the 2025 local paper, Q5(a) concerns devotion to duty; the organisational code question is 2025 GS-IV Q6(a).
**Breakdown:**
- 2024 Q5(a): design a suitable ethics model while acknowledging existing conduct rules.
- 2025 Q5(a): devotion to duty, responsibility and fulfilment.
- 2025 Q6(a): "for an ethical work culture, there must be code of ethics…" (value- and compliance-based culture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §4 and §18 (official local papers 2024 and 2025 as verified by owner).
**Links:** ETH-4033, ETH-4055.
**Trap:** Do not relabel the 2024 code-gap question as 2025; do not confuse 2025 Q5(a) with the gap question.
**Mains hook:** Start with 2024 Q5(a)'s premise (conduct exists, ethics code not in place) and then design the model.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four asks for the missing code; twenty-five Q6 asks for the culture."*

### Flashcard ETH-4035 — Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules: the rule families to know
**Front:** Which CCS (Conduct) Rules anchor the exam map, and what does each cover?
**Definition (learn):** The 1964 Rules contain general conduct and specific families of conduct rules that translate values into enforceable duties.
**Breakdown:**
- Rule 3: general conduct (integrity, devotion to duty, political neutrality, fairness, accountability, transparency, public interest).
- Rule 5: taking part in politics and elections.
- Rule 8: connection with press or other media.
- Rule 9: criticism of Government.
- Rule 11: communication of official information.
- Rule 13: gifts.
- Rule 15: private trade or employment.
- Rule 18: movable, immovable and valuable property.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §5 (rule families). Thresholds and amendment positions NOT stated; ⚠️ verify in the current consolidated rules before quoting any rupee figure.
**Links:** ETH-4036, ETH-4037, ETH-4038, ETH-4040, ETH-4041, ETH-4042, ETH-4043.
**Trap:** Quoting a gift threshold from memory. The owner explicitly says do not.
**Mains hook:** Use Rule 3 as the bridge that converts values into conduct duties, then name the specific rule families for gifts, property and outside work.
**Back:** *"Three general, five specific: general conduct, politics, press, criticism, information, gifts, trade, property."*

### Flashcard ETH-4036 — Rule 3 as the bridge, and the oral-direction protocol
**Front:** How does Rule 3 bridge ethics and conduct, and what is the correct response to an oral direction?
**Definition (learn):** Rule 3 contains absolute integrity and devotion to duty and expanded standards (high ethical standards, neutrality, merit, fairness, impartiality, accountability, transparency, responsiveness, courtesy, public-interest decisions, efficient use of resources, conflict declaration, professionalism). Official directions should be in writing where practicable; a subordinate receiving an oral direction must seek written confirmation.
**Breakdown:**
- Response sequence: (1) clarify direction and facts; (2) state legal or ethical concern respectfully; (3) seek written confirmation; (4) do not alter or destroy records; (5) use authorised superior, vigilance, legal or review channel; (6) refuse an unlawful act rather than hide behind "orders"; (7) preserve service in genuine emergencies.
- Urgency may justify oral coordination; material directions should be regularised and recorded.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §5.
**Links:** ETH-4035, ETH-4037, ETH-4038.
**Trap:** Rule 3 does not make every oral instruction invalid; it requires recording material directions.
**Mains hook:** Use this protocol for a question on an unlawful order; qualify that emergency operations need coordination and later regularisation.
**Back:** *"Ask in writing, refuse the unlawful, never destroy the trail."*

### Flashcard ETH-4037 — Political neutrality: candid non-partisan advice plus lawful implementation
**Front:** What does political neutrality require of a civil servant, and what is it not?
**Definition (learn):** Neutrality is professional, not moral emptiness: the civil servant gives candid non-partisan advice before a decision and implements lawful decisions faithfully afterwards.
**Breakdown:**
- Before decision: accurate facts, candid options, warning about illegality, non-partisan advice.
- After lawful decision: competent implementation, equal citizen treatment, truthful communication, no party favour.
- Three-sided duty: to elected government (faithful lawful implementation), to Constitution and law (resist unlawful instructions), to future governments and citizens (preserve capacity and records).
