- Examples: unclear document list; repeated biometric mismatch; unexplained transfer; inconsistent eligibility advice; wrong data not corrected; grievance closed without action.
- Ethical response is upstream repair, not more downstream complaint staff.
- Paired indicator: entitlement-realisation rate (eligible households actually receiving full entitlement) alongside leakage.
- A spike in exception requests, repeat visits or filings is a system alarm.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 §16A.5 and §17 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4090, ETH-4094, ETH-4049.
**Trap:** Reading failure demand as beneficiary misbehaviour.
**Mains hook:** Use failure demand to argue the metric must change; qualify that not all complaints are system failures.
**Back:** *"Count the repeat visit as the system's own alarm."*

### Flashcard ETH-4094 — Balanced dashboard and metric gaming
**Front:** Name the nine dashboard metrics and the gaming risk each carries.
**Definition (learn):** A balanced dashboard combines outcome, accuracy, first-contact resolution, repeat grievance, appeal reversal, accessibility, group-wise outcome, citizen feedback and sample-file audit.
**Breakdown:**
- Final-outcome timeliness (easy cases prioritised). Accuracy/correction (errors hidden outside review period). First-contact resolution (superficial closure). Repeat grievance (duplicates suppressed). Appeal reversal (appeals discouraged). Accessibility (only registered users counted). Group-wise outcome (sensitive categories not examined). Citizen feedback (fear, selection or popularity bias). Sample-file audit (weak sample or predictable audit).
- Goodhart-style adaptations: postpone complex cases; reject before cut-off; generic closure; survey only easy users; remove offline access; discourage correction requests.
- Balanced control: quantitative metric + case-sample audit + citizen verification + group-wise equity check + reason and appeal analysis.
- Report variation, not only the mean.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 §17 and advanced §9 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4072, ETH-4093, ETH-4110.
**Trap:** An average can improve while remote districts, disability claims or women without phones worsen.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a plural dashboard audited for gaming; qualify that numbers may reflect misconduct, impossible demand, broken technology or honest reporting.
**Back:** *"Nine lenses, one audit, and always look past the average."*

### Flashcard ETH-4095 — Ten ethical failure modes and the cause-to-response table
**Front:** List the ten failure modes and map causes to responses.
**Definition (learn):** The failure modes are paper charter, impossible charter, weak charter, clock gaming, easy-case selection, premature closure, digital burden transfer, agent capture, retaliation and heroic dependency.
**Breakdown:**
- Cause -> response: deliberate manipulation -> evidence, fair inquiry, proportionate consequence; skill gap -> training and supervision; unclear rule -> authoritative clarification; fragmented workflow -> process redesign; understaffing -> leadership and resource decision; technology defect -> correction, contingency, vendor accountability; impossible standard -> transparent revision after consultation; harmful incentive -> redesign appraisal and dashboard.
- Accountability fails when every problem is called misconduct or every problem "system failure".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 §18 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4058, ETH-4067, ETH-4100.
**Trap:** Blaming individuals for structural failure, or hiding misconduct under "system".
**Mains hook:** Diagnose causation before choosing a response.
**Back:** *"Ten ways to fail, eight causes, one diagnosis before any sanction."*

### Flashcard ETH-4096 — Service recovery: the nine-step sequence and remedy types
**Front:** Give the nine-step recovery sequence and the remedy types.
**Definition (learn):** When delivery fails, the institution should not begin with reputation management.
**Breakdown:**
- Sequence: acknowledge failure without prejudging disputed facts; protect citizen from further loss; restore service where due; correct records and consequences; explain and state review; investigate deliberate breach fairly; identify system causes; communicate corrective change; verify recovery.
- Remedies: service completion; correction; reconsideration; apology or explanation; fee refund or waiver where authorised; compensation only where legally or administratively authorised; disciplinary action through due process; process redesign.
- Concealment protects short-term image but deepens distrust.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 advanced §8 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4077, ETH-4050, ETH-4095.
**Trap:** Promising compensation without authority; an apology replacing the service.
**Mains hook:** Argue recovery restores the citizen before it repairs the institution.
**Back:** *"Acknowledge, protect, restore, correct, explain, investigate, learn, verify."*

### Flashcard ETH-4097 — Disposal versus resolution: the distinction that protects dashboards from reality
**Front:** Why is "disposed" not the same as "resolved"?
**Definition (learn):** Disposal is administrative closure; resolution is a justified outcome communicated and, where due, a correctable failure addressed.
**Breakdown:**
- Complaint disposal protects a dashboard; service recovery restores the citizen (advanced recovery thesis).
- Some grievances are unfounded; reasoned refusal is a valid resolution.
- Resolution-quality table (ETH-4072) supplies companion indicators.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 advanced §7 and §14.
**Links:** ETH-4072, ETH-4096, ETH-4094.
**Trap:** Counting closed tickets as service.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to diagnose a dashboard that looks good while citizens remain unserved.
**Back:** *"Closed is not corrected; reasoned refusal is still resolution."*

### Flashcard ETH-4098 — Promise versus entitlement: moral commitment, legal enforceability and the contract metaphor
**Front:** Why must a charter promise not be treated as a contract or an automatic legal right?
**Definition (learn):** A charter is a public administrative commitment whose force can be strengthened by standards, monitoring and remedy. Legal enforceability depends on the applicable instrument.
**Breakdown:**
- Metaphor helps: shows public promise, legitimate expectations, comparability and visible responsibility.
- Metaphor misleads: many charters are administrative; no market choice; contractual remedy does not arise from the title "Charter"; eligibility remains governed by law; compensation or penalty requires competent authority; equality prevents selective bargaining.
- Where law fixes a higher entitlement, administrative incapacity cannot dilute it; capacity must be repaired.
- Legitimate expectation: state the ethical and administrative reliance interest; leave legal effect to the competent forum.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 advanced §2 and §4.
**Links:** ETH-4068, ETH-4062, ETH-4100.
**Trap:** "The Charter is a contract, so the citizen can sue for compensation" — not established by the title.
**Mains hook:** Argue the safer formulation: a public administrative commitment, enforceable only through the right instrument.
**Back:** *"A charter binds conscience and management; only statute binds the State to pay."*

### Flashcard ETH-4099 — Just culture: fair causal accountability
**Front:** Which response fits each cause in a just-culture model?
**Definition (learn):** A just culture distinguishes causes of failure and matches the response to cause and culpability.
**Breakdown:**
- Good-faith error in a complex system -> correct, learn, coach.
- Capability gap -> train and supervise.
- Unreasonable workload -> redesign and resource.
- Reckless disregard -> formal accountability.
- Deliberate falsification or retaliation -> evidence-based inquiry and proportionate sanction.
- A blame-free culture can excuse misconduct; a blame-heavy culture hides errors.
- Psychological safety: staff should be able to report impossible standards, unsafe shortcuts, inaccessible design, data errors, agent capture, pressure to close falsely and deadlock; safety is not immunity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 advanced §13 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4095, ETH-4075, ETH-4050.
**Trap:** Equating psychological safety with impunity.
**Mains hook:** Use the table to match response to cause; qualify with due process.
**Back:** *"Match the response to the cause — learn from error, sanction deliberate harm."*

### Flashcard ETH-4100 — Street-level discretion and administrative burden: structured discretion, not zero or heroic discretion
**Front:** What can frontline discretion do, what can it do wrongly, and what design bounds it?
**Definition (learn):** Frontline officials translate general rules into individual encounters. Discretion can cure ambiguity, coordinate missing information, recognise vulnerability, sequence urgent cases and explain routes; it can also create informal requirements, favour agents, humiliate, ration through delay or hide behind literalism.
**Breakdown:**
- Structured discretion: clear eligibility and plain guidance -> limited documented discretion -> reasons, supervision and review -> learning from recurring exceptions.
- Administrative burden costs: learning cost (discovering eligibility, documents and route); compliance cost (forms, travel, authentication, repeated evidence); psychological cost (uncertainty, stigma, fear, humiliation).
- A citizen-centric charter should reduce all three while preserving checks.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 advanced §6 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4098, ETH-4095, ETH-4037.
**Trap:** Believing either zero discretion or heroic discretion solves the problem.
**Mains hook:** Argue discretion bounded by purpose, equal treatment, records and review.
**Back:** *"Bounded discretion, documented reasons, reviewed decisions — no heroes, no robots."*

### Flashcard ETH-4101 — Owner 17 PYQ map: 2019 to 2026 routes
**Front:** Which PYQs anchor Topic 17, and which owner section answers each?
**Definition (learn):** Verified routes: 2019 (principles and importance of Citizens' Charter movement); 2021 (social feedback, public-service performance, accountability); 2022 Q1(a) (core outcome vs peripheral bureaucracy); 2022 Q1(b) (empathy and compassion); 2022 Q2(a) (purposive rules and positive administration); 2022 Q4(a) (e-governance and beneficiary outcomes); 2022 Q4(b) (vulnerable-group access in online systems); 2024 Q6(b) (Mission Karmayogi and grassroots service); 2025 Q4(a) (reason, critical thinking and ethical welfare delivery); 2025 Q5(a) (professionalism and fulfilment); 2025 Q5(b) (enabler and active facilitator); 2025 Q6(a) (value-based and compliance-based culture).
**Breakdown:**
- 2026 routes (owner-recorded): Q5(a) graded enforcement (ETH-4083); Q9 tout capture (ETH-4080); Q10 authentication failure (ETH-4088).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 §20 (⚠️ owner says verify 2026 printed question numbering).
**Links:** ETH-4055, ETH-4080, ETH-4083, ETH-4088.
**Trap:** Owners 16 and 17 describe the same 2025 Q5(a) with different emphasis (devotion to duty in owner 16; professionalism and fulfilment in owner 17). Treat it as one question, not two.
**Mains hook:** Route each demand to the owner section named in the owner's route table before writing.
**Back:** *"Map the year to the owner, then answer the verb."*

### Flashcard ETH-4102 — Corollary thesis: who bears the cost of the institution's own failure?
**Front:** State the common test that governs both regulation and technology in this owner.
**Definition (learn):** The same test applies at the two points where service turns coercive: when the State enforces a norm against a provider, and when the State verifies an entitlement through technology. Does the institution bear the cost of its own failure, or does the citizen?
**Breakdown:**
- Enforcement: sanction must escalate predictably and never be discharged upon the beneficiary; continuity is a precondition of closure.
- Verification: technology may confirm who the person is but never decide the right; every path ends in delivery or a written, appealable ineligibility finding.
- Final thesis: charters improve service only when specific standards join capable workflow, humane culture, correct grievance and legal routes, inclusive access, balanced measurement and visible learning. The objective is timely, accurate, fair and correctable service, not faster disposal.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 17 final thesis and corollary (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4083, ETH-4088, ETH-4090, ETH-4056.
**Trap:** Presenting regulation as "rules versus compassion".
**Mains hook:** Close every 20-mark answer with the cost-bearing test.
**Back:** *"Does the institution pay for its failure, or does the citizen?"*

---

### GROUP — Utilisation of Public Funds and Challenges of Corruption (owner 18)

### Flashcard ETH-4103 — Core theme: public money is a fiduciary trust tied to authorised purpose
**Front:** What is the ethical starting point for public money, and what five duties follow?
**Definition (learn):** Fiduciary stewardship: a public official does not own the money controlled by a department. Authority is received for a defined public purpose and must be exercised for intended beneficiaries under law, reason, fairness and review.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Fidelity to purpose: no substitution of personal, partisan or organisational convenience.
- 2. Prudence: avoid foreseeable waste and whole-life cost.
- 3. Impartiality: no preference for a connected person or group.
- 4. Accountability: preserve records, reasons and a responsible decision-maker.
- 5. Restoration: correct the loss or denied service, not merely punish an offender.
- Chain: legislative authorisation -> executive sanction -> implementation, procurement, delivery, payment -> verified output and outcome -> audit -> remedy -> trust.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §1 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4104, ETH-4105, ETH-4130, ETH-4134.
**Trap:** "No private gain means no ethical problem" is false: waste and partisan diversion harm trust without enrichment.
**Mains hook:** Define the fiduciary norm first, then locate where the chain breaks.
**Back:** *"Money is entrusted, not owned — five duties keep it honest."*

### Flashcard ETH-4104 — Allocation is not utilisation: the five-stage public-value chain
**Front:** Distinguish allocation, release, expenditure, output and outcome, and say what each does not prove.
**Definition (learn):** Utilisation runs through a chain; each stage proves something different.
**Breakdown:**
- Allocation: legislature/budget authorised an amount (does not prove funds available or needed now).
- Release: funds available to spend (does not prove procurement or delivery).
- Expenditure: recorded payment or liability (does not prove goods, works or services were genuine).
- Output: immediate deliverable (does not prove social objective improved).
- Outcome: intended condition changed (does not prove economy or equity).
- Exam line: expenditure is an accounting event; ethical utilisation is a public-value chain.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §1 (table).
**Links:** ETH-4103, ETH-4107, ETH-4111.
**Trap:** Reporting "fully spent" as success.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to locate the failure stage before blaming anyone.
**Back:** *"Allocated, released, spent, delivered, changed — each proves only its own step."*

### Flashcard ETH-4105 — Classify before assigning blame: seven categories of financial failure
**Front:** Define the seven categories of financial failure and their primary responses.
**Definition (learn):** Categories overlap, and classifying precisely comes before any sanction.
**Breakdown:**
- Error: inadvertent mistake (wrong budget code) -> correction and training.
- Irregularity: departure from applicable rule (missing approval) -> regularise if lawful; fix control.
- Waste/squandering: avoidable loss (ostentatious furnishing) -> value-for-money review.
- Negligence: failure of due care (no receipt verification) -> assess gravity and capacity.
- Fraud: intentional deception (fictitious invoice or beneficiary) -> preserve evidence; investigate.
- Abuse of discretion: entrusted choice used for irrelevant purpose (arbitrary vendor preference) -> reasons, conflict and review.
- Corruption: public power for improper connected advantage (kickback, partisan diversion) -> competent disciplinary or criminal route.
- Overlap example: a false measurement used to pay a connected contractor may be irregular, fraudulent, wasteful and corrupt at once. Not every irregularity is corruption.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §1 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4104, ETH-4106, ETH-4117, ETH-4118.
**Trap:** Calling every irregularity corruption.
**Mains hook:** Classify first, then choose the control; the classification sets the remedy.
**Back:** *"Error, irregular, waste, negligent, fraud, abused, corrupt — name it before you punish it."*

### Flashcard ETH-4106 — Squandering without bribery: the Second ARC's point
**Front:** Why does the Second ARC treat squandering of public money as harmful even without misappropriation to an individual?
**Definition (learn):** Squandering is avoidable public loss; the exchequer, public interest and trust can suffer without any personal cash gain.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: prestige construction unrelated to functional need; avoidable luxury travel or furnishing; equipment bought without staff, maintenance or demand; hurried year-end purchasing to exhaust allocation.
- Opportunity cost: every avoidable rupee can displace maintenance, welfare, safety or debt reduction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §1 and §5 (Second ARC, Fourth Report).
**Links:** ETH-4105, ETH-4119, ETH-4111.
**Trap:** Treating "no one got rich" as "no problem".
**Mains hook:** Use opportunity cost to argue for value-for-money review even without corruption.
**Back:** *"No pocket picked, but the public still paid for the prestige."*

### Flashcard ETH-4107 — Economy, efficiency, effectiveness and equity: the CAG four-E frame
**Front:** Define economy, efficiency, effectiveness and equity, with the trap each invites.
**Definition (learn):** CAG defines performance audit around economy, efficiency and effectiveness (value for money, accountability, transparency). Equity is the ethical fourth test: who benefits, who bears cost, who is excluded.
**Breakdown:**
- Economy: suitable inputs at lowest defensible whole-life cost (trap: equating cheapest price with economy).
- Efficiency: greatest suitable output from resources (trap: raising disposal by sacrificing quality or access).
- Effectiveness: intended objective or outcome achieved (trap: treating outputs as outcomes).
- Equity: fair distribution considering unequal need and access (trap: treating identical spending as equal justice).
- Worked example: a district that spends its full medical-device grant fails economy if maintenance is unaffordable, efficiency if devices sit idle, effectiveness if health does not improve, equity if remote facilities get no usable access.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §2 (CAG performance-audit formulation as cited by owner; ✅ owner advanced §7).
**Links:** ETH-4104, ETH-4108, ETH-4109, ETH-4122.
**Trap:** The four dimensions can conflict; name the trade-off, do not assert one universal metric.
**Mains hook:** Use the four tests as a checklist for any scheme evaluation.
**Back:** *"Cheap, productive, effective, and fair to the last district."*

### Flashcard ETH-4108 — Efficiency versus effectiveness: the productivity identity and the Drucker caution (2026 Q4(b) route)
**Front:** Explain the productivity identity and the attribution caution for Drucker's formulation.
**Definition (learn):** Productivity = output or outcome produced / input. Effectiveness sets the numerator (right thing at all); efficiency governs the denominator (cost per unit); equity constrains both (for whom, who is left out).
**Breakdown:**
- Efficient and effective: genuine productivity (camps sited by mapped need, low cost per verified beneficiary, outcome follow-up).
- Efficient, not effective: fast production of the wrong thing; the costliest failure because it looks like success (grievances closed without resolution).
- Effective, not efficient: right objective at unsustainable cost (pilot cannot scale).
- Neither: waste plus failure (assets built without need assessment and left unmaintained).
- Drucker: "Efficiency is concerned with doing things right. Effectiveness is doing the right things" (*Management: Tasks, Responsibilities, Practices*, 1974, as summarised in the owner); the owner notes the one-line form is a condensation. Write "as Drucker put it"; do not invent a page or edition.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §2 (Drucker attribution with provenance caution; ⚠️ the quotation is a condensation as the owner states).
**Links:** ETH-4107, ETH-4109, ETH-4110, ETH-4134.
**Trap:** Treating efficiency and effectiveness as opposites and choosing one. Quoting Drucker with an invented page.
**Mains hook:** Answer 2026 Q4(b) by sequencing, not selection: validate the objective, then minimise its cost, with equity as a constraint.
**Back:** *"Effectiveness picks the target, efficiency prices the trip, equity checks who was left behind."*

### Flashcard ETH-4109 — The sequencing rule and the balancing instruments for productivity
**Front:** State the sequencing rule and name at least two balancing instruments.
**Definition (learn):** Effectiveness first (validate objective and causal chain), efficiency second (minimise cost of that objective), equity as a standing constraint on both.
**Breakdown:**
- Optimising efficiency before validating the objective is the most expensive administrative error: it accelerates in the wrong direction.
- Instruments: outcome budgeting / Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework (outcome budgeting introduced at Union level from 2005-06; now an output-outcome framework with NITI Aayog/DMEO); theory of change or logframe (⚠️ standard tool); CAG performance audit; balanced dashboard with leading and lagging indicators and failure-demand tracking; periodic re-validation and sunset review; protection of good-faith judgment.
- 10-mark spine: define both -> productivity identity -> one Indian illustration -> failure mechanism (goal displacement, Goodhart's law) -> sequencing rule -> two instruments -> verdict: productivity rises only when the right objective is pursued at a defensible cost.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §2 (outcome budgeting from 2005-06 and OOMF per owner). ⚠️ theory-of-change and sunset review as standard tools.
**Links:** ETH-4108, ETH-4110, ETH-4094.
**Trap:** Measurement alone does not solve it; Goodhart's law acts on measurement, so use plural indicators and field verification.
**Mains hook:** Use the verdict line in any productivity answer.
**Back:** *"Validate the goal, price it, protect fairness — then measure with more than one number."*

### Flashcard ETH-4110 — Goal displacement, Goodhart's law and Campbell's law: the named failure mechanisms
**Front:** Name the three mechanisms that explain how measures and means defeat purpose.
**Definition (learn):** Goal displacement (Merton): the means becomes the end. Goodhart's law: when a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure. Campbell's law: the more a quantitative indicator is used for social decision-making, the more it is subject to corruption pressure and distortion.
**Breakdown:**
- Goal displacement: "the file is complete but the citizen is not served."
- Target-chasing: disposal raised by closing without resolving; enrolment without attendance; beds installed instead of patients treated.
- Failure demand (ETH-4093): repeat contacts counted as productive workload rewards the defect.
- False economy: cheapest procurement generating unaffordable maintenance.
- Advanced examples: roads counted by length despite poor durability; toilets counted without water.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §2 and §10 (names as used by owner). ⚠️ Merton, Goodhart and Campbell attributions are standard knowledge not fetched in this build.
**Links:** ETH-4109, ETH-4093, ETH-4094, ETH-4097.
**Trap:** Blaming staff alone; the law explains the pattern, but causes vary.
**Mains hook:** Name the mechanism precisely (goal displacement or Goodhart) rather than saying "gaming".
**Back:** *"The means ate the end, the target spoiled the measure, the number corrupted the process."*

### Flashcard ETH-4111 — Under-utilisation and mis-utilisation: causes, and why "spend faster" is the wrong cure
**Front:** List the common causes of under-utilisation and of mis-utilisation, and say what not to recommend.
**Definition (learn):** Under-utilisation is idle or unspent allocation; mis-utilisation is spending that departs from purpose or integrity.
**Breakdown:**
- Under-utilisation: unrealistic or inflated budgeting; late release or short season; weak project design, land readiness or approvals; shortage of procurement, engineering or financial capacity; fragmented authority; litigation or changed need; fear of audit causing paralysis; unresolved grievances.
- Mis-utilisation: expenditure outside authorised purpose; procurement tailored to a connected vendor; false beneficiaries, attendance or completion; collusion, kickback or inflated variation; politically timed diversion; weak receipt and utilisation verification; rush-of-expenditure behaviour.
- Do not recommend "spend faster" for every low ratio; diagnose planning, authority, capacity, legality or changed need first.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §2 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4104, ETH-4119, ETH-4130.
**Trap:** Year-end spending to exhaust allocation is itself squandering.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose the bottleneck before prescribing acceleration.
**Back:** *"Idle money needs a diagnosis, not a deadline."*

### Flashcard ETH-4112 — General Financial Rules 2017 and GFR Rule 157 (Avoiding Piecemeal Purchases)
**Front:** What is the GFR's status, and what does Rule 157 prohibit?
**Definition (learn):** The General Financial Rules, 2017 (updated up to 31 January 2026, per the Department of Expenditure page cited by owner) form the Union framework. Rule 157 "Avoiding Piecemeal Purchases" states that a demand for goods should not be divided into small quantities to avoid the higher sanction required for the total estimated value.
**Breakdown:**
- States, public-sector undertakings and autonomous bodies may have their own rules.
- Rule 157 covers goods; splitting works or services to evade sanction is the same propriety failure, but cite the applicable works/services framework.
- Public-propriety tests: authorised purpose; necessary and proportionate; prudent use; no direct or indirect benefit to decision-maker or connected person; avoidable extravagance prevented; approval of total genuine requirement; auditor can reconstruct need, alternatives, sanction, receipt and payment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §3 and advanced §3 (📰 GFR updated to 31 January 2026, per owner's official-source citation; ⚠️ current consolidated text not fetched in this build).
**Links:** ETH-4114, ETH-4113, ETH-4115.
**Trap:** Presenting Rule 157's goods wording as a verbatim rule for all procurement.
**Mains hook:** Use Rule 157 precision: anti-splitting is about the total genuine requirement.
**Back:** *"Do not chop one need into small bills to dodge the bigger signature."*

### Flashcard ETH-4113 — Segregation of duties and reconciliation: who must not do what
**Front:** Which functions must be separated in public procurement and payment, and what compensating controls work in small offices?
**Definition (learn):** Separation prevents one person from creating demand, choosing vendors, evaluating bids, certifying receipt, authorising payment and reconciling records.
**Breakdown:**
- Identify need or specification -> prevents buyer inventing demand for a chosen seller.
- Create or approve vendor -> reduces fictitious or connected vendors.
- Evaluate bid -> protects fair competition.
- Certify receipt or measurement -> tests actual delivery.
- Authorise payment -> prevents self-certification.
- Reconcile bank, ledger and asset -> exposes duplicates, missing or diverted entries.
- Small offices: supervisory review, rotation, sample verification, direct confirmation, immutable logs and post-payment reconciliation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §3 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4112, ETH-4115, ETH-4127.
**Trap:** Assuming segregation is impossible in small offices and doing nothing.
**Mains hook:** Argue for proportionate compensating controls.
**Back:** *"Who needs it, who picks it, who checks it, who pays, who reconciles — never the same hand."*

### Flashcard ETH-4114 — Delegated financial power and re-appropriation: the total-requirement rule
**Front:** Why can an officer not fragment a genuine requirement, and what questions review re-appropriation?
**Definition (learn):** Delegation allocates responsibility, not an inconvenience threshold. An officer cannot create competence by renaming, fragmenting or shifting one genuine requirement.
**Breakdown:**
- Chain: total genuine requirement -> applicable category and value -> competent authority -> procurement method, review and record.
- Re-appropriation (budget transfer within the framework) review questions: who loses or waits; is the original purpose still urgent; less harmful financing; is it transparent to the legislature; contractual and opportunity costs; is an election or connected interest driving it?
- Formal legality is a floor, not a complete distributive justification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §3 and advanced §3 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4112, ETH-4113, ETH-4119.
**Trap:** Treating a transfer as proper merely because it is legal.
**Mains hook:** Use the total-requirement test to attack fragmentation and the re-appropriation questions to attack distributive injustice.
**Back:** *"One need, one authority; a transfer is legal, but is it fair?"*

### Flashcard ETH-4115 — Procurement lifecycle risk-and-control matrix
**Front:** Walk through the procurement lifecycle and name the integrity control at each stage.
**Definition (learn):** Procurement corruption is not confined to opening bids; a fair award can be reversed by false measurement, inferior supply, unjustified extension or inflated variation.
**Breakdown:**
- Lifecycle: need -> estimate -> specification -> tender -> evaluation -> award -> delivery -> variation -> payment -> use/warranty -> disposal.
- Need: invented or oversized requirement -> demand plan, affordability, outcome case.
- Estimate: inflated benchmark -> independent estimate.
- Specification: brand or vendor tailoring -> functional, neutral, reviewed specification.
- Tender: artificial urgency or restricted access -> proportionate publicity, adequate time.
- Evaluation: changed criteria, leakage, conflict -> criteria fixed beforehand; declarations; committee reasons.
- Award: arbitrary negotiation -> speaking record and challenge route.
- Delivery: inferior quantity or quality -> independent inspection and measurement.
- Variation: value expanded after competition -> predefined authority and reasoned change control.
- Payment: duplicate or false payment -> three-way match and milestone verification.
- Use: idle asset -> utilisation and maintenance review. Disposal: connected undervaluation -> transparent valuation.
- Conflict of interest: a procurement chair whose sibling controls a bidder should not cast the deciding vote; disclosure alone does not cure a material conflict. Conflict is a risk condition, not proof.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §4 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4039, ETH-4040, ETH-4113, ETH-4116.
**Trap:** Auditing only the tender stage.
**Mains hook:** Show corruption can enter at any stage and map a control to each.
**Back:** *"Need to disposal, every stage a door, every door a lock."*

### Flashcard ETH-4116 — Collusion and cartel red flags, and the lowest-price trap
**Front:** Name the cartel red flags and explain why the lowest price is not automatically best value.
**Definition (learn):** Red flags justify review, not automatic guilt.
**Breakdown:**
- Red flags: regular bid rotation among the same firms; predictable losing "cover bids"; identical unusual wording, errors, metadata or addresses; coordinated withdrawals or territorial allocation; common beneficial ownership or connected directors; repeated single bids after restrictive conditions; concentration of awards; subcontracting back to an apparent competitor.
- Lowest price trap: value for money includes fitness, quality, reliability, delivery, maintenance, disposal and competition; "quality" cannot become a slogan permitting arbitrary preference.
- Advanced: several bids do not prove independent rivalry; bid above estimate is not automatically corrupt; bid below estimate is not automatically sustainable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §4 and advanced §4.
**Links:** ETH-4115, ETH-4120, ETH-4122.
**Trap:** Treating a red flag as proof of cartel.
**Mains hook:** Use market analysis, ownership checks and fair process before any finding.
**Back:** *"Many bids is not many rivals; the cheapest is not always the best."*

### Flashcard ETH-4117 — The leakage map: five routes from allocation to loss
**Front:** Map the five leakage routes from an allocated fund.
**Definition (learn):** Leakage routes: idle balance or delay (under-utilisation); unauthorised head or partisan redirection (diversion); duplicate, ghost or false measurement (fraud); inflated input or idle asset (waste); bribe, favour or connected vendor (corruption).
**Breakdown:**
- Each route has a different remedy: utilisation review, authority check, reconciliation and verification, cost control, and investigation with fair process.
- Map the failure to the route before naming a control.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §5 (leakage map).
**Links:** ETH-4105, ETH-4111, ETH-4118, ETH-4125.
**Trap:** Calling every leak corruption.
**Mains hook:** Use the map to show the leak is not one thing.
**Back:** *"Idle, diverted, faked, wasted, bought — five leaks, five fixes."*

### Flashcard ETH-4118 — Coercive versus collusive bribery
**Front:** Distinguish coercive from collusive bribery and state the response emphasis for each.
**Definition (learn):** Coercive bribery: an official extorts a person seeking lawful service. Collusive bribery: giver and taker cooperate for improper advantage.
**Breakdown:**
- Coercive: example, payment demanded for a due certificate; immediate victim is the service-seeker; response: protect the complainant, deliver the service, preserve evidence.
- Collusive: example, contractor pays for false quality certification; victim is the State, competitors, users and public; response: investigate both sides and the transaction.
- Analytical distinction used by the Second ARC; Topic 19 owns the exact PC Act treatment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §5 (⚠️ Topic 19 statutory text not in this card).
**Links:** ETH-4105, ETH-4117, ETH-4119.
**Trap:** Protecting only the victim in collusive cases, or prosecuting only the giver in coercive cases.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to choose evidence and remedy.
**Back:** *"Extortion hits the seeker; collusion hits everybody else."*

### Flashcard ETH-4119 — Abuse by omission and squandering: corruption without a bribe
**Front:** How can wilful non-performance produce a corrupt advantage, and what test separates it from capacity failure?
**Definition (learn):** Wilful non-performance can yield corrupt advantage: deliberately delaying entitlement to extract payment; ignoring known overbilling by a connected contractor; failing to enforce penalties after political instruction; keeping inspection dormant so illegal activity continues.
**Breakdown:**
- Test: clear duty, knowledge, capacity, wilfulness or gross negligence, foreseeable harm, improper advantage, concealed reasons.
- Protect good-faith judgment; distinguish capacity failure from corrupt omission.
- Squandering: avoidable expenditure can be serious without bribery (ETH-4106).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §5 and 2019 Q2(b) route (owner 18 §10).
**Links:** ETH-4106, ETH-4114, ETH-4118, ETH-4129.
**Trap:** Every poor outcome is not corruption.
**Mains hook:** Argue: wilful omission can be corrupt; honest error should not be criminalised.
**Back:** *"Doing nothing on purpose can be a transaction too."*

### Flashcard ETH-4120 — Escalation of corruption: transaction, network and state capture (2022 mining route)
**Front:** Describe the three analytical levels of corruption and what state capture changes.
**Definition (learn):** Petty or transactional: one official, one service, one bribe. Networked: broker, official, contractor and political protection. Systemic or equilibrium: rules, appointments, enforcement and expectations sustain corruption.
**Breakdown:**
- State capture: powerful interests influence legislation and rules, appointments and transfers, budget priorities and eligibility, enforcement and prosecution, media or information channels, complaint and audit institutions.
- 2022 GS-IV stone-mining case: a neutral illustration of a nexus of illegal business, officials, politics and media ownership.
- Individual punishment fails if the network still controls appointments, permits, data and complaints.
- Equilibrium: honest actors face retaliation, unreliable protection, lost promotion, collective silence and vendor boycott.
- Reform must change expectations: visible protection for refusal, reliable service without bribe, certainty of detection, disruption of brokers, leadership accountability for retaliatory climate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §6 and advanced §5–6 (2022 route per owner).
**Links:** ETH-4118, ETH-4121, ETH-4126, ETH-4128.
**Trap:** Avoid conspiracy by assertion; network analysis needs corroborated relationships and decisions.
**Mains hook:** Write state-capture features, not merely one bribe.
**Back:** *"One bribe, one network, one state — match the remedy to the level."*

### Flashcard ETH-4121 — Regulatory capture: six levels and the test that distinguishes lobbying from capture
**Front:** Name the six levels of capture and the signs that separate capture from lawful lobbying.
**Definition (learn):** Capture occurs when a regulator persistently serves the regulated interest instead of statutory public purpose. No envelope of cash is required.
**Breakdown:**
- Cognitive capture (regulator adopts industry's worldview); access capture (dominant actors monopolise consultation); information capture (regulator cannot test industry data); personnel capture (revolving doors); political capture (enforcement responds to partisan protection); state capture (laws and institutions reshaped).
- Lobbying is permitted; capture is indicated when access is systematically unequal, interests or data hidden, contrary evidence excluded, reasons depart from statutory purpose, appointments or enforcement connected, and the pattern persists.
- Industry consultation is not automatically capture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §5 and advanced §6.
**Links:** ETH-4120, ETH-4043, ETH-4126.
**Trap:** "Industry consultation proves capture" is false.
**Mains hook:** Use the six levels to diagnose; use the persistence test to qualify.
**Back:** *"Six levels from worldview to statute — lobbying is allowed, persistent displacement is not."*

### Flashcard ETH-4122 — The audit ecosystem: CAG performance audit and the complementary oversight table
**Front:** What role does each oversight mechanism play, and what can it not do alone?
**Definition (learn):** CAG audit is independent external scrutiny of public finances and performance; its performance audit asks whether programmes operate with economy, efficiency and effectiveness and whether citizens received value for money.
**Breakdown:**
- Management control: prevents and corrects during implementation; cannot supply independent external assurance.
- Internal audit: tests controls for management; cannot replace constitutional external audit.
- CAG audit: cannot itself impose every disciplinary or criminal sanction.
- Legislative committee: examines audit findings and executive explanation; cannot conduct every field verification.
- Social audit: compares records with lived delivery; cannot replace professional audit or courts.
- Citizen complaint and RTI: supply local evidence; cannot establish guilt without fair process.
- Financial, compliance, performance, forensic and social audits ask different questions (advanced §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §7 and advanced §7.
**Links:** ETH-4107, ETH-4123, ETH-4124, ETH-4125.
**Trap:** A clean financial audit does not show outcome effectiveness.
**Mains hook:** Argue that complementary oversight, not one super-agency, makes accountability work.
**Back:** *"Each auditor sees a different question — none sees everything."*

### Flashcard ETH-4123 — Audit-to-remedy chain: an audit paragraph is not a conviction
**Front:** Give the audit-to-remedy chain and explain why an audit finding is not guilt.
**Definition (learn):** Chain: audit evidence -> entity response -> CAG report or competent finding -> legislative or management scrutiny -> correction, recovery or referral -> action-taken follow-up -> system learning.
**Breakdown:**
- An audit paragraph can identify loss, weak control or suspicious pattern and trigger competent investigation or disciplinary review.
- The affected person must receive the process required by the applicable forum.
- Advanced: audit can assess criteria only with information available at the time; hindsight is a trap. A fair audit still holds officials responsible for ignored evidence, conflicts, concealment or reckless assumptions.
- Do not collapse audit, legislature, executive and recovery into one punitive body.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §7 and advanced §7.
**Links:** ETH-4122, ETH-4124, ETH-4129.
**Trap:** Writing "audit proved fraud".
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to show accountability has stages and due process at each.
**Back:** *"Audit finds, legislature asks, executive acts, forum decides."*

### Flashcard ETH-4124 — Public Accounts Committee: composition, chair convention and function
**Front:** Describe the PAC's composition, chair convention and core function.
**Definition (learn):** The PAC examines the Comptroller and Auditor General's audit report once laid before Parliament and checks whether money granted was spent within the scope of the demand.
**Breakdown:**
- Composition: not more than 22 members; 15 elected by Lok Sabha and not more than 7 by Rajya Sabha; proportional representation by single transferable vote; one-year term; no minister may sit on it (✅ Wikipedia).
- Chair appointed by the Lok Sabha Speaker; 1950–1967 chair from ruling party; since 1967 convention is an opposition member, usually the Leader of Opposition (✅ Wikipedia).
- The C&AG assists the committee during investigation (✅ Wikipedia).
- Origin: first conceived in 1921 after the Montagu-Chelmsford reforms (✅ Wikipedia); post-independence body from 1950 per infobox.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia: Public Accounts Committee (India). Chair: K.C. Venugopal (INC) since August 2024 as stated on the page (📰 as at the fetch date; check currency before quoting).
**Links:** ETH-4122, ETH-4123, ETH-4125.
**Trap:** Saying the C&AG is the committee's "guide, friend and philosopher" — the fetched page does not say this; treat as unverified (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Argue the opposition chair convention builds neutrality in oversight; qualify that the committee recommends and does not punish alone.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two members, a fifteen-seven split, an opposition chair — the audit's parliamentary bridge."*

### Flashcard ETH-4125 — Social audit: what citizens can verify and its limits
**Front:** What can social audit verify, and what safeguards does it require?
**Definition (learn):** Social audit compares official records with lived delivery through citizen verification.
**Breakdown:**
- Verifiable items: who was eligible and selected; attendance or service receipt; sanctioned work and physical existence; measured quantity and payment; exclusion, delay or intimidation; action taken on findings.
- Requirements: accessible records, independent facilitation, local-language explanation, public hearing, safety, time-bound response.
- Distortions: local power, retaliation, inaccessible records, partisan mobilisation, privacy breaches. Safeguards: independent facilitators, random verification, protected testimony, reasoned findings, appeal, follow-up by competent authority.
- Privacy-sensitive data must not be disclosed merely because a public hearing occurs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §7 and advanced §7. 2021 PYQ route per owner §10.
**Links:** ETH-4122, ETH-4124, ETH-4094.
**Trap:** Social audit does not replace professional audit, investigation or courts.
**Mains hook:** Use social audit as the bridge between records and lived experience.
**Back:** *"Citizens check the record against the road — and protect the privacy of those who speak."*

### Flashcard ETH-4126 — Five-stage anti-corruption system: prevent, detect, investigate, adjudicate, sanction
**Front:** Name the five stages and the separate institutions that own them.
**Definition (learn):** Anti-corruption design separates prevention, detection, investigation, adjudication and sanction, then reconnects them through remedy and system redesign.
**Breakdown:**
- Prevention: simplify and publish rules; identify high-value discretion; role separation; manage conflicts; rotate sensitive posts where risk justifies; safe advice.
- Detection: reconciliation; internal audit; complaints and whistleblowing; duplicate and concentration analytics; field and social audit; variation review.
- Investigation: secure records; trace decision and money flows; obtain expertise; distinguish error, negligence, fraud and corrupt motive; protect confidentiality; corroborate.
- Adjudication: competent forum; notice, access to material, impartiality, proof, reasoned decision, review.
- Sanction: record correction; recovery; disciplinary; debarment; prosecution; institutional responsibility.
- Boundaries: Topic 19 (corruption statutes), Topic 20 (CVC, CBI, Lokpal), Topic 21 (vigilance and protection of honest officials), Topic 22 (case architecture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §8 (⚠️ analysis; institution jurisdictions not restated here).
**Links:** ETH-4120, ETH-4123, ETH-4129, ETH-4130.
**Trap:** Punishing a junior operator while leaving a captured approval chain intact is not systemic accountability.
**Mains hook:** Use the five stages to show that one agency cannot do all five.
**Back:** *"Prevent, detect, investigate, adjudicate, sanction — five stages, five owners, one remedy."*

### Flashcard ETH-4127 — PFMS/GIFMIS and the e-bill (3 March 2022): what technology can and cannot prove
**Front:** When was the e-bill system launched, and what does a digital "PAID" record not prove?
**Definition (learn):** The PFMS/GIFMIS description states that centralised treasury payments use electronic instruments for vendors, employees, States and implementing agencies. The e-bill system, launched on 3 March 2022, permits trackable online claims, reduced payment cycles and audit trails.
**Breakdown:**
- Technology can improve: timestamped trails; reduced cash handling; role-based access and maker-checker approval; duplicate beneficiary or bank-account alerts; split-order and vendor-concentration analysis; reconciliation; safe scheme-level progress publication.
- Technology cannot prove: beneficiary genuineness; valid consent or identity; asset or work existence; quantity and quality; bias in specification; collusion; intended outcome.
- Risks: garbage data, ghost beneficiaries, shared credentials, collusive vendors, algorithmic exclusion, privacy harm, cyber manipulation, dashboard gaming.
- Balanced design: legal authority; minimum data; accurate master data; least-privilege and immutable logs; human review of adverse flags; field verification; accessible correction; independent audit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §9 (📰 official PFMS/GIFMIS description as cited by owner); e-bill launch date 3 March 2022 per owner.
**Links:** ETH-4113, ETH-4126, ETH-4087, ETH-4088.
**Trap:** Treating "PAID" as proof of delivery.
**Mains hook:** Argue digital traceability improves but does not replace field verification.
**Back:** *"The system says paid; the field must say delivered."*

### Flashcard ETH-4128 — Whistleblowing: responsible steps and the conditions for external disclosure
**Front:** Give the responsible whistleblowing sequence and the conditions under which external disclosure is ethically stronger.
**Definition (learn):** Observe -> verify -> preserve evidence -> use safe authorised channel -> minimise unrelated disclosure -> protect source and citizen -> escalate if captured -> receive feedback and protection.
**Breakdown:**
- Trustworthy system: confidential and accessible reporting; protection against retaliation; independent triage; acknowledgement and feedback; sanctions for malicious retaliation; protection of the accused from publicity on vague allegations; escalation when the ordinary chain is implicated.
- External disclosure stronger when: wrongdoing is grave and well evidenced; internal or independent channels are captured or unsafe; delay would cause serious harm; disclosure is necessary and narrowly tailored; sources and citizens are protected.
- Weaker where evidence is unverified, ordinary remedies work or indiscriminate release causes avoidable harm.
- Not a licence to publish an entire beneficiary or commercial database.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §10 and advanced §8 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4038, ETH-4120, ETH-4129.
**Trap:** Whistleblowing as a licence for indiscriminate leakage.
**Mains hook:** Use the external-disclosure conditions to qualify whistleblower protection.
**Back:** *"Verify, preserve, report safely — leak only what the public needs."*

### Flashcard ETH-4129 — The bona fide decision test: protecting honest officials while investigating corruption
**Front:** What seven questions test whether a decision was bona fide?
**Definition (learn):** Anti-corruption systems fail if officials equate every commercial loss or unsuccessful policy with misconduct. The bona fide test asks whether authority was established, relevant facts and alternatives considered, conflicts disclosed, reasons recorded contemporaneously, personal or connected advantage absent, decision proportionate to urgency and information, and evidence not concealed, fabricated or ignored.
**Breakdown:**
- Seven questions as listed; the test protects reasoned good faith.
- Topic 21 owns exact legal safeguards and sanction rules.
- Complaint triage (advanced §8): specific? within jurisdiction? verifiable? urgent or retaliation risk? preliminary check? close, refer or investigate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 advanced §8 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4126, ETH-4128, ETH-4119, ETH-4131.
**Trap:** Using "good faith" to excuse concealment.
**Mains hook:** Argue that protecting reasoned good faith makes honest decisions possible; qualify with investigation of specific evidence.
**Back:** *"Authority, facts, conflicts, reasons, no gain, proportion, no concealment — then you are protected."*

### Flashcard ETH-4130 — TRUST TO REMEDY: the eight-step answer framework and the final thesis
**Front:** Give the TRUST TO REMEDY steps and the final thesis for public-fund questions.
**Definition (learn):** Define the precise failure; state the fiduciary thesis; locate the break in need, allocation, release, sanction, procurement, delivery, payment, access or outcome; evaluate with 3E, equity, legality and trust; name the control and institution; separate the five stages; add one India-centric illustration and one limitation; conclude with timely delivery, protected judgment, fair consequence and learning.
**Breakdown:**
- Final thesis: ethical utilisation turns authorised money into lawful, economical, efficient, effective and equitable public value.
- Corruption control is a chain of competent authority, contestable decisions, independent verification, protected voice, fair adjudication, proportionate consequence and restoration, not one heroic agency or digital portal.
- Advanced: name the appropriate forum without inventing jurisdiction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §10 and advanced §10.
**Links:** ETH-4103, ETH-4109, ETH-4126, ETH-4134.
**Trap:** Stopping at "define" without locating the break.
**Mains hook:** Use the framework as a template for all 20-mark utilisation answers.
**Back:** *"Define, thesis, locate, evaluate, name, separate, illustrate, remedy."*

### Flashcard ETH-4131 — Structured discretion and control accumulation: why more controls can create new rent points
**Front:** Why does "more controls always reduce corruption" fail, and what is structured discretion?
**Definition (learn):** After each scandal, institutions may add approvals, certificates or portals; excessive layers diffuse responsibility, create delay and rent points, encourage copy-paste certification, deter honest innovation and privilege firms able to manage complexity.
**Breakdown:**
- Structured discretion: clear purpose + decision factors + conflict rule + reasons + record + review + exception sunset.
- Risk-based controls: high-value, novel, concentrated or conflict-prone decisions get more scrutiny; routine transactions get simpler controls plus sampling.
- Excessive discretion gives arbitrary selection and hidden bargaining; excessive rigidity gives mechanical delay and box-ticking.
- Technology relocates discretion into data, specification, access, code, exception and override; it does not remove it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 advanced §3 and §10 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4129, ETH-4130, ETH-4127, ETH-4100.
**Trap:** Assuming technology removes discretion.
**Mains hook:** Argue for risk-based controls; qualify that discretion must be bounded.
**Back:** *"More gates, more gatekeepers — structure the discretion, do not multiply it."*

### Flashcard ETH-4132 — Emergency procurement: an exception route, not abandonment of stewardship
**Front:** What seven conditions make an emergency procurement defensible?
**Definition (learn):** Urgency can justify an exception route; it does not justify abandoning stewardship. Retrospective paperwork that invents reasons is not regularisation.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Defined trigger and authorised decision-maker. 2. Minimum necessary departure. 3. Market comparison where feasible. 4. Contemporaneous reason and conflict declaration. 5. Delivery and quality verification. 6. Rapid post-event review. 7. Expiry of emergency arrangements.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 advanced §3 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4114, ETH-4115, ETH-4131.
**Trap:** Using "emergency" to avoid competition as a habit.
**Mains hook:** Use the seven conditions to show emergency powers must be narrow and recorded.
**Back:** *"Emergency licenses speed, not silence."*

### Flashcard ETH-4133 — Reform matrix and the sequencing of reform after a failure
**Front:** Match failures to their first responses, and state the eight-step reform sequence.
**Definition (learn):** Reform should match the failure; sequencing protects entitlement first.
**Breakdown:**
- Honest error -> correction, guidance, control redesign (repeated ignored errors may become negligence). Irregularity -> restore authority and procedure. Waste -> whole-life and opportunity-cost review. Fraud -> evidence preservation and investigation. Coercive bribery -> victim protection and service restoration. Collusive bribery -> trace both parties. Cartel -> market and ownership analysis. Capture -> plural institutions. Under-utilisation -> readiness and capacity. Digital anomaly -> independent data and field verification.
- Sequence: protect continuity of genuine entitlement; contain loss and preserve evidence; correct records and pay lawful dues; investigate through competent process; impose proportionate consequence; repair control and incentive; publish safe learning; evaluate whether reform itself creates delay, exclusion or fear.
- Advanced: reform matrix qualifications (for example, avoid labelling all consultation corrupt; avoid reckless year-end spending).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §10 and advanced §10 (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** ETH-4111, ETH-4121, ETH-4130, ETH-4131.
**Trap:** Reform that creates new delay or fear is not reform.
**Mains hook:** Use the eight-step sequence to show reform order matters.
**Back:** *"Keep the entitlement alive first, fix the books, fix the system last."*

### Flashcard ETH-4134 — Owner 18 PYQ routes: 2019 to 2026 Q4(b)
**Front:** Which PYQs anchor Topic 18, and what does each demand?
**Definition (learn):** 2019 Q2(a): define under- and mis-utilisation; causes by stage; fiscal, developmental, distributive and trust implications; specific controls. 2019 Q2(b): agree conditionally; wilful omission can create corrupt advantage; protect honest error and capacity-constrained officials. 2020 Rajesh Kumar case: legal competence, welfare and development trade-off, alternatives, recorded advice, resignation only after remedies fail. 2021 social audit: usable records, citizen verification, hearing, action taken. 2022 mining nexus: identify state-capture features. 2023 coercion: explicit threat vs domination through dependency. 2025 Q6(b): accountability from allocation to outcome; technology plus independent verification. 2025 procurement case: consolidate genuine requirement; Rule 157 precision; higher sanction; neutral tender; written escalation. 2025 MGNREGA case: continue genuine entitlement while securing evidence, reconciling records, paying dues and repairing controls. 2026 Q4(b): productivity (see ETH-4108–4109).
**Breakdown:**
- Mapping to cards: 2019 Q2(a) -> ETH-4104, 4111; 2019 Q2(b) -> ETH-4119; 2021 -> ETH-4125; 2022 -> ETH-4120; 2023 -> ETH-4118; 2025 procurement -> ETH-4112, 4114; 2025 MGNREGA -> ETH-4130.
- Full 2026 Section-A routing ledger is cited by owner as `_PYQ-GS4-SectionA-2026.md` (not reviewed in this build).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 18 §10 (route list as recorded by owner; ⚠️ verify printed question text against official papers).
**Links:** ETH-4108, ETH-4109, ETH-4118, ETH-4125, ETH-4130.
**Trap:** Merging the 2025 procurement case and the 2025 MGNREGA case into one answer.
**Mains hook:** Route each PYQ to its named framework before writing.
**Back:** *"Each year, one verb: define, locate, reform — and never confuse the cases."*

## ETHICS — Part 5: Anti-Corruption Law and Institutions, Whistleblower Protection and Case-Study Method

> Section note: Owners: `upsc-ai-kit\knowledge\Ethics\basic\` and `advanced\`, files 19–22.
> Source tags: `(✅ owner NN)` = owner-only; `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = confirmed on a fetched page; `⚠️` = unverified or conflict, re-check before relying.
> Verification run 2026-11-01: Wikipedia PC Act 1988 and Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act 2013 pages were fetched for the dates and section points they cover.

### GROUP — 19 Corruption Legal Framework

### Flashcard ETH-5001 — Corruption legal framework: the core theme
**Front:** What does India's anti-corruption legal framework consist of, and what is its core analytical distinction?
**Definition (learn):** A set of statutes that create specific bribery and criminal-misconduct offences, plus asset-recovery and whistleblower layers, read alongside the 2nd ARC's split between coercive and collusive bribery. (✅ owner 19)
**Breakdown:**
- Criminal layer: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 (PC Act), as amended in 2018.
- Asset-recovery layer: Benami Transactions (Prohibition) Act, 1988, and its 2016 amendment.
- Informant-safety layer: Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014 (not in force) plus the PIDPI Resolution, 2004.
- Four parallel tracks on one act: vigilance screening, departmental proceedings, criminal prosecution, civil recovery or confiscation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2nd ARC, 4th Report *Ethics in Governance* (2007), Ch.3 paras 3.1–3.7 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5002, ETH-5003, ETH-5025, ETH-5028 (whistleblower layer), ETH-5033 (2018 trade-off).
**Trap:** The PC Act does not itself use the terms "coercive" or "collusive"; they are the ARC's analytical categories (ETH-5004).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate India's anti-corruption law as a set of complementary layers, stating which layer is weakest today (whistleblower protection). Qualified conclusion: the layers are complementary, but the whistleblower layer is still not operative.
**Back:** *"Bribe, benami, whistle: three layers, one frame, one gap."*

### Flashcard ETH-5002 — Coercive bribery
**Front:** Define coercive bribery in the ARC's sense.
**Definition (learn):** The citizen pays a public servant for a service to which they are already entitled, to avoid delay, harassment or loss. The citizen is an unwilling victim of extortion. (✅ owner 19, ARC 1.13 and 3.2.2.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Victim: the citizen (bribe-giver).
- Beneficiary: the public servant who extracts the payment.
- Typical instance: "speed money" for a birth certificate the applicant is entitled to (⚠️ owner illustration).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conviction rates were higher for coercive than collusive bribery under the pre-2018 law (ARC 3.2.2.3; ✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5003, ETH-5004, ETH-5035 (s.8 coercion defence).
**Trap:** Coercive bribery is not "every bribe the victim dislikes". The payment must be for something the citizen was already entitled to.
**Mains hook:** Classify the transaction before recommending a response. Protect the coerced complainant and use the reporting route. Qualified point: the classification changes the remedy.
**Back:** *"Entitled, extorted, unwilling: the citizen is the victim."*

### Flashcard ETH-5003 — Collusive bribery
**Front:** Define collusive bribery and state why it is harder to prosecute.
**Definition (learn):** Bribe-giver and bribe-taker both benefit from the transaction, and the state or public bears the loss. Examples: substandard-works certification, tax evasion, procurement kickbacks. (✅ owner 19, ARC 1.13 and 3.2.2.6)
**Breakdown:**
- No unwilling victim, so no willing complainant. Detection is harder.
- The ARC recommended a distinct offence with a presumption of guilt where state or public loss is shown (ARC 3.2.2.6–3.2.2.7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC punishment figures differ inside the report: the body of 3.2.2.6 says "10 years"; the formal recommendation 3.2.2.7(c) says "double that of other cases of bribery" (⚠️ owner conflict, see ETH-5033).
**Links:** ETH-5002, ETH-5004, ETH-5032.
**Trap:** Collusive bribery is not less serious because both parties "agreed". It is a loss to the state.
**Mains hook:** Argue for or against a separate, harsher collusive-bribery offence, citing the ARC. Qualified conclusion: the distinct offence helps detection only if proof rules change too.
**Back:** *"Both smile, the public pays."*

### Flashcard ETH-5004 — Coercive vs collusive: the close-option trap
**Front:** Which of these is true: "The PC Act explicitly distinguishes coercive and collusive bribery"?
**Definition (learn):** False. The ARC's own text (3.2.2.2) says the Act "does not differentiate" between the two. The distinction is an analytical framework from the ARC (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Statute: one bribery offence structure (ss.7, 8, 9).
- ARC framework: coercive versus collusive, used to design responses.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 1.13, 3.2.2.1–3.2.2.7 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5002, ETH-5003, ETH-5001.
**Trap:** Exam option "The Act specifically defines coercive bribery" is wrong. Answer by saying the category is the ARC's.
**Mains hook:** Use the framework by name and cite ARC paragraphs; do not present it as statutory language.
**Back:** *"The Act does not sort the bribes; the ARC does."*

### Flashcard ETH-5005 — Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988: basic architecture
**Front:** What did the PC Act, 1988 do, and when did it commence?
**Definition (learn):** It consolidated the Prevention of Corruption Act, 1947 and the Criminal Law (Amendment) Act, 1952. It broadened "public servant", created bribery and criminal-misconduct offences, and provided for trial by Special Judges (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Trial court: Special Judge (s.4).
- Offences: bribery and criminal misconduct.
- Commencement: 9 September 1988 (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Evolution: IPC ss.161–165 → PC Act 1947 → Criminal Law (Amendment) Act 1952 → 1964 amendments on the Santhanam Committee → PC Act 1988 → PC (Amendment) Act 2018 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5006, ETH-5008, ETH-5025.
**Trap:** The 1988 Act did not create the 1947 Act; it consolidated it. Do not describe 1988 as "first" anti-corruption law.
**Mains hook:** Trace the statutory evolution in one paragraph, then move to the 2018 reform.
**Back:** *"Forty-seven, fifty-two, then eighty-eight consolidated them; 2018 amended."*

### Flashcard ETH-5006 — PC Act s.4(4): trial timelines
**Front:** What timelines does s.4(4) of the PC Act set for trial?
**Definition (learn):** Day-to-day trial as far as practicable. An endeavour to conclude within two years, extendable by six months at a time for recorded reasons, with total time not ordinarily above four years (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988).
**Breakdown:**
- Day-to-day trial: as far as practicable.
- Endeavour: 2 years.
- Extension: 6 months at a time, recorded reasons.
- Ceiling: 4 years ordinarily.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wikipedia confirms the 2 years, 6 months and 4 years figures.
**Links:** ETH-5005, ETH-5021.
**Trap:** These are "endeavour" timelines, not hard limits. Non-compliance does not void the trial.
**Mains hook:** Use timelines to argue for speedy trial, then add the qualification that the endeavour language has limited enforceability.
**Back:** *"Two years to try, six to extend, four to end."*

### Flashcard ETH-5007 — Section 7 PC Act (2018): undue advantage
**Front:** What does s.7 (as amended in 2018) penalise?
**Definition (learn):** A public servant who obtains, accepts or attempts to obtain an undue advantage, with the aim of performing a public duty improperly or dishonestly. (✅ Wikipedia: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988; ✅ owner 19)
**Breakdown:**
- Replaced "gratification other than legal remuneration" with "undue advantage" (✅ Wikipedia).
- Punishment: 3 to 7 years' imprisonment (✅ Wikipedia).
- Actor, conduct, intent and public-duty link must all be proved (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Amendment in force 26 July 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-5008, ETH-5009, ETH-5019.
**Trap:** Every gift or poor decision is not an s.7 offence. The public-duty nexus must be proved.
**Mains hook:** Use the change from "gratification" to "undue advantage" to show the law became broader in wording but still needs proof of nexus.
**Back:** *"Undue, not just unwanted: the duty must be bought."*

### Flashcard ETH-5008 — Section 7A PC Act: the intermediary (tout) provision
**Front:** Which PC Act section reaches a tout or middleman who is not a public servant?
**Definition (learn):** Section 7A (inserted 2018) reaches a person who accepts, obtains or attempts to obtain an undue advantage as motive or reward to induce a public servant, by corrupt or illegal means or by personal influence, to perform or forbear a public duty improperly (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- s.7: the public servant.
- s.7A: the intermediary who monetises access.
- s.8: the bribe-giver.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inserted 2018 (✅ owner 19). Wikipedia fetch did not cover s.7A; wording is owner text, check the Gazette (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5007, ETH-5009, ETH-5040 (tout case).
**Trap:** A common error: "tout is outside the PC Act, so only departmental action is possible". The answer is s.7A.
**Mains hook:** A tout-network case engages all three sections against different actors. An answer citing only s.7 leaves the intermediary unaccounted for.
**Back:** *"Seven is the official, seven-A the broker, eight the payer."*

### Flashcard ETH-5009 — Section 8: bribe-giving offence and seven-day coercion defence
**Front:** What is the punishment for giving a bribe, and what defence exists?
**Definition (learn):** Under s.8 (2018), giving an undue advantage is a standalone offence, punishable with imprisonment up to seven years, fine, or both. Defence: the giver was compelled and reported the matter to a law-enforcement or investigating agency within seven days of giving (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Standalone bribe-giving offence (new in 2018).
- Defence requires both compulsion and reporting within seven days.
- Replaces the old s.24 protection (ETH-5010).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Up to 7 years (✅ owner 19). Wikipedia confirms bribe-giving is an offence but did not cover the seven-day text (⚠️ re-check Gazette text).
**Links:** ETH-5002, ETH-5010, ETH-5035.
**Trap:** The seven-day window is a reporting condition, not a limitation on prosecution in general.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the seven-day window helps prevent false "coercion" claims but may burden low-literacy victims. Qualified conclusion: keep the defence, extend reporting help.
**Back:** *"Pay under pressure? Tell within seven days."*

### Flashcard ETH-5010 — Section 24 PC Act: omitted in 2018
**Front:** What did s.24 protect, and what happened to it?
**Definition (learn):** Before 2018, s.24 protected a person who stated in proceedings against a public servant that he had offered gratification, from prosecution under s.12 (abetment-type offence). It was omitted by the 2018 Amendment (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Protection was tied to a statement in proceedings. It was not a blanket immunity.
- Omitted 2018 (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wikipedia does not cover s.24 (⚠️ re-check Gazette).
**Links:** ETH-5009, ETH-5005.
**Trap:** Do not say "s.24 gave all bribe-givers immunity". It applied only to a statement made in proceedings.
**Mains hook:** Explain why the 2018 omission closed a coercive-versus-collusive mismatch, and what is lost for prosecution.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four spoke, twelve was spared; 2018 struck the page."*

### Flashcard ETH-5011 — Sections 9 and 10: commercial organisations
**Front:** What liability does s.9 create for commercial organisations?
**Definition (learn):** A commercial organisation commits an offence if a person associated with it gives or promises an undue advantage to a public servant to obtain or retain business. Penalty: a fine (✅ Wikipedia: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988).
**Breakdown:**
- s.9: the organisation is liable for fine (fine amount not prescribed in the Act, per Wikipedia).
- s.10: directors, managers or officers are liable where the offence was with their "consent or connivance" (✅ owner 19, s.10 not confirmed by Wikipedia; ⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** New in 2018 (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-5007, ETH-5009, ETH-5012.
**Trap:** Do not confuse corporate liability (s.9) with individual liability (s.10 "consent or connivance").
**Mains hook:** Use corporate liability to argue the 2018 Act closed the "only the individual pays" gap. Qualify that the fine is not fixed.
**Back:** *"The firm pays a fine; the boss pays for connivance."*

### Flashcard ETH-5012 — Section 13: narrowed criminal misconduct
**Front:** What does s.13 cover after 2018?
**Definition (learn):** Criminal misconduct now covers (a) dishonest or fraudulent misappropriation or conversion of entrusted property, and (b) intentional illicit enrichment during office. The abuse-of-position head was removed (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Head (a): entrusted property.
- Head (b): illicit enrichment.
- Removed: abuse of position to obtain an advantage (pre-2018 s.13(1)(d)).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wikipedia says the threshold "has been increased" (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-5033 (2018 trade-off), ETH-5014.
**Trap:** The 2018 Amendment narrowed, not expanded, s.13. Do not reverse this.
**Mains hook:** Assess whether narrowing s.13 weakens protection against favouritism without enrichment. Qualified conclusion: it reduces reach against favouritism while improving certainty for officials.
**Back:** *"Thirteen keeps the till and the wealth; it lost the favour."*

### Flashcard ETH-5013 — Section 19: sanction for prosecution
**Front:** Which PC Act offences need prior sanction before cognizance, and what is the timeline?
**Definition (learn):** Prior sanction of the competent authority before a court takes cognizance of offences under ss.7, 10, 11, 13 and 15 against a public servant. The authority must endeavour to decide within three months, extendable by one month where legal consultation is needed (✅ owner 19, 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Stage: before cognizance (court-level, later than s.17A).
- Sections: 7, 10, 11, 13, 15.
- Timeline: three months plus one month.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner text; Wikipedia page only says sanction is required for serving public servants (⚠️ detail not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-5014 (s.17A), ETH-5016, ETH-5043.
**Trap:** s.19 is sanction for prosecution. s.17A is approval for enquiry or investigation. Do not mix the two stages.
**Mains hook:** Argue that fixed timelines protect honest officials, and recommend a default or escalation rule, since neither s.17A nor s.19 creates deemed approval.
**Back:** *"Nineteen says court-ready; seventeen-A says probe-ready."*

### Flashcard ETH-5014 — Section 17A PC Act: prior approval before enquiry
**Front:** What does s.17A (2018) require before a police officer acts?
**Definition (learn):** Prior approval of the appropriate authority before any enquiry, inquiry or investigation into an offence "relatable to any recommendation made or decision taken by such public servant in discharge of his official functions or duties" (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Rank-neutral: not based on joint-secretary rank.
- Applies to serving and retired public servants (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 19).
- Exception: arrest on the spot accepting or attempting to accept an undue advantage (✅ Wikipedia "redhanded").
- Timeline: three months, extendable by one month for recorded reasons (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inserted 2018 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5015, ETH-5016, ETH-5013.
**Trap:** Section 17A is not a sanction for prosecution. It applies earlier, at enquiry.
**Mains hook:** Assess 17A as a safeguard for honest decision-makers and as a possible delay. Name the reform: a time-bound decision with a consequence for delay.
**Back:** *"Seventeen-A asks before the first question."*

### Flashcard ETH-5015 — Section 17A: judicial status (2026)
**Front:** What is the current judicial status of s.17A's constitutional validity?
**Definition (learn):** In *Centre for Public Interest Litigation v. Union of India*, 2026 INSC 55 (13 January 2026), separate opinions diverged and the operative order referred the issues through the Chief Justice of India for an appropriate Bench. No final binding resolution (📰 ✅ owner 19, 22; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Separate opinions: divergent.
- Operative step: referral to CJI for Bench.
- Result: validity still open.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 January 2026; 2026 INSC 55 (⚠️ check the official order before quoting).
**Links:** ETH-5014, ETH-5045, ETH-5059.
**Trap:** Do not say the Supreme Court "upheld" or "struck down" s.17A in January 2026.
**Mains hook:** State the statutory text, the pending question, and say it is not finally settled.
**Back:** *"Opinions split, referral sent: still open."*

### Flashcard ETH-5016 — DSPE s.6A single directive vs s.17A
**Front:** How does the old s.6A single directive differ from s.17A?
**Definition (learn):** s.6A of the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 (added by CVC Act, 2003) required prior Central Government approval for investigating officers of Joint Secretary rank and above. It was rank-based. s.17A is rank-neutral and conduct-specific (✅ owner 19, 21).
**Breakdown:**
- s.6A: rank-based; struck down 2014 (ETH-5017).
- s.17A: decision-based; applies regardless of rank.
- Both: prior approval before investigation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.6A struck down 6 May 2014 (✅ owner 21, ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-5014, ETH-5017, ETH-5018.
**Trap:** The "Joint Secretary and above single directive" is no longer current law.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2014-to-2018 shift as evidence that the protection moved from rank to conduct.
**Back:** *"Rank fell in 2014; decision rose in 2018."*

### Flashcard ETH-5017 — Subramanian Swamy: s.6A struck down (2014)
**Front:** Which case struck down DSPE Act s.6A, and when?
**Definition (learn):** *Dr. Subramanian Swamy v. Director, CBI*, (2014) 8 SCC 682, decided 6 May 2014, held the rank-based single directive violated Article 14 (✅ owner 21, 22; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Court: Constitution Bench.
- Ground: Article 14 (rank-based classification is arbitrary).
- Consequence: s.17A (2018) is its differently framed successor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (2014) 8 SCC 682; 6 May 2014.
**Links:** ETH-5016, ETH-5018.
**Trap:** Do not cite the Supreme Court as having upheld s.17A in this judgment. s.17A came later.
**Mains hook:** The Article 14 reasoning ("corruption knows no rank") supports the conduct-based design of s.17A.
**Back:** *"Six-May-two-fourteen: rank out, Article 14 in."*

### Flashcard ETH-5018 — Vineet Narain: the Jain Hawala case
**Front:** What did *Vineet Narain* (1997) decide on the single-point directive, and what did it lead to?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court annulled the executive-direction version of the single-point directive. The CVC was given statutory superintendence over CBI's anti-corruption functions by the CVC Act, 2003 (✅ owner 20, 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Decision date: 18 December 1997 (✅ owner 20).
- Report: (1998) 1 SCC 226 (✅ owner 20).
- Consequence: single directive re-enacted as DSPE s.6A (2003), then struck down (2014).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Jain Hawala case (✅ owner 21, ARC 7.4).
**Links:** ETH-5017, ETH-5020, ETH-5037.
**Trap:** Dates: decision 1997; report volume 1998. Do not write "1998 judgment".
**Mains hook:** Use Vineet Narain as the origin of the CVC's statutory status and of the ban on executive-only shields.
**Back:** *"Ninety-seven decided, ninety-eight reported, two-thousand-three gave the CVC its statute."*

### Flashcard ETH-5019 — PC (Amendment) Act 2018: dates
**Front:** When did the PC (Amendment) Act, 2018 take effect?
**Definition (learn):** It came into force on 26 July 2018 (✅ Wikipedia: Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988; "16 of 2018").
**Breakdown:**
- Amending Act number: 16 of 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Commencement: 26 July 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner gives assent also on 26 July 2018 and S.O. 3662(E) (✅ owner 19, ⚠️ assent date not confirmed by fetch).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Commencement 26 July 2018.
**Links:** ETH-5007 to ETH-5014, ETH-5033.
**Trap:** Commencement and assent may differ; the owner's "same day" statement needs the Gazette (⚠️).
