**Mains hook:** Use the date as a reference when comparing pre- and post-2018 provisions.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six July, sixteen of 2018."*

### Flashcard ETH-5020 — Benami law: 1988 Act and s.5 confiscation
**Front:** Why was the original Benami Act, 1988 largely inoperative in confiscation?
**Definition (learn):** The Benami Transactions (Prohibition) Act, 1988 prohibits holding property in another's name to conceal true ownership. Its s.5 confiscation provision needed rules that were not framed for 18 years (ARC 3.5.3; ✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Prohibition: benami holding.
- Confiscation: s.5, needed rules.
- Gap: 18 years without rules.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 3.5.1–3.5.4 (✅ owner 19, 20).
**Links:** ETH-5021, ETH-5022, ETH-5023.
**Trap:** Do not say confiscation was "freely" possible under the 1988 Act. It was largely inoperative.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the 2016 amendment fixed the design but the rules gap shows how implementation can fail.
**Back:** *"Eighty-eight law, eighteen years of rule-less rest."*

### Flashcard ETH-5021 — Benami Amendment 2016: in force 1 November 2016
**Front:** When did the 2016 Benami Amendment take effect, and what is the Act called now?
**Definition (learn):** Benami Transactions (Prohibition) Amendment Act, 2016 substantially re-enacted the 1988 Act as the Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act, 1988, with fuller definitions, adjudication and confiscation (✅ owner 19, 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Assent: 10 August 2016 (✅ owner 19).
- In force: 1 November 2016 (S.O. 3289(E), 25 October 2016) (✅ owner 19, 20).
- Renamed: Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act, 1988.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not verified by fetch (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5020, ETH-5022, ETH-5023.
**Trap:** Do not say the 2016 amendment is a new Act with a new year (it is the 1988 Act as amended).
**Mains hook:** Explain why the retrospectivity question matters for pending cases.
**Back:** *"Sixteen amended, eighty-eight remains: one-November live."*

### Flashcard ETH-5022 — Ganpati Dealcom: 2022 holding and 2024 recall
**Front:** What did *Union of India v. Ganpati Dealcom* (2022) hold, and what happened in October 2024?
**Definition (learn):** On 23 August 2022 a two-judge Bench held the 2016 Amendment could not apply retrospectively to pre-2016 transactions and struck down the unamended Act's ss.3(2) and 5. On 18 October 2024 the Supreme Court recalled that judgment in full and restored the appeal (📰 ✅ owner 19, 20; ⚠️ not fetched; the official recall order is the anchor).
**Breakdown:**
- 2022: retrospectivity denied; ss.3(2), 5 struck (owner).
- 2024: recall in full (2024 INSC 799, owner 20).
- Current: retrospectivity open.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 August 2022; 18 October 2024; 2024 INSC 799 (⚠️ verify).
**Links:** ETH-5021, ETH-5023, ETH-5050.
**Trap:** Do not cite the 2022 holding as settled. Cite the recall.
**Mains hook:** Use the open question to show how legislative strengthening can face years of contestation.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two said no; twenty-four said recall: it is open."*

### Flashcard ETH-5023 — Four parallel processes on one act
**Front:** What four processes can one suspected corrupt act trigger?
**Definition (learn):** (a) vigilance screening for a "vigilance angle"; (b) departmental proceedings under CCS or All-India Services rules, on preponderance of probability; (c) PC Act prosecution before a Special Judge, beyond reasonable doubt; (d) civil or recovery action such as Benami confiscation or money-laundering attachment (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Different standards of proof.
- Different timelines.
- Acquittal in criminal case does not end departmental proceedings (⚠️ owner analytical point).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Departmental penalties can extend to dismissal (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5026, ETH-5045.
**Trap:** Acquittal in court is not the end of departmental action. Answers often miss this.
**Mains hook:** Map the four tracks to the case facts and say which track bears the burden of proof.
**Back:** *"Four tracks, four standards: vigil, depart, court, recover."*

### Flashcard ETH-5024 — Non-performance as an ethical failure (acts of omission)
**Front:** When can wilful non-performance count as an ethical or corruption failure, and what is the limit?
**Definition (learn):** ARC's broader framing covers acts of omission: wilful inaction or failure to enforce known rules that causes public loss or undue private benefit. The base is Rule 3 (devotion to duty) of the CCS/AIS Conduct Rules (✅ owner 19; ⚠️ rule number from owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Requires wilfulness or gross negligence.
- Not a bad outcome from a good-faith decision.
- Bona fides test (ETH-5040) guards the limit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 3.2.1.7 "squandering" category (✅ owner 19, 18).
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5040, ETH-5044.
**Trap:** "Non-performance can never be corruption unless a bribe is proved" is wrong. But a bad outcome is not corruption either.
**Mains hook:** Use the wilfulness threshold to separate misconduct from honest risk-taking. Qualified conclusion: act on wilful omission, protect good-faith risk.
**Back:** *"Wilful silence is misconduct; honest failure is not."*

### Flashcard ETH-5025 — Whistleblowing ladder (basic version)
**Front:** What ladder should a whistleblower follow before external disclosure?
**Definition (learn):** Owner sequence: (a) verify facts and preserve records; (b) use authorised internal or vigilance channels (CVO, CVC under PIDPI, or Lokpal); (c) rely on confidentiality and anti-retaliation safeguards; (d) external disclosure only where internal channels failed and harm is grave and imminent; (e) resignation only after exhaustion (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Rung 1: records.
- Rung 2: internal channels.
- Rung 3: confidential route.
- Rung 4: external, last resort.
- Rung 5: resignation, last.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner calls it "nine-step" in section 7 but lists five rungs (a)–(e) in section 3 (⚠️ owner internal inconsistency; use five rungs).
**Links:** ETH-5027, ETH-5028, ETH-5041, ETH-5049.
**Trap:** The external-disclosure step is not the first move.
**Mains hook:** A PYQ-style whistleblower question should follow the ladder step by step, not a generic "report and resign".
**Back:** *"Records, channel, shield, last-resort press, then resign."*

### Flashcard ETH-5026 — Whistleblower protection: comparative models
**Front:** Which comparative statutes did the ARC cite for whistleblower protection?
**Definition (learn):** ARC 3.6.2 listed the UK Public Interest Disclosure Act, 1998; Australia's Public Interest Disclosure Act, 1994; New Zealand's Protected Disclosures Act, 2000; and a US statute (ARC names it "1984"). The US federal statute is the Whistleblower Protection Act of 1989 (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- UK 1998.
- Australia 1994.
- NZ 2000.
- US 1989 (correct year, ARC text says 1984).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ The ARC's US year is wrong; flag if quoted.
**Links:** ETH-5027, ETH-5028.
**Trap:** Quoting "1984" as the US federal statute year.
**Mains hook:** Use the comparative models to argue for a statutory route with clear identity protection.
**Back:** *"Britain ninety-eight, Oz ninety-four, Kiwi two-thousand, US eighty-nine."*

### Flashcard ETH-5027 — Law Commission 179th Report and the 2014 Act
**Front:** What is the origin of the Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014?
**Definition (learn):** The Law Commission's 179th Report proposed a Public Interest Disclosure (Protection of Informers) Bill. That proposal is the direct ancestor of the 2014 Act (✅ owner 19, ARC 3.6.3).
**Breakdown:**
- 179th Report (Law Commission).
- Bill: Public Interest Disclosure (Protection of Informers).
- Outcome: Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Assent 9 May 2014 (✅ owner 19, ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-5026, ETH-5029, ETH-5043.
**Trap:** The 2014 Act is not the same text as the 179th Report's bill.
**Mains hook:** Trace origin, then assess the gap: enacted but not in force.
**Back:** *"One-seventy-nine proposed, two-fourteen enacted, never switched on."*

### Flashcard ETH-5028 — Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014: status
**Front:** What is the current status of the Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014?
**Definition (learn):** Assented 9 May 2014. As of the owner's verification it has not been brought into force. The 2015 Amendment Bill lapsed (✅ owner 19, 21, 22; 📰 as-of date not fixed; ⚠️ re-check commencement notice).
**Breakdown:**
- Assent: 9 May 2014.
- Commencement: not notified (owner).
- 2015 Amendment Bill: lapsed with dissolution of 16th Lok Sabha (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Commencement status: owner text as at its verification date (⚠️ time-varying).
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5029, ETH-5030.
**Trap:** Do not describe the Act as "in force" or "protecting" whistleblowers statutorily.
**Mains hook:** A whistleblower question should state that statutory protection is not operative and use PIDPI as the interim channel.
**Back:** *"Assented May, not commenced: protection on paper."*

### Flashcard ETH-5029 — PIDPI Resolution, 2004
**Front:** What is the PIDPI Resolution and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** The Public Interest Disclosure and Protection of Informers Resolution designates the CVC as the receiving authority for corruption complaints against central-government and PSU employees. Identity is kept confidential and no acknowledgment is issued (✅ owner 19, 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Receiving authority: CVC.
- Scope: central government and PSU employees.
- Safeguard: confidentiality, no acknowledgment.
- Nature: non-statutory administrative resolution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Resolution No. 371/12/2002-AVD-III, 21 April 2004 (✅ owner 19, 21; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5027, ETH-5030, ETH-5031.
**Trap:** PIDPI does not cover state-government employees (owner), and it excludes anonymous or pseudonymous complaints.
**Mains hook:** Argue PIDPI is the operative interim mechanism, but non-statutory. Qualify: the interim system depends on executive goodwill.
**Back:** *"Oh-four, the CVC receives; the name stays hidden."*

### Flashcard ETH-5030 — Whistleblower protection gap: cases
**Front:** Which two cases illustrate the whistleblower protection gap in ARC Box 3.1?
**Definition (learn):** Satyendra Dubey (NHAI, exposed road-construction corruption; found dead November 2003) and Manjunath Shanmugam (IOC, refused bribes and resisted petrol adulteration; shot dead November 2005) (✅ owner 19, 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Dubey: NHAI, road construction, 2003.
- Shanmugam: IOC, petrol adulteration, 2005.
- Both cited by ARC as protection gaps.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nov 2003; Nov 2005 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5028, ETH-5025, ETH-5041.
**Trap:** Do not present the deaths as proved murders in court in this card; the owner states the deaths as ARC facts only.
**Mains hook:** Use the cases as the human cost of the protection gap, then propose the enforceable statutory fix.
**Back:** *"Dubey for roads, Manjunath for petrol: both silenced, the law still asleep."*

### Flashcard ETH-5031 — ARC proposal: NGO and public-utility coverage
**Front:** Which bodies did the ARC propose bringing under the PC Act?
**Definition (learn):** Privately provided public-utility services, and substantially government-funded NGOs (more than 50% of annual operating cost, or at least ₹1 crore in any of the preceding three years) (✅ owner 19, ARC 3.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Public-utility services provided privately.
- NGOs: >50% of operating cost, or ≥₹1 crore in any of 3 preceding years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 3.3 (✅ owner 19, 20).
**Links:** ETH-5032, ETH-5046.
**Trap:** The 2013 Lokpal Act's thresholds for bodies differ (ETH-5046). Do not mix the two.
**Mains hook:** Recommend coverage of substantially state-funded NGOs, and state the threshold you propose.
**Back:** *"Half the cost or a crore: the NGO enters the net."*

### Flashcard ETH-5032 — Collusive bribery penalty: inconsistency inside the ARC
**Front:** What penalty did the ARC recommend for collusive bribery?
**Definition (learn):** Two ARC statements differ. The body of 3.2.2.6 says the punishment "should be increased to 10 years". The formal recommendation 3.2.2.7(c) says "double that of other cases of bribery" (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Body text: 10 years.
- Formal recommendation: double the general bribery penalty.
- Owner flags this as a conflict.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 3.2.2.6–3.2.2.7 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5003, ETH-5031.
**Trap:** Quote the formal recommendation when asked for the recommendation. Mention the body text if asked for the figure in discussion.
**Mains hook:** Use the double-penalty recommendation to argue for a distinct offence with a presumption where state loss is proved.
**Back:** *"Ten in the body, double in the rule: quote the rule."*

### Flashcard ETH-5033 — 2018 PC Amendment: a double-edged reform
**Front:** Why does the 2018 PC Amendment count as a "double-edged" reform?
**Definition (learn):** It strengthens bribe-giving liability and adds corporate liability, while narrowing criminal misconduct (s.13) and adding a pre-investigation approval (s.17A) (✅ owner 19, advanced 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Strengthened: s.8 bribe-giving; ss.9–10 corporate liability.
- Narrowed: s.13.
- Added hurdle: s.17A.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 26 July 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-5012, ETH-5014, ETH-5033.
**Trap:** The 2018 Act did not simply "strengthen" the law. Give both sides.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate the trade-off: protects honest officials and coerced givers, but may limit reach against favouritism. Qualified conclusion: depends on timelines and reporting help.
**Back:** *"Strong on givers, narrow on takers, careful on enquiry."*

### Flashcard ETH-5034 — Section 8 coercion defence: critique
**Front:** What is the advanced critique of the seven-day reporting window?
**Definition (learn):** The window may be too short for a genuinely coerced, low-literacy or fearful citizen to use the reporting process. This could undermine protection for the very victims the ARC wanted to protect (⚠️ owner analysis, advanced 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Defence: compulsion plus reporting within seven days.
- Concern: access and literacy.
- Counter: window also blocks after-the-fact false claims.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Seven days (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5009, ETH-5035.
**Trap:** Do not treat the window as a rule against all bribe-givers. It is conditional.
**Mains hook:** Recommend a reporting help desk rather than a longer window. Qualify the tradeoff.
**Back:** *"Seven days, fair to the coerced, hard for the frightened."*

### Flashcard ETH-5035 — Section 13(1)(e) and s.20: presumption-shifting provisions
**Front:** Which provisions shift the burden of proof in PC Act cases?
**Definition (learn):** Pre-2018 s.13(1)(e) made a public servant guilty if unable to account satisfactorily for disproportionate assets. s.20 shifts the burden to the accused once acceptance of an undue advantage is proved (✅ owner 19, ARC 3.2.2.5).
**Breakdown:**
- Burden shift on disproportionate assets (pre-2018).
- Burden shift on acceptance (s.20).
- Post-2018 illicit enrichment head sits in s.13(1)(b) (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 3.2.2.5 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5012, ETH-5009.
**Trap:** These are recognised exceptions to the presumption of innocence. Do not describe them as ordinary.
**Mains hook:** Justify the shift in proof for illicit wealth, then state the safeguard: the accused must be given a chance to account.
**Back:** *"Account for your wealth, prove what you took."*

### Flashcard ETH-5036 — Mechanism: the tout network under the 2026 GS-IV fact pattern
**Front:** How should a tout network inside a public office be classified and charged?
**Definition (learn):** Hybrid: applicants who pay for entitled approvals are coercive-bribery victims. Touts and colluding subordinates who share rent are in a collusive relationship (⚠️ owner 19 analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Section 7 against the public servant.
- Section 7A against the tout.
- Section 8 against an applicant who paid willingly for an unlawful advantage.
- Section 17A where conduct relates to an official decision.
- Section 19 sanction before cognizance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q9 (✅ owner 19, 22).
**Links:** ETH-5008, ETH-5009, ETH-5014, ETH-5015.
**Trap:** Citing only s.7 leaves the tout unaccounted for.
**Mains hook:** Classify, then route each actor to the statutory response. Qualified conclusion: act on evidence, not sweeping action.
**Back:** *"Seven, seven-A, eight, seventeen-A: name each actor."*

### Flashcard ETH-5037 — Mechanism: the statutory response layered by actor
**Front:** Which PC Act provision applies to which actor in a bribery chain?
**Definition (learn):** Public servant: s.7. Intermediary: s.7A. Bribe-giver: s.8. Commercial organisation: s.9. Director or officer: s.10. Prior approval for enquiry: s.17A. Sanction for cognizance: s.19 (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- One chain, several actors.
- Each actor needs its own provision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As per owner, Wikipedia confirms s.7, s.9 (⚠️ others).
**Links:** ETH-5036, ETH-5008, ETH-5011.
**Trap:** Using one section for the whole network.
**Mains hook:** Use the actor table to structure any bribery answer.
**Back:** *"Seven official, seven-A broker, eight payer, nine firm."*

### Flashcard ETH-5038 — Coercive-plus-collusive case: continuing injury
**Front:** Why is delay in acting against an extortion network not neutral?
**Definition (learn):** Extortion from an entitled citizen is a continuing injury. Each day of delay adds harm (⚠️ owner 19 analytical point).
**Breakdown:**
- Continuing harm: fresh loss each day.
- Remedy must still be evidence-based.
- Sweeping unsupported action replaces one arbitrariness with another.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Refs: 21, 22 Sec. 14.18 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** ETH-5036, ETH-5045.
**Trap:** "Speed is always right" is wrong; the remedy must still be proportionate.
**Mains hook:** State the urgency and the evidence standard together.
**Back:** *"Delay is harm, but haste is not proof."*

### Flashcard ETH-5039 — Rent is priced on opacity
**Front:** Why does publishing timelines reduce intermediary income?
**Definition (learn):** Where queues are invisible and timelines unpublished, the intermediary sells information and certainty. Publishing the disposal sequence and pendency removes the rent (⚠️ owner 19 analysis; 17, 22 Sec. 14A.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Rent sources: opacity, delay, uncertainty.
- Publishing: attacks the rent directly.
- Legal action and transparency are complements.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Refs: 17 (service delivery), 22 Sec. 14.18.
**Links:** ETH-5036, ETH-5049 (two-track template).
**Trap:** Do not treat transparency as an alternative to prosecution.
**Mains hook:** The transparency measure works before trials conclude. Qualify with enforcement.
**Back:** *"Publish the queue, the price collapses."*

### Flashcard ETH-5040 — Bona fides test: the vigilance standard
**Front:** What is the bona fides test, and where does it sit in this framework?
**Definition (learn):** Whether a person of common prudence, working within the rules, would have taken the decision in the prevailing circumstances in the organisation's genuine interest. If yes, no vigilance angle, even if the outcome went wrong (ARC 7.1; ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Standard: common prudence.
- Scope: decision process, not outcome.
- Link to moral luck (ETH-5058).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.1 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** ETH-5024, ETH-5044, ETH-5105.
**Trap:** Judging by outcome. The test looks at process.
**Mains hook:** Apply the test to a good-faith loan or contract that defaults. Conclude: no vigilance angle unless misconduct is shown.
**Back:** *"Prudent process, not lucky outcome."*

### Flashcard ETH-5041 — Acts of omission: safeguard against over-criminalising
**Front:** How is the bona fides test a safeguard against over-criminalising non-performance?
**Definition (learn):** The test separates an honest, failed decision from wilful or grossly negligent omission (⚠️ owner 19 and 21 analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Wilful omission: misconduct.
- Honest failure: no angle.
- Limit: needs evidence, not outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Refs: 19, 21.
**Links:** ETH-5024, ETH-5040.
**Trap:** Conflating a bad outcome with corruption.
**Mains hook:** Name the test explicitly in any omission case.
**Back:** *"Omission needs will; failure needs only bad luck, not guilt."*

### Flashcard ETH-5042 — Mains concept: classify then respond
**Front:** What is the first analytical move in any bribery case study?
**Definition (learn):** Classify the bribery as coercive or collusive, then apply the response that fits (⚠️ owner 19 section 8).
**Breakdown:**
- Coercive: protect the complainant.
- Collusive: investigate willing beneficiary and recipient under the ingredients.
- Both: hybrid, layer the response.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 1.13, 3.2.2.
**Links:** ETH-5002, ETH-5003, ETH-5036.
**Trap:** Skipping classification and recommending "strengthen the law".
**Mains hook:** Name the specific reform, for example collusive-bribery classification, NGO coverage or whistleblower enforcement.
**Back:** *"Classify, then cure."*

### Flashcard ETH-5043 — Whistleblower law: the answer structure
**Front:** How should a whistleblower-law answer be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Cite Manjunath and Dubey (ARC Box 3.1), the 179th Report, the 2014 Act's assent and non-commencement, and the PIDPI interim route (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Human cost: Dubey, Shanmugam.
- Origin: 179th Report.
- Status: assent 9 May 2014, not in force.
- Interim: PIDPI 2004.
- Reform: enforce the 2014 Act.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5027, ETH-5028, ETH-5029, ETH-5030.
**Trap:** Stating the 2014 Act "protects whistleblowers".
**Mains hook:** Recommend commencement, identity protection and a fixed follow-up timeline.
**Back:** *"Cite the dead, the report, the assent, the interim, the fix."*

### Flashcard ETH-5044 — Mains: reforms by name (not generic)
**Front:** Which reforms should a Mains answer name for anti-corruption law?
**Definition (learn):** Collusive-bribery classification; PC Act coverage for substantially state-funded NGOs; clear Benami transition rules; whistleblower-law enforcement; time-bound s.17A decisions (✅ owner 19, advanced 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Named reforms, not "strengthen the law".
- Each reform needs one sentence of rationale.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5003, ETH-5031, ETH-5022, ETH-5028, ETH-5045.
**Trap:** Vague reform lists score less.
**Mains hook:** Pick three reforms and justify each.
**Back:** *"Name the reform, give the reason."*

### Flashcard ETH-5045 — Section 17A: time limits without deemed approval
**Front:** Do ss.17A and 19 create deemed approval if the timeline lapses?
**Definition (learn):** No. Both provide a timeline (three months, extendable by one month), but neither provides for deemed approval or sanction on expiry (✅ owner 19, advanced 19).
**Breakdown:**
- s.17A: three months, one extension, recorded reasons.
- s.19: endeavour, three months, one extension.
- No deemed approval.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5013, ETH-5014, ETH-5059.
**Trap:** "Lapse means approval granted" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Recommend a default or escalation rule, not deemed approval.
**Back:** *"Clock runs out, approval does not."*

### Flashcard ETH-5046 — 2013 Lokpal Act: FCRA and government-financed bodies
**Front:** What thresholds does the 2013 Lokpal Act use for bodies outside government?
**Definition (learn):** s.14(1)(g) covers bodies receiving foreign contribution under FCRA 2010 in excess of ₹10 lakh a year. s.14(1)(h) covers bodies wholly or partly government-financed above an amount the Central Government notifies (✅ owner 22 advanced 20; ⚠️ not verified by fetch).
**Breakdown:**
- FCRA route: ₹10 lakh a year.
- Government-financed route: notified amount.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Section numbers and threshold from owner, not fetched.
**Links:** ETH-5031, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Do not use the ARC's 50%/₹1 crore NGO test for the Lokpal Act.
**Mains hook:** Compare the ARC proposal with the enacted thresholds.
**Back:** *"Ten lakh foreign, notified domestic."*

### Flashcard ETH-5047 — Anti-corruption law: the three-layer check
**Front:** What three checks should a legal-framework answer include?
**Definition (learn):** (1) Offence provision; (2) procedural gate (ss.17A, 19); (3) asset-recovery and whistleblower layer. Note the status of each (⚠️ synthesis of owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Offence: ss.7–13.
- Gate: 17A, 19.
- Recovery and informant: Benami Act, whistleblower law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5001, ETH-5013, ETH-5014.
**Trap:** Describing only the offence layer.
**Mains hook:** Use this check to structure any PC Act answer.
**Back:** *"Offence, gate, recovery: the law's three doors."*

### Flashcard ETH-5048 — Mechanism: the 2018 Act's structure in one table
**Front:** Compare pre-2018 and post-2018 PC Act provisions.
**Definition (learn):** Pre-2018: s.7 gratification; no bribe-giving offence; s.24 protection; s.13 includes abuse of position; s.19 sanction only. Post-2018: s.7 undue advantage; s.8 bribe-giving; s.24 omitted; s.13 narrowed; s.17A added; ss.9–10 corporate liability (✅ owner 19, advanced 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Offences: broader on givers.
- Procedure: stronger gate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5007 to ETH-5014.
**Trap:** Stating that pre-2018 law covered non-cash benefits poorly; the old text said gratification was not limited to money.
**Mains hook:** Use the comparison table to argue for and against.
**Back:** *"Gratification became undue; givers got their own section."*

### Flashcard ETH-5049 — Special Judge trial and sanction link
**Front:** Who tries PC Act offences, and what must precede cognizance?
**Definition (learn):** Special Judges try PC Act offences (s.4). For public servants, sanction (s.19) must precede cognizance of listed offences (✅ owner 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Trial: Special Judge.
- Sanction: s.19.
- Enquiry approval: s.17A.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5006, ETH-5013.
**Trap:** Special Judge trial does not remove sanction requirement.
**Mains hook:** State the forum and the gate.
**Back:** *"Special judge, sanction first."*

### Flashcard ETH-5050 — Current anchor: 2026 INSC 55 and the 2024 recall
**Front:** Which two current-anchor cases must an anti-corruption answer state accurately?
**Definition (learn):** 2026 INSC 55 (s.17A, divergent opinions, referral to CJI) and the 18 October 2024 recall of *Ganpati Dealcom* (📰 ✅ owner 19, advanced 19; ⚠️ re-check official text).
**Breakdown:**
- Two live, unsettled questions.
- Neither is a final resolution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 January 2026; 18 October 2024.
**Links:** ETH-5015, ETH-5022.
**Trap:** Presenting either as settled law.
**Mains hook:** Use them to show current gaps.
**Back:** *"Two open doors, not two closed ones."*

### GROUP — 20 Anti-Corruption Institutions

### Flashcard ETH-5051 — Institutional multiplicity: the ARC diagnosis
**Front:** What did the 2nd ARC say about anti-corruption institutions?
**Definition (learn):** They suffer from "a multiplicity... with overlapping functions", which undermined coherence and lacked unified focus (ARC 4.2.5; ✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Union level: CVC, departmental vigilance, CBI.
- State level: Lokayuktas, State Vigilance Commissions, Anti-Corruption Bureaus.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.2.5 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** ETH-5052, ETH-5070.
**Trap:** The 2013 Lokpal Act did not fully resolve State-level multiplicity (ETH-5073).
**Mains hook:** Diagnose multiplicity, then propose coordination, not one more agency.
**Back:** *"Many watchdogs, no single bark."*

### Flashcard ETH-5052 — CVC: origin and statute
**Front:** When was the CVC set up, and when did it get statutory status?
**Definition (learn):** The CVC was set up by executive resolution in 1964 on the Santhanam Committee's recommendation. The CVC Act, 2003 gave statutory status after *Vineet Narain* (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- 1964: executive setup.
- 2003: CVC Act (assent 11 September 2003, owner).
- Statutory superintendence over CBI's anti-corruption functions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner data; Wikipedia not fetched (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5018, ETH-5053.
**Trap:** The CVC was not created by the CVC Act; it existed from 1964.
**Mains hook:** Use 1964 and 2003 as the two stages of CVC's legitimacy.
**Back:** *"Sixty-four set up, two-thousand-three made statute."*

### Flashcard ETH-5053 — CVC: advisory and superintending, not prosecuting
**Front:** Does the CVC prosecute corruption cases?
**Definition (learn):** No. The CVC is advisory and superintending. Investigation and prosecution remain with CBI or state agencies, subject to sanction (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Advises the Union on vigilance.
- Superintends DSPE investigation of PC Act offences (not every CBI case).
- Does not determine guilt.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5052, ETH-5054.
**Trap:** "CVC directly prosecutes" is a trap option.
**Mains hook:** Name the CVC's role precisely in any institutional answer.
**Back:** *"CVC advises and watches; CBI investigates; courts decide."*

### Flashcard ETH-5054 — CBI: creation and legal base
**Front:** What created the CBI, and what gives its police powers?
**Definition (learn):** CBI was created by executive resolution in 1963. Its police powers derive from the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Creation: 1963 executive resolution.
- Police powers: DSPE Act, 1946.
- Anti-Corruption Division: investigates within jurisdiction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5055, ETH-5018.
**Trap:** CBI is not a statute-created body by its 1963 origin.
**Mains hook:** Link CBI's legal base to its federal consent gate (ETH-5055).
**Back:** *"Sixty-three resolved, forty-six gives the power."*

### Flashcard ETH-5055 — State consent under the DSPE Act
**Front:** What is the State-consent rule, and what caveat applies?
**Definition (learn):** DSPE police powers ordinarily need State consent within the State (general or case-specific). Withdrawal changes the route for fresh cases but does not stop the Supreme Court (Article 32) or High Courts (Article 226) from directing a CBI investigation (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Ordinary gate: State consent.
- Constitutional caveat: Art 32, 226.
- Exceptional, not a Union bypass.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5054, ETH-5056.
**Trap:** "Withdrawal makes every CBI investigation impossible" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Argue for federal consent and for exceptional court-ordered investigations.
**Back:** *"State says no, Articles 32 and 226 can still say go."*

### Flashcard ETH-5056 — ARC's Lok Pal proposal (2007)
**Front:** What did the ARC propose for a Lok Pal?
**Definition (learn):** A three-member body: a serving or retired Supreme Court judge as Chairman, an eminent jurist, and the CVC ex officio. It would enquire into allegations against Ministers and MPs in organic link with the CVC (ARC 4.3.1–4.3.4; ✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Members: 3.
- Chairman: judge.
- CVC: ex officio.
- Jurisdiction: Ministers and MPs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 2007 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** ETH-5057, ETH-5058, ETH-5059.
**Trap:** Do not describe the ARC body as the enacted Lokpal.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2007 design as a benchmark for lean, judiciary-anchored bodies.
**Back:** *"Three members, one judge, the CVC ex officio."*

### Flashcard ETH-5057 — Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act, 2013: composition
**Front:** What is the enacted Lokpal's composition?
**Definition (learn):** A Chairperson and not more than eight Members. At least 50% must be judicial members. At least 50% must come from SC, ST, OBC, minorities and women (✅ Wikipedia: Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act, 2013).
**Breakdown:**
- Chairperson: Wikipedia says "is or has been a Chief Justice of India" (⚠️ Act also allows other eligible persons, check).
- Members: up to 8.
- Judicial share: 50%.
- Reservation share: 50%.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Assent 1 January 2014; in force 16 January 2014 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-5056, ETH-5058.
**Trap:** Composition differs from the ARC's three-member proposal.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the 2013 design trades lean independence for representative legitimacy.
**Back:** *"Chair, eight, half judges, half representative."*

### Flashcard ETH-5058 — Lokpal and PM jurisdiction: ARC vs 2013 Act
**Front:** How does the 2013 Act treat the Prime Minister compared with the ARC?
**Definition (learn):** ARC: PM excluded; accountability to Parliament via no-confidence. 2013 Act: PM included, subject to exclusions (international relations, external and internal security, public order, atomic energy and space), a two-thirds approval of Members, and in-camera proceedings (✅ owner 20 advanced; ⚠️ the two-thirds and exclusions not confirmed by Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- ARC: blanket exclusion.
- Act: qualified inclusion.
- Threshold: higher procedural bar.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.3.5–4.3.10 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** ETH-5059, ETH-5060.
**Trap:** "The Act excludes the PM entirely" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Present both sides of the ARC's debate, then state the enacted compromise.
**Back:** *"ARC kept the PM for Parliament; the Act brings him in, carefully."*

### Flashcard ETH-5059 — ARC's PM accountability argument
**Front:** Why did the ARC think a Lok Pal enquiry into the PM would damage governance?
**Definition (learn):** The PM's Parliamentary-confidence basis means a formal enquiry, even if baseless, could paralyse government during the enquiry period. This is an institutional-continuity argument, not a claim of immunity (ARC 4.3.6–4.3.9; ✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Premise: PM depends on confidence.
- Risk: enquiry paralysis.
- Resolution: Parliament check.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.3.9 called the equality principle "technically correct" (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** ETH-5058, ETH-5015.
**Trap:** Reading the ARC argument as special privilege.
**Mains hook:** Assess it both ways in a 10-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Confidence keeps the PM; enquiry can paralyse him."*

### Flashcard ETH-5060 — Organic link: CVC under Lok Pal guidance
**Front:** What is the "organic link" the ARC proposed?
**Definition (learn):** The CVC works under the overall guidance and superintendence of the Lok Pal while keeping functional autonomy (ARC 4.3.3; ✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: coherent handling of minister-official collusion.
- Model: coordination, not merger.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.3.3.
**Links:** ETH-5051, ETH-5061.
**Trap:** Describing the link as a merger or as subordination with no autonomy.
**Mains hook:** Use the organic link as a model for coordination reform.
**Back:** *"Guided, not governed; autonomous, not alone."*

### Flashcard ETH-5061 — Lokpal power over CBI in referred cases
**Front:** What power does the 2013 Act give the Lokpal over CBI?
**Definition (learn):** The Lokpal may direct or refer investigation to CBI, and has its own inquiry and prosecution wings (✅ owner 20, advanced 20). Statutory power does not prove effectiveness.
**Breakdown:**
- Direct or supervise CBI in referred cases.
- Own inquiry wing.
- Verify operations from official data.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner text; not fetched (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5060, ETH-5054.
**Trap:** Power on paper is not operational effectiveness.
**Mains hook:** Ask for staffing, referrals and outcomes data before judging.
**Back:** *"Power on paper, proof in outcomes."*

### Flashcard ETH-5062 — Lokpal commencement and first Chairperson
**Front:** When did the Lokpal Act commence, and when was the first Chairperson appointed?
**Definition (learn):** Assent 1 January 2014; commencement 16 January 2014 (✅ Wikipedia). First Chairperson: Justice Pinaki Chandra Ghose. Owner says appointed 19 March 2019, assumed 23 March 2019; Wikipedia says 17 March 2019 (⚠️ conflict; check the Lokpal portal).
**Breakdown:**
- Commencement: 16 January 2014.
- Chair appointment: 17 or 19 March 2019 (conflict).
- Gap: about five years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5057, ETH-5063.
**Trap:** Quoting the appointment date without verifying the conflict.
**Mains hook:** Use the five-year gap as evidence of implementation lag.
**Back:** *"Commenced in fourteen, operated in nineteen."*

### Flashcard ETH-5063 — Lokayukta mandate: section 63
**Front:** What did s.63 require of States, and what is the gap?
**Definition (learn):** States had to establish a Lokayukta within one year of commencement, i.e. by 16 January 2015 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 20 advanced). No uniform structural model was prescribed (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Deadline: one year.
- Model: none uniform.
- Result: heterogeneity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section number from owner (⚠️ Wikipedia gave no number).
**Links:** ETH-5051, ETH-5062, ETH-5073.
**Trap:** Thinking States must copy a single model.
**Mains hook:** Propose common minimum safeguards for State Lokayuktas.
**Back:** *"One year to set up, no model to copy."*

### Flashcard ETH-5064 — Institutional-jurisdiction routing: three channels
**Front:** Which channel handles which actor in a district intermediary case?
**Definition (learn):** CVO and then CVC (or State Vigilance Commission) for official discipline and secret verification. State Anti-Corruption Bureau for trap or criminal action against touts. PIDPI-style confidential route for frightened staff (⚠️ owner 20 section 3, mechanism 7).
**Breakdown:**
- Officials: CVO, CVC.
- Touts: ACB.
- Frightened staff: confidential route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only.
**Links:** ETH-5029, ETH-5065, ETH-5074.
**Trap:** A single "refer to CVC" answer is undifferentiated.
**Mains hook:** Name the right channel for each actor and purpose.
**Back:** *"CVO for officials, ACB for brokers, PIDPI for the frightened."*

### Flashcard ETH-5065 — PIDPI limits: anonymous complaints
**Front:** Can a whistleblower complain anonymously under PIDPI and still get protection?
**Definition (learn):** No. PIDPI excludes anonymous and pseudonymous complaints. Identity must be known to the CVC and kept confidential (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Anonymous: excluded.
- Known but confidential: the design.
- Also: does not cover state-government staff (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5029, ETH-5064.
**Trap:** Anonymity and confidentiality are not the same safeguard.
**Mains hook:** State that confidentiality protects identity while the complaint is known to CVC.
**Back:** *"Known in the file, hidden from the room."*

### Flashcard ETH-5066 — Lokpal vs CVC vs CBI: comparison
**Front:** Compare CVC, CBI and the Lokpal.
**Definition (learn):** CVC: advisory and superintending. CBI: investigating agency. Lokpal: overarching jurisdiction over PM (qualified), Ministers, MPs, central officials, specified bodies; can direct or refer to CBI (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- CVC: oversight.
- CBI: investigation.
- Lokpal: jurisdiction and direction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5052, ETH-5054, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Calling the Lokpal a substitute for CVC or CBI. Owner says it works alongside them.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-part comparison in any institution answer.
**Back:** *"Advise, investigate, oversee: three hands, one job."*

### Flashcard ETH-5067 — 2013 Lokpal Act is not the ARC's proposal
**Front:** Is the 2013 Lokpal identical to the ARC's 2007 proposal?
**Definition (learn):** No. Composition, size and jurisdiction differ materially (✅ owner 20, advanced 20).
**Breakdown:**
- ARC: three members, lean.
- Act: up to nine including chair, broad jurisdiction.
- Both: related, distinct.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5056, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Treating them as identical.
**Mains hook:** Always distinguish proposal from enacted law.
**Back:** *"Recommended is not enacted."*

### Flashcard ETH-5068 — Lokpal Circular 01/2026 (current anchor)
**Front:** What current Lokpal procedure does the owner cite?
**Definition (learn):** Lokpal Circular No. 01/2026 dated 24 July 2026 sets procedures on complaints, listing, uploading orders and Section 14 jurisdiction (📰 ✅ owner 20; ⚠️ not verified).
**Breakdown:**
- Complaints procedure.
- Listing.
- Uploading orders.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 July 2026 (⚠️ verify).
**Links:** ETH-5057, ETH-5069.
**Trap:** Treating listing or upload as proof of guilt.
**Mains hook:** Use as transparency anchor, not as proof.
**Back:** *"Listed is not proven."*

### Flashcard ETH-5069 — Lokpal FY 2025-26 statistics
**Front:** What do the owner's FY 2025-26 Lokpal counts record?
**Definition (learn):** 558 complaints received, 431 registered, 407 registered disposed, 81 preliminary inquiries, 8 investigations, 0 prosecution sanctions, 2 disciplinary directions, 99 pending (📰 ✅ owner 20; ⚠️ unverified and time-varying).
**Breakdown:**
- Intake: 558.
- Registered: 431.
- Disposed: 407.
- Action: 81 PI, 8 investigations.
- Prosecution sanctions: 0.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FY 2025-26 (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5068, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Counts are not one cohort and do not prove prevalence.
**Mains hook:** Use as evidence of low conversion from complaint to sanction, with caution.
**Back:** *"Five-five-eight in, zero prosecutions out."*

### Flashcard ETH-5070 — Lokpal organic-link trap pair
**Front:** Which is a trap: "CVC works under Lok Pal guidance" or "Lok Pal replaces CVC"?
**Definition (learn):** The first is the ARC's organic link. The second is wrong (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Organic link: guidance, autonomy.
- Replacement: not proposed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.3.3.
**Links:** ETH-5060, ETH-5066.
**Trap:** Replacement reading.
**Mains hook:** Use guidance-not-replacement in coordination answers.
**Back:** *"Guidance, not replacement."*

### Flashcard ETH-5071 — Lokpal and the ARC's 2nd diagnosis
**Front:** Does the 2013 Act resolve ARC's multiplicity problem?
**Definition (learn):** Partly. It addresses the central level and high-level functionaries. State-level heterogeneity remains (✅ owner 20 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Central: addressed.
- State: largely unresolved.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 4.2.5.
**Links:** ETH-5051, ETH-5063, ETH-5073.
**Trap:** "Lokpal Act resolved all multiplicity" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** State the central/state split.
**Back:** *"Centre tidied, States still mixed."*

### Flashcard ETH-5072 — Lokpal proposal vs enacted Act: political economy
**Front:** Why did the enacted Lokpal broaden beyond the ARC proposal?
**Definition (learn):** Public mobilisation after 2011 and parliamentary negotiation pushed wider coverage (⚠️ owner 20 advanced analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- ARC: lean, high-independence.
- Act: comprehensive coverage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2011 movement.
**Links:** ETH-5056, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Presenting the change as purely technocratic.
**Mains hook:** Say design is political as well as technical.
**Back:** *"Streets shaped the statute."*

### Flashcard ETH-5073 — State Lokayukta heterogeneity
**Front:** Why does State-level heterogeneity matter?
**Definition (learn):** No uniform model was prescribed for State Lokayuktas. Composition, jurisdiction and independence still vary (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Variation: composition, jurisdiction, independence.
- Reform: common minimum safeguards.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.63.
**Links:** ETH-5063, ETH-5071.
**Trap:** Assuming the 2013 Act standardised States.
**Mains hook:** Propose common minimum safeguards through cooperative federalism.
**Back:** *"Many Lokayuktas, many rules."*

### Flashcard ETH-5074 — Institutions: the Polity cross-reference
**Front:** Where is full CVC, CBI and Lokpal constitutional detail covered?
**Definition (learn):** Polity advanced files 37 (CVC and CBI) and 38 (Lokpal and Lokayuktas) (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Polity 37: CVC and CBI.
- Polity 38: Lokpal and Lokayuktas.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner reference.
**Links:** ETH-5052, ETH-5057.
**Trap:** Using Ethics owner as the full constitutional source.
**Mains hook:** Cross-refer to Polity when precision is required.
**Back:** *"Ethics names them; Polity explains them."*

### Flashcard ETH-5075 — Ethics answer: the institutional-design thesis
**Front:** What thesis should an institutional-design answer state?
**Definition (learn):** Institutional architecture historically suffered from multiplicity and lack of coordination. A defensible response combines precise roles, lawful gates, secure hand-offs and independent adjudication. The ARC proposal must be distinguished from the 2013 Act (✅ owner 20).
**Breakdown:**
- Roles.
- Gates.
- Hand-offs.
- Adjudication.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5051, ETH-5067, ETH-5060.
**Trap:** Ignoring the ARC/Act distinction.
**Mains hook:** Use this four-part thesis for any 10- or 15-mark question.
**Back:** *"Roles, gates, hand-offs, adjudication."*

### GROUP — 21 Protecting Honest Officials and Vigilance Administration

### Flashcard ETH-5076 — Vigilance purpose: enhance, not reduce
**Front:** What is the stated purpose of vigilance administration?
**Definition (learn):** To enhance managerial efficiency and effectiveness, not to reduce them. Risk-taking is legitimate, and not every loss needs a vigilance inquiry (ARC 7.1; ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: enhance.
- Risk: legitimate.
- Loss: not automatically an inquiry.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.1.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5077.
**Trap:** Any pecuniary loss means vigilance inquiry: wrong.
**Mains hook:** Apply the purpose statement to a risk-taking case.
**Back:** *"Vigilance should enhance, not freeze."*

### Flashcard ETH-5077 — Vigilance angle
**Front:** What is a "vigilance angle"?
**Definition (learn):** The question whether a case involves suspected corrupt motive or misconduct, as opposed to a good-faith decision that went wrong (⚠️ owner 21 definition).
**Breakdown:**
- Test: motive and misconduct.
- Not: outcome alone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5076.
**Trap:** Equating a bad outcome with a vigilance angle.
**Mains hook:** Decide the vigilance angle first.
**Back:** *"Motive first, outcome second."*

### Flashcard ETH-5078 — Single-point directive (ARC 7.3)
**Front:** What was the single-point directive?
**Definition (learn):** Prior central government approval needed before investigating an officer of Joint Secretary rank or above (or equivalent in PSUs). It was an executive instruction, then DSPE s.6A (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Rank-based.
- Executive origin.
- Statutory form: s.6A.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.3–7.4 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** ETH-5016, ETH-5017, ETH-5018.
**Trap:** Describing it as current law.
**Mains hook:** Use it as the historical contrast for s.17A.
**Back:** *"Joint Secretary shield, now archived."*

### Flashcard ETH-5079 — Sanction and prior approval today
**Front:** What are the live safeguards for honest officials?
**Definition (learn):** s.19 PC Act (sanction before cognizance), s.17A PC Act (approval before enquiry), and s.197 CrPC or its BNSS equivalent for acts in official duty (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- s.17A: enquiry stage.
- s.19: cognizance stage.
- s.197 CrPC / BNSS: general official-duty protection.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner text; BNSS numbering to verify (⚠️).
**Links:** ETH-5013, ETH-5014, ETH-5016.
**Trap:** Mixing sanction stages.
**Mains hook:** Name the live gate for each stage.
**Back:** *"Seventeen-A before probe, nineteen before court."*

### Flashcard ETH-5080 — Section 197 CrPC / BNSS: official-duty protection
**Front:** What does s.197 CrPC (now carried into BNSS) protect?
**Definition (learn):** Prior sanction protection for public servants for acts done in discharge of official duty (✅ owner 21; ⚠️ BNSS section number to verify).
**Breakdown:**
- Protects official acts.
- Parallels PC Act sanction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only.
**Links:** ETH-5079.
**Trap:** Quoting the BNSS section number without verifying.
**Mains hook:** Use it as the general protection beside PC Act gates.
**Back:** *"Official act, official shield."*

### Flashcard ETH-5081 — Section 17A rank-neutral but conduct-specific
**Front:** What makes s.17A different from the old s.6A?
**Definition (learn):** Rank-neutral; applies to any public servant for a decision or recommendation in official functions (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: decision.
- Rank: irrelevant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018.
**Links:** ETH-5014, ETH-5016.
**Trap:** Assuming s.17A depends on rank.
**Mains hook:** Use the basis shift in the answer.
**Back:** *"Decision decides, not rank."*

### Flashcard ETH-5082 — POSH Act: Internal Committee composition and scope
**Front:** Which workplaces must form an Internal Committee under the POSH Act, 2013?
**Definition (learn):** Every workplace with ten or more employees must constitute an Internal Committee. Presiding officer: senior woman employee; at least two members with social-work or legal experience; one external member from a women's cause NGO (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold: 10 employees.
- Presiding officer: senior woman.
- Members: two experienced.
- External: one NGO member.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** ETH-5083, ETH-5084, ETH-5095.
**Trap:** Confusing IC with a criminal complaint forum.
**Mains hook:** Name the IC composition in any POSH answer.
**Back:** *"Ten staff, senior woman leads, outsider joins."*

### Flashcard ETH-5083 — POSH: complaint and inquiry timelines
**Front:** What timelines does the POSH Act set?
**Definition (learn):** Complaint within three months of the incident, extendable by three more months for sufficient reason. Inquiry ordinarily within 90 days. Action on report within 60 days (✅ owner 21, 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Complaint: 3 months, +3.
- Inquiry: 90 days.
- Action: 60 days.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above (⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5084.
**Trap:** Treating the 90 days as the complaint deadline.
**Mains hook:** Map the timeline to a case.
**Back:** *"Three, three, ninety, sixty."*

### Flashcard ETH-5084 — POSH IC vs criminal complaint
**Front:** Does an IC inquiry replace a criminal complaint?
**Definition (learn):** No. The IC is a workplace service-rule process. A criminal complaint is separate, and the complainant may pursue either or both (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- IC: service-rule finding; disciplinary action.
- Criminal: penal law.
- Parallel: allowed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5083.
**Trap:** "POSH complaint is the same as an FIR" is a trap.
**Mains hook:** Say both routes stay open.
**Back:** *"Internal finding, external charge, both open."*

### Flashcard ETH-5085 — Vishaka guidelines: the POSH ancestor
**Front:** Which judgment is the judicial ancestor of the POSH Act's IC architecture?
**Definition (learn):** *Vishaka & Ors v. State of Rajasthan* (1997) 6 SCC 241, which set binding guidelines including a complaints committee with a woman presiding officer and external member (✅ owner 21, 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Guidelines in absence of law.
- Committee with woman head.
- External member.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (1997) 6 SCC 241 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5086.
**Trap:** Saying Vishaka is legislation.
**Mains hook:** Use Vishaka as the origin of enforceable committee design.
**Back:** *"Vishaka wrote the committee; POSH made it law."*

### Flashcard ETH-5086 — Mechanism: employer duties under POSH
**Front:** What must an employer do under POSH s.19?
**Definition (learn):** Constitute and assist the IC; display the anti-harassment policy; ensure confidentiality; protect against victimisation; act on IC recommendations and treat harassment as misconduct (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Constitute and assist.
- Display policy.
- Confidentiality.
- Anti-retaliation.
- Act on report.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.19 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5087.
**Trap:** Treating the employer as a passive recipient.
**Mains hook:** List the five duties in any harassment answer.
**Back:** *"Build, display, guard, protect, act."*

### Flashcard ETH-5087 — Whistleblower ladder for honest officials
**Front:** What steps should an officer facing pressure follow?
**Definition (learn):** (a) record facts and preserve evidence; (b) raise internally through CVO or PIDPI or Lokpal; (c) rely on confidentiality; (d) external disclosure last; (e) resignation not first (✅ owner 21, 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Record.
- Internal.
- Confidential.
- External last.
- Resign last.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.9.
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5088.
**Trap:** Resignation as first move.
**Mains hook:** Apply the ladder step by step.
**Back:** *"Record, route, shield, last, resign."*

### Flashcard ETH-5088 — Implementation sequence for political pressure
**Front:** What six steps should an official follow in a politically pressed decision?
**Definition (learn):** (i) written file note; (ii) disclose and recuse on conflict; (iii) committee and published criteria for discretion; (iv) reasoned written dissent; (v) escalate if overridden; (vi) monitor residual risk (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Record.
- Recuse.
- Committee.
- Dissent.
- Escalate.
- Monitor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only.
**Links:** ETH-5087, ETH-5089, ETH-5049.
**Trap:** Silent compliance or silent refusal.
**Mains hook:** Walk through all six in a 20-mark case.
**Back:** *"File, recuse, committee, dissent, escalate, monitor."*

### Flashcard ETH-5089 — ARC 7.9(a): screening allegations
**Front:** What must a complaint pass before inquiry?
**Definition (learn):** Specificity, credibility and verifiability (ARC 7.9(a); ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Specific.
- Credible.
- Verifiable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5090, ETH-5091.
**Trap:** Opening inquiry on vague complaints.
**Mains hook:** Use the three tests to screen an anonymous complaint.
**Back:** *"Specific, credible, checkable: then inquire."*

### Flashcard ETH-5090 — ARC 7.9(b): secret preliminary verification
**Front:** Why is secret preliminary verification valuable?
**Definition (learn):** It protects the honest official if the allegation is false and protects the integrity of any open inquiry (ARC 7.9(b); ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Protects reputation.
- Preserves evidence.
- Allows parallel service continuity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5089, ETH-5049, ETH-5106.
**Trap:** Treating secrecy as concealment.
**Mains hook:** Use it in any tout network case.
**Back:** *"Secret check, public service."*

### Flashcard ETH-5091 — ARC 7.9(c)–(f): evaluation and prosecution standards
**Front:** What do ARC 7.9(c) to (f) require?
**Definition (learn):** Competent, honest, impartial evaluation; expert consultation; capacity-building; prosecution only where evidence is strong (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- (c)–(d): impartial evaluation.
- (e): capacity.
- (f): strong evidence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5089, ETH-5092.
**Trap:** Believing protection means minimal investigation.
**Mains hook:** Argue that protection and diligence co-exist.
**Back:** *"Strong evidence, not strong suspicion."*

### Flashcard ETH-5092 — ARC 7.6: over-suspicion lowers conviction
**Front:** What did ARC 7.6 say about over-suspicion?
**Definition (learn):** Investigators often assume a decision must be wrong for corruption, and treat "conspiracy" as easier than finding the actual wrongdoers. Result: low conviction, demoralised honest officials, dishonest ones going free (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Assumption 1: bad outcome implies corruption.
- Assumption 2: involve everyone.
- Result: worse outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.6.
**Links:** ETH-5093, ETH-5105.
**Trap:** "Stricter always helps" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Use the paradox to argue for precision.
**Back:** *"Suspect all, convict few."*

### Flashcard ETH-5093 — ARC 7.5: bad-faith complaints
**Front:** Who may file bad-faith complaints against honest officers?
**Definition (learn):** Dishonest subordinates facing discipline, or outsiders whose wrongful way the officer blocked (ARC 7.5; ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Subordinates.
- Outsiders.
- Screening must filter them.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5089.
**Trap:** Assuming every complainant is in good faith.
**Mains hook:** Use screening to filter bad-faith complaints.
**Back:** *"Disgruntled staff, blocked outsiders: screen them."*

### Flashcard ETH-5094 — ARC 7.8: unit to probe investigators
**Front:** What did ARC 7.8 recommend?
**Definition (learn):** A special unit reporting to the Lok Pal, with State equivalents, to investigate corruption or harassment by investigating agencies (✅ owner 21, 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Target: investigating agencies.
- Reporting: Lok Pal.
- Status: implementation not traced (📰 owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.8.
**Links:** ETH-5066, ETH-5095.
**Trap:** Presenting ARC 7.8 as in force.
**Mains hook:** Ask "who watches the watchmen".
**Back:** *"Watch the watchmen."*

### Flashcard ETH-5095 — ARC 7.4: safeguards called to serious question
**Front:** What did ARC 7.4 say about single-point directive and sanction requirements?
**Definition (learn):** They protect honest officials but have been called obstructive of investigating agencies. ARC calls for "due discrimination" in application, not removal (✅ owner 21, advanced 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Protection: yes.
- Obstruction: possible.
- Remedy: due discrimination.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.4.
**Links:** ETH-5016, ETH-5014.
**Trap:** "ARC wants safeguards abolished" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Argue for timely scrutiny, not abolition.
**Back:** *"Due discrimination, not abolition."*

### Flashcard ETH-5096 — Preventive vigilance measures
**Front:** Which measures reduce the opportunity for corruption?
**Definition (learn):** Rotation of sensitive seats; surprise checks; random or committee-based file allocation; process review; published timelines; scrutiny of officers of doubtful integrity (⚠️ owner 21 section 3; verify CVC circular).
**Breakdown:**
- Rotation.
- Surprise checks.
- Random allocation.
- Published timelines.
- Integrity scrutiny.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only, verify list.
**Links:** ETH-5097, ETH-5106.
**Trap:** Quoting the list as an official CVC list without verification.
**Mains hook:** Use preventive vigilance to remove the niche, not only the person.
**Back:** *"Rotate, surprise, randomise, publish, scrutinise."*

### Flashcard ETH-5097 — Residual-risk mitigation: punitive plus preventive
**Front:** Why do punitive and preventive vigilance both matter?
**Definition (learn):** Punitive action removes the current wrongdoer; preventive vigilance removes the niche the next one would fill (⚠️ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Punitive: current actor.
- Preventive: opportunity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5096, ETH-5106.
**Trap:** Stopping at punishment.
**Mains hook:** Use as closing element in a case study.
**Back:** *"Punish the holder, close the niche."*

### Flashcard ETH-5098 — Transfer industry: tenure recommendation
**Front:** What tenure did the Fifth Pay Commission recommend for postings?
**Definition (learn):** Minimum three to five years, with two to three years for especially sensitive posts, to curb the transfer industry (✅ owner 21, advanced 21; ARC 9.7–9.11).
**Breakdown:**
- General: 3–5 years.
- Sensitive: 2–3 years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC endorsed.
**Links:** ETH-5099, ETH-5100.
**Trap:** Mixing general and sensitive tenures.
**Mains hook:** Recommend published transfer policy with tenure and exceptions.
**Back:** *"Five for most, three for sensitive."*

### Flashcard ETH-5099 — Transfer industry: Robert Wade and N.N. Vohra
**Front:** What is the "transfer industry" and who documented it?
**Definition (learn):** Transfers used as politicians' control weapon over bureaucracy, creating a circuit of transactions. Robert Wade's Andhra Pradesh study and N.N. Vohra's characterisation (ARC 9.4–9.5; ✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Control weapon.
- Circuit of transactions.
- Documented by Wade, Vohra.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 9.4–9.5.
**Links:** ETH-5098, ETH-5100.
**Trap:** Ignoring transfers as harassment.
**Mains hook:** Cite Wade and the tenure remedy.
**Back:** *"Transfers as leash."*

### Flashcard ETH-5100 — Common Cause / Shourie PIL: no general transfer rules
**Front:** Did the Supreme Court frame general transfer-policy rules in the Common Cause/Shourie PIL?
**Definition (learn):** No. The Court said guidelines are settled and arbitrary cases can be challenged individually, leaving policy reform to the executive and legislature (ARC 9.6; ✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Court: declined general rules.
- Remedy: individual challenge.
- Reform: executive or legislative.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 9.6.
**Links:** ETH-5099, ETH-5101.
**Trap:** "SC has binding transfer rules" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** State the judicial limit.
**Back:** *"Court declined; policy stays political."*

### Flashcard ETH-5101 — Karnataka Lokayukta's committee model
**Front:** What committee model did the Karnataka Lokayukta propose for transfers?
**Definition (learn):** A committee of the Chief Secretary, the next senior-most Secretary and the relevant Secretary, working transparently, with Ministry oversight limited to complaints (ARC 9.12; ✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Members: three officials.
- Oversight: complaints only.
- Early template for Civil Services Board.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 9.12.
**Links:** ETH-5100, ETH-5102.
**Trap:** Presenting it as enacted law.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a precedent for committee transfers.
**Back:** *"Three secretaries decide, the minister reviews complaints."*

### Flashcard ETH-5102 — Civil Services Board and Public Services Bill 2006
**Front:** What did ARC 9.13 and 9.14 propose?
**Definition (learn):** A Civil Services Board (9.13, National Commission to Review the Working of the Constitution proposal) and a Central Public Services Authority with advisory powers under the Draft Public Services Bill, 2006 (ARC 9.14; ✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- CSB: transfer correctives.
- CPSA: advisory.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5101, ETH-5098.
**Trap:** Presenting the 2006 draft as law.
**Mains hook:** Name the CSB as a corrective to transfer industry.
**Back:** *"Board for postings, authority for advice."*

### Flashcard ETH-5103 — Soft harassment through transfer
**Front:** How can an honest official face harassment without formal misconduct?
**Definition (learn):** Punitive reassignment without ground or tenure protection. The bona fides test and s.17A/19 do not reach it (⚠️ owner 21 advanced analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: transfer.
- Gap: formal safeguards miss it.
- Fix: published transfer policy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5098, ETH-5100.
**Trap:** Thinking vigilance safeguards cover all harassment.
**Mains hook:** Name transfer as a separate harassment channel.
**Back:** *"Transfer hurts without a charge."*

### Flashcard ETH-5104 — Two structural failure modes
**Front:** What two opposite failure modes does ARC Chapter 7 identify?
**Definition (learn):** Over-suspicion, which demoralises honest officials and lowers conviction (7.6–7.7). Under-suspicion, where safeguards shield corrupt officials or delay investigation (7.4) (✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Failure 1: over-suspicion.
- Failure 2: safeguard misuse.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.4, 7.6–7.7.
**Links:** ETH-5092, ETH-5095.
**Trap:** One-directional "get tougher" or "protect officials" answers.
**Mains hook:** Design for both failure modes.
**Back:** *"Too hot burns the honest, too cold shields the crook."*

### Flashcard ETH-5105 — Moral luck and the bona fides test
**Front:** How does moral luck connect to the bona fides test?
**Definition (learn):** Judging decision process, not outcome, guards against moral-luck bias (Nagel and Williams taxonomy; owner refers to 08 Section 12A; ⚠️ owner analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Process versus outcome.
- Resultant luck.
- Circumstantial luck.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ETH-5040 cross-ref.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5106.
**Trap:** Judging by result.
**Mains hook:** Cite moral luck as the philosophical basis for the bona fides test.
**Back:** *"Judge the path, not the weather."*

### Flashcard ETH-5106 — Sanction misuse: fixed timelines
**Front:** What reform prevents sanction-requirement misuse?
**Definition (learn):** Fixed decision timelines with escalation or default if lapsed (⚠️ owner 21 Mains angles).
**Breakdown:**
- Timelines.
- Escalation.
- Default.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.17A and s.19 timelines.
**Links:** ETH-5045, ETH-5095.
**Trap:** Assuming lapse means approval.
**Mains hook:** Recommend escalation not deemed approval.
**Back:** *"Clock with escalation."*

### Flashcard ETH-5107 — PYQ: bona fide mistakes (2019 GS-IV Q8)
**Front:** What does the 2019 GS-IV Q8 ask?
**Definition (learn):** Civil servants implicated and imprisoned for bona fide mistakes; how this affects functioning and measures to protect honest officers. Case study, 20 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 21 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: effect on functioning.
- Demand: protection measures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 GS-IV Q8.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5076.
**Trap:** Treating it as an essay.
**Mains hook:** Use bona fides plus safeguards.
**Back:** *"Good faith, protected process."*

### Flashcard ETH-5108 — PYQ: municipal commissioner and POSH threat (2020 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What does the 2020 GS-IV Q10 combine?
**Definition (learn):** Mall collapse, corruption nexus, minister relative POSH threat, ethical issues and course (✅ owner 21 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Corruption nexus.
- POSH threat.
- Choose course.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5014.
**Trap:** Missing the POSH strand.
**Mains hook:** Address both strands.
**Back:** *"Collapse, nexus, threat, choose."*

### Flashcard ETH-5109 — PYQ: whistleblower measures (2022 GS-IV Q6a)
**Front:** What does the 2022 GS-IV Q6(a) ask?
**Definition (learn):** Policy measures to strengthen whistle-blower protection mechanisms (suggest). 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 21 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Suggest measures.
- 10 marks.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-IV Q6(a).
**Links:** ETH-5028, ETH-5043.
**Trap:** Generic answer without statute status.
**Mains hook:** Name commencement and PIDPI.
**Back:** *"Commence, route, protect."*

### Flashcard ETH-5110 — PYQ: Sunil and sand mining (2021 GS-IV Q7)
**Front:** What does the 2021 GS-IV Q7 ask?
**Definition (learn):** Sunil combats sand mining mafia with violence threats: options, evaluation and best course (✅ owner 21 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Options.
- Evaluate.
- Best course.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 GS-IV Q7.
**Links:** ETH-5087, ETH-5088.
**Trap:** No option evaluation.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder.
**Back:** *"Options, evaluate, choose."*

### Flashcard ETH-5111 — PYQ: honest IAS officer at SRTC (2023 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What does the 2023 GS-IV Q10 ask?
**Definition (learn):** Honest IAS officer, SRTC MD, corrupt Chairman and Opposition pressure (✅ owner 21 PYQ integration; full answer in card ETH-5135).
**Breakdown:**
- Pressure from both sides.
- Ethical path.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5135, ETH-5088.
**Trap:** Acting on Opposition's terms.
**Mains hook:** Use neutrality and written record.
**Back:** *"Two pressures, one record."*

### Flashcard ETH-5112 — Trap: pecuniary loss equals vigilance
**Front:** Does any pecuniary loss warrant vigilance inquiry?
**Definition (learn):** No. ARC 7.1 rejects this. The bona fides test decides (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Loss alone: not enough.
- Test: prudence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.1.
**Links:** ETH-5076, ETH-5040.
**Trap:** Trap option "any loss".
**Mains hook:** Apply bona fides.
**Back:** *"Loss is not guilt."*

### Flashcard ETH-5113 — Trap: safeguards have no downside
**Front:** Do sanction and single-directive safeguards have downsides?
**Definition (learn):** Yes. ARC 7.4 says they are called to serious question as shields or delays (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Benefit: protection.
- Cost: delay or shielding.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.4.
**Links:** ETH-5095.
**Trap:** Presenting safeguards as costless.
**Mains hook:** Name both sides.
**Back:** *"Shield has a shadow."*

### Flashcard ETH-5114 — Trap: resignation as first response
**Front:** Is resignation the honest official's correct first response to pressure?
**Definition (learn):** No. File-note, disclose, dissent, escalate first. Resignation is last (✅ owner 21, 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Internal steps first.
- Resignation after exhaustion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5088, ETH-5126.
**Trap:** "Resign in protest" as reflex.
**Mains hook:** Run the resignation test.
**Back:** *"Exhaust, then exit."*

### Flashcard ETH-5115 — Trap: POSH complaint equals criminal complaint
**Front:** Is a POSH complaint the same as a criminal complaint?
**Definition (learn):** No. Separate forums, possibly parallel (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- IC: workplace.
- Criminal: penal.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5084.
**Trap:** Equating them.
**Mains hook:** Both routes.
**Back:** *"Two forums, one harm."*

### Flashcard ETH-5116 — Trap: protecting honest officials means minimal investigation
**Front:** Does protecting honest officials mean minimising all investigation?
**Definition (learn):** No. ARC 7.9(f): only those against whom evidence is strong (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Protection plus evidence.
- Not inaction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.9(f).
**Links:** ETH-5091.
**Trap:** Blanket non-investigation.
**Mains hook:** Argue coexistence.
**Back:** *"Evidence decides who falls."*

### Flashcard ETH-5117 — Trap: over-suspicion improves outcomes
**Front:** Does stricter suspicion of every decision improve anti-corruption outcomes?
**Definition (learn):** No. ARC 7.6 shows over-suspicion correlates with lower conviction (✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Broad suspicion: low yield.
- Precision: effective.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.6.
**Links:** ETH-5092.
**Trap:** "Stricter is better".
**Mains hook:** Precision over breadth.
**Back:** *"Precision beats breadth."*

### Flashcard ETH-5118 — Trap: SC binding transfer rules
**Front:** Has the Supreme Court laid binding transfer-policy rules?
**Definition (learn):** No. It declined in the Common Cause/Shourie PIL (✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Declined.
- Reform executive.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 9.6.
**Links:** ETH-5100.
**Trap:** Saying binding rules exist.
**Mains hook:** State judicial limit.
**Back:** *"Court declined, executive owns it."*

### Flashcard ETH-5119 — Trap: ARC wants sanction requirements removed
**Front:** Does ARC Chapter 7 recommend weakening sanction requirements to speed prosecution?
**Definition (learn):** No. It calls for due discrimination (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Not removal.
- Timely scrutiny.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.4.
**Links:** ETH-5095.
**Trap:** Misreading ARC.
**Mains hook:** Due discrimination.
**Back:** *"Discriminate, don't delete."*

### Flashcard ETH-5120 — Trap: dismantling network requires suspending whole section
**Front:** Must a corrupt network inside an office be dismantled by suspending the whole section first?
**Definition (learn):** No. Secret verification allows parallel cleanup and continuity (ARC 7.9(b); owner 21 section 6).
**Breakdown:**
- Parallel tracks.
- Service continues.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.9(b).
**Links:** ETH-5090, ETH-5126.
**Trap:** Section-wide suspension.
**Mains hook:** Two-track.
**Back:** *"Clean without closing."*

### Flashcard ETH-5121 — Trap: every failure is corruption
**Front:** Is every failure to achieve a good outcome misconduct?
**Definition (learn):** No. Bona fides separates honest failure from wilful non-performance (✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Honest failure: no angle.
- Wilful: misconduct.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.1.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5024.
**Trap:** Conflation.
**Mains hook:** Test motive.
**Back:** *"Failure is not felony."*

### Flashcard ETH-5122 — Honest official's bank-loan scenario
**Front:** Should a bank manager's good-faith loan that later defaults face vigilance inquiry?
**Definition (learn):** Not automatically. Bona fides test clears genuine commercial decisions (⚠️ owner 21 illustration).
**Breakdown:**
- Due diligence: yes.
- Default: outcome.
- Test: process.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner illustration.
**Links:** ETH-5040, ETH-5076.
**Trap:** Judging by default.
**Mains hook:** Apply the test.
**Back:** *"Due diligence, honest default, no inquiry."*

### Flashcard ETH-5123 — Improper tender direction: applied sequence
**Front:** How should an officer handle a political superior's direction to favour a bidder?
**Definition (learn):** File-note; disclose upward; insist on published criteria; reasoned dissent; escalate if overridden (⚠️ owner 21 illustration).
**Breakdown:**
- Record.
- Disclose.
- Criteria.
- Dissent.
- Escalate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5088.
**Trap:** Silent compliance or instant resignation.
**Mains hook:** Full sequence.
**Back:** *"Record, disclose, criteria, dissent, escalate."*

### Flashcard ETH-5124 — Vishaka and the honest employee
**Front:** How does Vishaka protect a complainant from retaliation?
**Definition (learn):** Confidentiality and anti-retaliation duties (POSH s.19 summary; ✅ owner 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Retaliation: barred.
- Confidentiality: required.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5085, ETH-5086.
**Trap:** Ignoring retaliation.
**Mains hook:** Name protections.
**Back:** *"Complainant shielded, identity kept."*

### Flashcard ETH-5125 — Shared-identity officers and neutrality
**Front:** Does an officer's community identity alone make them partisan?
**Definition (learn):** Not by itself. Conduct decides (⚠️ owner 22 Sec. 14.20 point).
**Breakdown:**
- Identity: not enough.
- Conduct: test.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner reference.
**Links:** ETH-5135, ETH-5182.
**Trap:** Collective punishment by identity.
**Mains hook:** Conduct-based reconstitution.
**Back:** *"Judge the act, not the surname."*

### Flashcard ETH-5126 — Resignation last-resort test
**Front:** What three questions test whether resignation is justified?
**Definition (learn):** (1) Have all lawful internal options been exhausted? (2) Does resigning improve the public outcome? (3) Is staying with recorded dissent and refusal to participate more effective? (✅ owner 22 Sec. 10).
**Breakdown:**
- Exhaust.
- Outcome.
- Stay alternative.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5114, ETH-5087.
**Trap:** Emotional escape.
**Mains hook:** Apply the three tests.
**Back:** *"Exhausted, useful, better inside?"*

### Flashcard ETH-5127 — Advanced: current anchor for Section 17A
**Front:** What current application anchors Section 17A's advanced analysis?
**Definition (learn):** 2026 INSC 55 and the 2014 s.6A strike-down. Rank-neutral successor (📰 ✅ owner 21 advanced; ⚠️ verify).
**Breakdown:**
- 2014: rank out.
- 2018: decision in.
- 2026: pending.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5015, ETH-5017.
**Trap:** Treating 17A as settled.
**Mains hook:** State pending status.
**Back:** *"Rank out, decision in, validity pending."*

### Flashcard ETH-5128 — Advanced: investigative paradox
**Front:** Why can over-suspicion produce lower conviction rates?
**Definition (learn):** It diverts investigative capacity to marginal cases and widens suspicion, so serious cases get less focus (⚠️ owner 21 advanced analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Resource misallocation.
- Demoralisation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.6.
**Links:** ETH-5092, ETH-5117.
**Trap:** Ignoring capacity.
**Mains hook:** Risk-graded scrutiny.
**Back:** *"Broad net, thin catch."*

### Flashcard ETH-5129 — Advanced: Lokpal-linked unit and recursive accountability
**Front:** What problem does a unit investigating investigators raise?
**Definition (learn):** A "who watches the watchmen" recursion needing its own independence safeguards (⚠️ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Watchmen.
- Independence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ARC 7.8.
**Links:** ETH-5094.
**Trap:** Assuming the unit is automatically independent.
**Mains hook:** Build independence.
**Back:** *"Watchmen need watching."*

### Flashcard ETH-5130 — Advanced: tenure exceptions
**Front:** Why do fixed tenure rules need an exception clause?
**Definition (learn):** Continued proximity to a live investigation may need urgent reassignment (⚠️ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Tenure default.
- Justified exception.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5098.
**Trap:** Rigid tenure.
**Mains hook:** Narrow exception clause.
**Back:** *"Tenure with a narrow exit."*

### Flashcard ETH-5131 — Advanced: secret verification vs transparency
**Front:** What is the risk of secret preliminary verification?
**Definition (learn):** Indefinite shelving of credible complaints without external check (⚠️ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Benefit: reputation.
- Risk: opacity.
- Check: external review.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5090.
**Trap:** Secrecy as shelving.
**Mains hook:** External check.
**Back:** *"Secret, not shelved."*

### Flashcard ETH-5132 — Advanced: Lokayukta and CVC transfer checks
**Front:** Which transfer-policy correctives does the owner list?
**Definition (learn):** Published transfer policies, minimum tenure, reasons for premature transfer and appeal, no transfer to bypass discipline (ARC 9.7–9.11; ✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Published.
- Tenure.
- Reasons and appeal.
- No bypass.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** ETH-5098.
**Trap:** Missing the anti-bypass rule.
**Mains hook:** List correctives.
**Back:** *"Publish, tenure, reason, no bypass."*

### Flashcard ETH-5133 — Advanced: 17A and 19 current-anchor table
**Front:** What is the advanced use of the 17A/19 anchor?
**Definition (learn):** Shows the basis of protection moved from rank to decision nature (✅ owner 21 advanced; ⚠️ current).
**Breakdown:**
- Rank to decision.
- Calibration question.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5081.
**Trap:** Ignoring calibration.
**Mains hook:** Ask if balance tilted.
**Back:** *"From rank to decision."*

### Flashcard ETH-5134 — PYQ: vigilance balance Mains (15 marks)
**Front:** How should a 15-mark vigilance-balance answer be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Name both failure modes, apply bona fides, cite screening and secret verification, propose calibrated reforms (✅ owner 21 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Modes.
