- Test.
- Screening.
- Reform.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner framework.
**Links:** ETH-5104, ETH-5040, ETH-5089.
**Trap:** One-sided answer.
**Mains hook:** Use the frame.
**Back:** *"Two modes, one test, calibrated reform."*

### Flashcard ETH-5135 — PYQ: honest IAS officer and SRTC (2023 GS-IV Q10) — answer route
**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2023 GS-IV Q10 SRTC case?
**Definition (learn):** Document irregularities; written clarification; escalate through audit and vigilance; decline partisan channel; maintain service (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.15).
**Breakdown:**
- Record.
- Clarify.
- Escalate.
- Decline partisan.
- Continue.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5111, ETH-5170.
**Trap:** Partisan leak.
**Mains hook:** Full route.
**Back:** *"Record, escalate, stay neutral, keep serving."*

### GROUP — 22 Case-Study Method and Answer Architecture

### Flashcard ETH-5136 — Eight-element case-study architecture
**Front:** What are the eight elements of a GS-IV case-study answer?
**Definition (learn):** (1) Facts; (2) Stakeholders; (3) Constraints; (4) Options (3–4 realistic); (5) Ethical evaluation; (6) Decision; (7) Implementation and communication; (8) Residual-risk mitigation (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Facts.
- Stakeholders.
- Constraints.
- Options.
- Ethics.
- Decision.
- Implementation.
- Residual risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Official GS-IV Section B format (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** ETH-5137–ETH-5143, ETH-5147.
**Trap:** Narrative essay instead of structure.
**Mains hook:** Use all eight in every case.
**Back:** *"Eight steps: facts to residual."*

### Flashcard ETH-5137 — Element 1: facts versus assumptions
**Front:** What must Element 1 separate?
**Definition (learn):** Verified facts given in the case versus assumptions requiring verification (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Given facts.
- Assumptions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Presenting assumptions as facts.
**Mains hook:** Two columns.
**Back:** *"Given, not assumed."*

### Flashcard ETH-5138 — Element 2: stakeholders including silent ones
**Front:** Who counts as a stakeholder?
**Definition (learn):** Direct and indirect or silent parties, including future generations, absent parties and the institution's credibility (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Direct.
- Silent.
- Future.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136, ETH-5139.
**Trap:** Ignoring silent parties.
**Mains hook:** Name one silent stakeholder.
**Back:** *"Include the absent and the future."*

### Flashcard ETH-5139 — Element 3: hard constraints
**Front:** What constraints bound a case-study decision?
**Definition (learn):** Legal and delegation limits, time, information, resources, jurisdiction and capacity. An option violating a hard constraint is not an option (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal.
- Time.
- Information.
- Resource.
- Jurisdiction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136, ETH-5140.
**Trap:** Proposing options the protagonist cannot execute.
**Mains hook:** Name the binding constraint.
**Back:** *"Name the wall before the door."*

### Flashcard ETH-5140 — Element 4: options
**Front:** How many options should be generated, and what must each carry?
**Definition (learn):** At least three realistic options, including hybrid or sequenced ones, each with one concrete pro and one con (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Count: 3+.
- Pro and con for each.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Binary options only.
**Mains hook:** Include a sequenced option.
**Back:** *"Three roads, each with a cost."*

### Flashcard ETH-5141 — Element 5: six-check framework
**Front:** What are the six checks?
**Definition (learn):** Conflict of interest; legality; public interest; proportionality; transparency; compassion plus long-term consequence (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- COI.
- Legality.
- Public interest.
- Proportionality.
- Transparency.
- Compassion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Synthesis heuristic, not official (⚠️ owner 22 advanced).
**Links:** ETH-5142, ETH-5143.
**Trap:** Treating as official UPSC list.
**Mains hook:** Name the checks.
**Back:** *"Conflict, law, public, proportion, openness, care."*

### Flashcard ETH-5142 — Hard thresholds versus weighted checks
**Front:** Which checks are hard thresholds and which are weighted?
**Definition (learn):** Legality and conflict of interest are hard thresholds that eliminate options. Public interest, proportionality, transparency and compassion are weighted (✅ owner 22 Sec. 3B).
**Breakdown:**
- Hard: legality, COI.
- Weighted: other four.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5141, ETH-5143.
**Trap:** Treating all six equally.
**Mains hook:** State which weighted check decided.
**Back:** *"Two walls, four weights."*

### Flashcard ETH-5143 — Exam technique: sequencing the checks
**Front:** In what order should checks be applied?
**Definition (learn):** Hard thresholds first, then weighted checks on survivors, then state the decisive weighted check (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Step 1: eliminate.
- Step 2: weigh.
- Step 3: decide.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5142.
**Trap:** Random order.
**Mains hook:** Use the sequence explicitly.
**Back:** *"Eliminate, weigh, decide."*

### Flashcard ETH-5144 — Decision ledger: eight steps
**Front:** What are the eight steps of the GS-IV decision ledger?
**Definition (learn):** Fact/value separation; stakeholders; ethical issues; legal and constitutional floor; legality versus ethics; options and tests; reasoned choice; implementation and prevention (✅ owner 22 Sec. 2).
**Breakdown:**
- Eight steps.
- Written before prose.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Skipping the ledger.
**Mains hook:** Use ledger to audit the answer.
**Back:** *"Ledger before prose."*

### Flashcard ETH-5145 — Conflict of interest: disclose, recuse, refer, publish
**Front:** What is the four-step conflict-of-interest sequence?
**Definition (learn):** Disclose in writing on file; recuse from the decision; route to an untainted authority; publish or apply criteria in advance (✅ owner 22 Sec. 4).
**Breakdown:**
- Disclose.
- Recuse.
- Route.
- Publish.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5141, ETH-5146, ETH-5151.
**Trap:** "Family member is trustworthy" as mitigation.
**Mains hook:** Apply in procurement cases.
**Back:** *"Disclose, recuse, route, publish."*

### Flashcard ETH-5146 — Nested-dilemma decomposition
**Front:** How should a multi-dilemma case be handled?
**Definition (learn):** Identify analytically separable sub-dilemmas; solve each with its own check; integrate into one recommendation (✅ owner 22 Sec. 5).
**Breakdown:**
- Identify.
- Solve.
- Integrate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5147, ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Over-decomposing simple cases.
**Mains hook:** Decompose only what is separable.
**Back:** *"Split, solve, stitch."*

### Flashcard ETH-5147 — Steelmanning the counter-argument
**Front:** What is steelmanning in a case answer?
**Definition (learn):** State the strongest objection to the chosen decision, then either explain why it fails or change the decision to meet it (✅ owner 22 Sec. 6).
**Breakdown:**
- Strongest, not weak.
- Concise.
- Address.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5146, ETH-5148.
**Trap:** Relitigating the whole case.
**Mains hook:** Two or three lines.
**Back:** *"Strongest objection, one answer."*

### Flashcard ETH-5148 — Residual-risk closing formula
**Front:** What three parts must a closing residual-risk statement have?
**Definition (learn):** (1) name the residual risk; (2) state the monitoring or review mechanism; (3) name the accountable office (✅ owner 22 Sec. 7).
**Breakdown:**
- Risk.
- Mechanism.
- Owner.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136, ETH-5147.
**Trap:** "It will be monitored" in passive voice.
**Mains hook:** End every answer this way.
**Back:** *"Name the risk, the watch, the watcher."*

### Flashcard ETH-5149 — Written-order response to oral pressure
**Front:** How should an official respond to an oral instruction to bend a decision?
**Definition (learn):** Convert pressure to a written record: file note seeking written direction; state reasoned position; refer up if persists; continue duty per own judgment (✅ owner 22 Sec. 8).
**Breakdown:**
- Record.
- Position.
- Refer.
- Continue.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5088, ETH-5150.
**Trap:** Verbal refusal only.
**Mains hook:** Use written order as standard response.
**Back:** *"Oral pressure, written answer."*

### Flashcard ETH-5150 — Whistleblowing ladder for compromised channels
**Front:** What is the four-rung ladder when the internal channel is compromised?
**Definition (learn):** (1) internal reporting on file; (2) escalation to independent authority; (3) statutory or lawful external channel; (4) public disclosure last. Skip a compromised rung (✅ owner 22 Sec. 9).
**Breakdown:**
- Internal.
- Escalation.
- Statutory.
- Public last.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5025, ETH-5087, ETH-5149.
**Trap:** Staying silent when superior is compromised.
**Mains hook:** Skip rung, not silence.
**Back:** *"Skip the rotten rung, not the duty."*

### Flashcard ETH-5151 — Triage and scarce-resource allocation method
**Front:** What four steps govern scarce-resource allocation?
**Definition (learn):** Publish criteria in advance; state the ethical trade-off explicitly; keep a record of criteria and applier; review and adjust (✅ owner 22 Sec. 11).
**Breakdown:**
- Publish.
- Trade-off.
- Record.
- Review.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5141, ETH-5173.
**Trap:** Ad hoc allocation.
**Mains hook:** Name the principle prioritised.
**Back:** *"Publish, weigh, record, revise."*

### Flashcard ETH-5152 — Mission Karmayogi case-based learning
**Front:** How does Mission Karmayogi link to case-study method?
**Definition (learn):** Mission Karmayogi (Cabinet approval 2 September 2020) uses case-based and simulation learning on iGOT-Karmayogi (📰 ✅ owner 22; ⚠️ verify the named module).
**Breakdown:**
- Programme: capacity building.
- Method: case and simulation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2 September 2020 (⚠️ verify).
**Links:** ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Presenting a module as mandatory without verification.
**Mains hook:** Practice the architecture for competency learning.
**Back:** *"Karmayogi trains judgment, not only memory."*

### Flashcard ETH-5153 — Case 14.1: GFR-type expenditure splitting (2025 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What is the Rajesh culvert case and its correct answer?
**Definition (learn):** Estimate ₹42 lakh beyond delegated ₹25 lakh; contractor offers two ₹21 lakh orders. Split is sanction evasion. Correct: sanction only the separate urgent stabilisation within delegation, refer the balance upward (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (a): split, rejected.
- Option (c): decision.
- Caution: works, not goods; GFR Rule 157 covers goods piecemeal purchase (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹42 lakh; ₹25 lakh; two ₹21 lakh orders (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5144, ETH-5154.
**Trap:** Citing GFR Rule 157 verbatim for works.
**Mains hook:** Legality as hard threshold.
**Back:** *"Forty-two split into two-ones: sanction evasion."*

### Flashcard ETH-5154 — Case 14.2: pharma batch whistleblowing (illustrative)
**Front:** What is the illustrative pharma case's decision?
**Definition (learn):** Meera, QA officer, batch failed stability; manager orders re-run. Decision: written report with evidence to independent quality council, then regulator (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.2; illustrative, not an exam case).
**Breakdown:**
- Options: comply, silent, skip rung, resign.
- Decision: skip rung.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Illustrative.
**Links:** ETH-5150, ETH-5087.
**Trap:** Silent compliance.
**Mains hook:** Skip the compromised rung.
**Back:** *"Fail is fail; report to whom they cannot silence."*

### Flashcard ETH-5155 — Case 14.3: rumour-driven communal tension (illustrative)
**Front:** What is the decision in the rumour and procession case?
**Definition (learn):** Verify video; engage both communities; permit scaled-down rerouted assembly; written time-bound restriction (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.3; illustrative).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c): decision.
- Legal: narrowly tailored powers.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Illustrative.
**Links:** ETH-5141, ETH-5170.
**Trap:** Blanket indefinite restriction.
**Mains hook:** Least restrictive lawful measure.
**Back:** *"Verify, dialogue, reroute, write it down."*

### Flashcard ETH-5156 — Case 14.4: Dr Srinivasan, consent and data integrity (2024 GS-IV Q12)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Dr Srinivasan case?
**Definition (learn):** Report both consent and data-integrity lapses to IEC; propose ICMR-compliant re-consent; reinstate excluded data for IEC decision (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Constraint: ICMR 2017 guidelines.
- Option (c): decision.
- Untainted authority: IEC.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ICMR National Ethical Guidelines 2017 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5157, ETH-5144.
**Trap:** Unilateral halt bypassing IEC.
**Mains hook:** IEC as untainted authority.
**Back:** *"Report to the IEC, reconsent, reinstate, let them decide."*

### Flashcard ETH-5157 — Case 14.5: Ashok and cross-border influx (2025 GS-IV Q12)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Ashok border-influx case?
**Definition (learn):** Separate the armed individuals for security screening under ordinary law; give civilians humanitarian shelter and registration; no uniform push-back or open entry (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.5).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c): decision.
- Civilians: shelter and register.
- Armed: disarm and refer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** India not party to 1951 Convention or 1967 Protocol (✅ owner); Mohammad Salimullah interim order 8 April 2021 (✅ owner, interim); NHRC v Arunachal (1996) final (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5158, ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Refugee status automatic; uniform deportation.
**Mains hook:** Separate by risk and status.
**Back:** *"Separate, shelter, screen, register."*

### Flashcard ETH-5158 — India's refugee law position (for Ashok)
**Front:** What is India's legal position on refugees, per the owner?
**Definition (learn):** No dedicated refugee statute; not party to 1951 Convention or 1967 Protocol; Foreigners Act governs status; Articles 14 and 21 apply to every person (✅ owner 22; ⚠️ verify current status).
**Breakdown:**
- No refugee law.
- Foreigners Act.
- Articles 14, 21.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5157.
**Trap:** Saying India has refugee law.
**Mains hook:** Use Article 21 for everyone on soil.
**Back:** *"No refugee statute, full dignity under Article 21."*

### Flashcard ETH-5159 — Case 14.6: POSH complaint with biased presiding officer (illustrative)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Anjali POSH case?
**Definition (learn):** File the statutory complaint and flag the presiding officer's conflict in writing for recusal or reconstitution (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.6; illustrative).
**Breakdown:**
- Constraint: 3 months, 90 days, 60 days.
- Decision: (c).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** POSH Act (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5082, ETH-5145.
**Trap:** Informal settlement to protect confirmation.
**Mains hook:** Conflict sequence applies to IC.
**Back:** *"File, flag the bias, reconstitute."*

### Flashcard ETH-5160 — Case 14.7: Sneha, conflict-of-interest procurement (2024 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Sneha case?
**Definition (learn):** Disclose in writing; recuse; alternate evaluator against published criteria; communicate on objective basis (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.7).
**Breakdown:**
- Hard threshold COI.
- Option (c).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5145, ETH-5161.
**Trap:** Trusting one's own objectivity.
**Mains hook:** Process legitimacy, not intent.
**Back:** *"Own bid, own recusal."*

### Flashcard ETH-5161 — Case 14.8: forest diversion for housing (2025 GS-IV Q8)
**Front:** What is the decision in the forest-housing case?
**Definition (learn):** Compare non-forest, brownfield and in-situ options; any forest diversion subject to lawful independent assessment (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.8).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Time-bound alternatives matrix.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q8.
**Links:** ETH-5141.
**Trap:** Open-ended study or immediate bypass.
**Mains hook:** Least damaging feasible route.
**Back:** *"Alternatives first, diversion last."*

### Flashcard ETH-5162 — Case 14.9: ABC AI emissions (2024 GS-IV Q7)
**Front:** What is the decision in the AI and emissions case?
**Definition (learn):** Retain innovation with time-bound, independently verifiable efficiency and renewable milestones; reject continue-as-is, reject halt, reject offset-only (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.9).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Board accountability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 48% rise in emissions 2023 vs 2019 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5147.
**Trap:** Offsets without demand reduction.
**Mains hook:** Steelman competition.
**Back:** *"Verify the pledge, meter the progress."*

### Flashcard ETH-5163 — Case 14.10: disaster personal-duty cross-reference
**Front:** Which case family does 14.10 cross-reference?
**Definition (learn):** Personal-duty versus professional-duty tension in disaster cases, anchored on DC Vijay (Sec. 13), with variants 2019 GS-IV Q7 and 2020 GS-IV Q12 (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Anchor: 2025 Q7.
- Variants: 2019 Q7, 2020 Q12.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5136.
**Trap:** Treating variants as separate methods.
**Mains hook:** Same eight-element sequence.
**Back:** *"One duty, many floods."*

### Flashcard ETH-5164 — Case 14.11: Rajesh Kumar NHS welfare re-appropriation (2020 GS-IV Q7)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Rajesh Kumar case, and is resignation evaluated?
**Definition (learn):** Written reasoned objection; seek written direction; escalate with welfare-impact analysis; resignation reserved (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.11).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (b): resign immediately, premature.
- Option (c): decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 GS-IV Q7.
**Links:** ETH-5126, ETH-5149.
**Trap:** Immediate resignation.
**Mains hook:** Resignation test.
**Back:** *"Object on file, escalate, resign only if forced to sign."*

### Flashcard ETH-5165 — Case 14.12: Priya, custodial excess (D.K. Basu)
**Front:** What must a station house officer do after custodial excess?
**Definition (learn):** Immediate medical examination; preserve evidence; report to superiors and magistrate; independent departmental inquiry; calm communication (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.12).
**Breakdown:**
- Medical.
- Evidence.
- Report.
- Inquiry.
- Communication.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** D.K. Basu v State of West Bengal (1997) 1 SCC 416 (✅ owner); BNSS numbering to verify.
**Links:** ETH-5166.
**Trap:** Accepting officers' account or presuming guilt.
**Mains hook:** Procedural obligation regardless of outcome.
**Back:** *"Heal, preserve, report, inquire."*

### Flashcard ETH-5166 — D.K. Basu safeguards
**Front:** Which custodial safeguards does D.K. Basu require?
**Definition (learn):** Arrest memo attested by a witness and countersigned; informing a friend or relative; medical examination at arrest and about every 48 hours; entries in diary; information to magistrate (✅ owner 22; ⚠️ BNSS renumbering to verify).
**Breakdown:**
- Memo.
- Relative notified.
- Medical at 48-hour intervals.
- Diary.
- Magistrate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (1997) 1 SCC 416; decided 18 December 1996 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5165.
**Trap:** Citing old CrPC numbers.
**Mains hook:** Name the safeguards.
**Back:** *"Memo, relative, doctor, diary, magistrate."*

### Flashcard ETH-5167 — Case 14.13: Meera, welfare data for policing (Puttaswamy, DPDP)
**Front:** What is the decision in the data-sharing case?
**Definition (learn):** Insist on a specific written competent-authority order; minimum data; log disclosure; record political pressure in writing (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.13; illustrative).
**Breakdown:**
- Puttaswamy proportionality.
- DPDP Act 2023 purpose limitation.
- Option (c).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (2017) 10 SCC 1 (✅ owner); DPDP Act 2023 (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5168, ETH-5149.
**Trap:** Bulk sharing on urgency.
**Mains hook:** Expedite lawful route, do not refuse.
**Back:** *"Written order, minimum data, logged."*

### Flashcard ETH-5168 — Puttaswamy proportionality for data
**Front:** What does Puttaswamy require for state infringement of privacy?
**Definition (learn):** Legitimate aim, necessity, and proportionate means-end balance (✅ owner 22; (2017) 10 SCC 1, nine-judge bench).
**Breakdown:**
- Aim.
- Necessity.
- Proportionality.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (2017) 10 SCC 1.
**Links:** ETH-5167.
**Trap:** Urgency alone is not a legal basis.
**Mains hook:** Use three limbs.
**Back:** *"Aim, need, measure."*

### Flashcard ETH-5169 — Case 14.14: triage in COVID surge (2021 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What is the decision in the triage case?
**Definition (learn):** Publish clinically anchored criteria in advance, applied uniformly regardless of payment; equity safeguard; fixed review cycle (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.14).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Public versus private: same ethical floor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5151.
**Trap:** Ability to pay as priority.
**Mains hook:** Same triage ethic in private hospitals.
**Back:** *"Publish, equalise, review."*

### Flashcard ETH-5170 — Case 14.15: SRTC Managing Director (2023 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What is the decision in the SRTC case?
**Definition (learn):** Document irregularities; written clarification; escalate through audit and vigilance and the administrative department; decline partisan leak (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.15).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Resignation premature.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5135.
**Trap:** Partisan leak.
**Mains hook:** Non-partisan process.
**Back:** *"Document, escalate, stay neutral."*

### Flashcard ETH-5171 — Case 14.16: Lata and Dr Mansi (2026 GS-IV Q7) and Samira Kohli
**Front:** What is the consent-validity analysis in the Lata case, and what does Samira Kohli hold?
**Definition (learn):** Diagnostic consent does not extend to hysterectomy; relative's consent invalid for a competent adult; emergency needs both limbs. Samira Kohli v Dr Prabha Manchanda, (2008) 2 SCC 1, decided 16 January 2008 (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.16).
**Breakdown:**
- Competence, authority, voluntariness, information, scope, emergency, prospective design.
- Subparts: (a) ethical issues; (b) doctor's conduct, motive, rightness, aftermath.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q7.
**Links:** ETH-5172, ETH-5174.
**Trap:** Motive as excuse.
**Mains hook:** Not malicious, not defensible.
**Back:** *"Good motive, no authority: a record, not consent."*

### Flashcard ETH-5172 — Consent-validity test (seven steps)
**Front:** What are the seven steps of the consent-validity test?
**Definition (learn):** Competence; authority; voluntariness; adequate information; scope; emergency (both limbs); prospective consent design (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14A.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Seven steps.
- Applies to research, data, land.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5171.
**Trap:** Documentation as validity.
**Mains hook:** Run all seven.
**Back:** *"Seven gates before consent."*

### Flashcard ETH-5173 — Case 14.17: SP Ravi predictive policing (2026 GS-IV Q8)
**Front:** What is the decision in the predictive-policing case?
**Definition (learn):** Calibrated suspension of person-level targeting and crowd biometrics; retain place-based deployment; time-bound independent bias audit; notice and contestation; review past detentions (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.17).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Feedback loop bias.
- Accountability non-delegation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q8.
**Links:** ETH-5174, ETH-5151.
**Trap:** Outcome gain validates means.
**Mains hook:** Contestability.
**Back:** *"Suspend the person, audit the record, keep the place."*

### Flashcard ETH-5174 — Automated adverse-decision template
**Front:** What are the seven steps of the automated adverse-decision template?
**Definition (learn):** Never auto-final; notice with reason; access to record; alternative cascade; human reconsideration; reasoned outcome and review; published error-rate audit (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14A.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Seven steps.
- Structure, not abolish, the exception.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5173, ETH-5175.
**Trap:** Free discretion.
**Mains hook:** Numbered, recorded, countersigned, rate-capped, audited.
**Back:** *"Never final, always visible, always appealable."*

### Flashcard ETH-5175 — Case 14.18: DC Seema tout network (2026 GS-IV Q9)
**Front:** What is the decision in the tout-network case?
**Definition (learn):** Parallel continuity track (chronological FIFO, dashboard, single window, random allocation) and accountability track (secret verification, audit, CVO and ACB referral, s.17A, targeted suspension) (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.18).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Continuity and accountability together.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q9.
**Links:** ETH-5036, ETH-5176.
**Trap:** Mass suspension.
**Mains hook:** Clean up without shutting down.
**Back:** *"Publish the queue, verify in secret, run both."*

### Flashcard ETH-5176 — Two-track template (clean up without shutting down)
**Front:** What are the two tracks of the parallel template?
**Definition (learn):** Continuity track runs publicly from day one; accountability track runs confidentially from day one (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14A.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Continuity: publish, dashboard, single window, allocation, timelines.
- Accountability: secret check, audit, referral, protected channel, targeted action.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5175, ETH-5177.
**Trap:** Picking one track.
**Mains hook:** Simultaneous, not sequential.
**Back:** *"Public service, private proof."*

### Flashcard ETH-5177 — Criteria-based denial template (seven steps)
**Front:** What are the seven steps for a criteria-based refusal?
**Definition (learn):** Pre-published criteria; hard screens first; claimant-by-claimant record; denial in process terms; conditional future channel; record override; build conditions into the instrument (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14A.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Seven steps.
- Legitimacy from criteria.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5178, ETH-5176.
**Trap:** Denial in moral terms.
**Mains hook:** Process terms.
**Back:** *"Criteria first, moral language never."*

### Flashcard ETH-5178 — Case 14.19: DC Vikas biometric PDS (2026 GS-IV Q10)
**Front:** What is the decision in the PDS biometric case?
**Definition (learn):** Structured cascade: fingerprint, iris or face, OTP, numbered recorded exception, rate-capped audit, doorstep delivery, portability, display, grievance route; connectivity fix prioritised (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.19).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Exclusion versus inclusion error.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q10; Article 21 named in question (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5179, ETH-5177.
**Trap:** Strict biometric-only enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Technology as evidence-aid, not sole gate.
**Back:** *"Cascade, register, cap, carry."*

### Flashcard ETH-5179 — Technology as gatekeeper: ethical test
**Front:** What is the operative test for using technology as a gate to welfare?
**Definition (learn):** Acceptable only if a genuine beneficiary who fails retains a usable, non-humiliating, time-bound, reviewable route, and someone is accountable for the failure rate (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.19).
**Breakdown:**
- Burden allocation.
- Disparate impact.
- Accountability displacement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5178, ETH-5174.
**Trap:** Technology is the ethical variable.
**Mains hook:** The fallback is the variable.
**Back:** *"The fallback, not the device, decides."*

### Flashcard ETH-5180 — Fiscal probity defined as leakage prevention
**Front:** What is fiscal probity in welfare delivery?
**Definition (learn):** Preventing leakage to the ineligible, not minimising disbursal. A scheme that excludes the eligible has achieved under-delivery (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.19).
**Breakdown:**
- Leakage: prevent.
- Under-delivery: failure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5178, ETH-5181.
**Trap:** Stricter gate equals probity.
**Mains hook:** Define probity correctly.
**Back:** *"Probity stops leakage, not the eligible."*

### Flashcard ETH-5181 — Exclusion error versus inclusion error
**Front:** Why are exclusion and inclusion errors asymmetric?
**Definition (learn):** Leakage is recoverable; a missed ration is an irreversible harm to a specific person (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.19).
**Breakdown:**
- Inclusion: recoverable.
- Exclusion: irreversible.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5180.
**Trap:** Treating errors as symmetric.
**Mains hook:** Weight errors.
**Back:** *"Leakage is recoverable; hunger is not."*

### Flashcard ETH-5182 — Case 14.20: DM Rajan, ethnic conflict and neutrality (2026 GS-IV Q11)
**Front:** What is the decision in the Rajan ethnic-conflict case?
**Definition (learn):** Sequenced package: life-preservation convoys; reconstitute neutral crisis team; visible symmetry; joint peace committee; restraint with written orders (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.20).
**Breakdown:**
- Option (c).
- Five stages.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q11; Anuradha Bhasin line (✅ owner).
**Links:** ETH-5183, ETH-5125.
**Trap:** Blanket curfew.
**Mains hook:** Sequencing defended.
**Back:** *"Feed first, rebuild neutrality, open talks."*

### Flashcard ETH-5183 — Non-partisanship: symmetry of process, not outcome
**Front:** What does non-partisanship require in a communal conflict?
**Definition (learn):** Symmetry of process and criteria, not symmetry of outcome. Equidistance from aggressor and victim is a second injustice (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.20).
**Breakdown:**
- Process symmetry.
- Outcome follows criteria.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5182, ETH-5184.
**Trap:** Equal outcome for both sides.
**Mains hook:** Reconciling principle.
**Back:** *"Same rules, not same results."*

### Flashcard ETH-5184 — Conduct-rule limits of non-partisanship
**Front:** Does non-partisanship require an official to have no opinions?
**Definition (learn):** No. It constrains official conduct, not private opinion (✅ owner 22 citing 03).
**Breakdown:**
- Conduct: constrained.
- Opinion: free.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner cross-ref 03.
**Links:** ETH-5183.
**Trap:** Demanding no opinions.
**Mains hook:** State the line.
**Back:** *"Act neutral, need not be empty."*

### Flashcard ETH-5185 — Case 14.21: Ajit arms export (2026 GS-IV Q12)
**Front:** What is the decision in the arms-export case?
**Definition (learn):** Sell to Country A with end-user certificate, no-re-transfer, verification and phased delivery; deny Country B by objective criteria in process terms; keep a conditional channel (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.21).
**Breakdown:**
- Hard threshold: proxy-support risk.
- Option (b).
- Screen before commercial.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-IV Q12; owner doctrine in 12.
**Links:** ETH-5186, ETH-5177.
**Trap:** Balancing advance payment against proxy support.
**Mains hook:** Time-horizon argument.
**Back:** *"Screen first, sell to the clean buyer, deny in process terms."*

### Flashcard ETH-5186 — Replaceability defence
**Front:** Does "someone else will sell" justify a transfer?
**Definition (learn):** No. Complicity is not excused by substitutability, and the argument proves too much (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.21).
**Breakdown:**
- Complicity: own act.
- Proves too much.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5185.
**Trap:** Accepting replaceability.
**Mains hook:** Rebut it.
**Back:** *"Mine is mine, whoever else would."*

### Flashcard ETH-5187 — Arms-export ethics: time horizon
**Front:** How do economic and ethical interests relate over time?
**Definition (learn):** Time-horizon-dependent, not opposed. Ethics is the asset of an export programme (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.21 part b).
**Breakdown:**
- Single deal: ethics costs.
- Programme: ethics builds.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5185, ETH-5177.
**Trap:** Opposing ethics and economy.
**Mains hook:** Corollaries: instrument, criteria, written override.
**Back:** *"Ethics is the export's long asset."*

### Flashcard ETH-5188 — Advanced: Subash conflict and PC Act escalation (2025 GS-IV Q9)
**Front:** What extra legal issue does the 2025 Subash case raise?
**Definition (learn):** Leaked price-sensitive information acted upon becomes a PC Act undue-advantage issue, not only a family conflict (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Family COI.
- Minister pressure.
- Law: PC Act if acted upon.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q9.
**Links:** ETH-5145, ETH-5007.
**Trap:** Treating as family-only.
**Mains hook:** Name the legal escalation.
**Back:** *"Family, then felony: check the act."*

### Flashcard ETH-5189 — Advanced: Sneha as conflict test
**Front:** Why is Sneha's case a hard-threshold conflict?
**Definition (learn):** The personal link disqualifies in itself; management trust is the reasoning a corrupt outcome would use (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Hard threshold.
- Trust is not a mitigant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-IV Q10.
**Links:** ETH-5160.
**Trap:** Mitigating by trust.
**Mains hook:** Name the reasoning.
**Back:** *"Trust is not a recusal."*

### Flashcard ETH-5190 — Advanced: 2024 Q7 steelman
**Front:** What is the advanced steelman for the 2024 AI emissions case?
**Definition (learn):** Competitors unconstrained by discipline could out-innovate a self-restrained firm, eroding its capacity to fund sustainability. Answer: time-bound, verifiable commitments (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Objection.
- Design response.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-IV Q7.
**Links:** ETH-5162, ETH-5147.
**Trap:** Ignoring the objection.
**Mains hook:** Turn objection into constraint.
**Back:** *"Turn the objection into the design."*

### Flashcard ETH-5191 — Advanced: GS-IV has no official model answer
**Front:** Is the six-step architecture an official UPSC model answer?
**Definition (learn):** No. It is a defensible synthesis heuristic (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Heuristic.
- Not official.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5141.
**Trap:** Quoting it as official.
**Mains hook:** Say it is a method.
**Back:** *"Method, not mandate."*

### Flashcard ETH-5192 — Trap: all six checks equal
**Front:** Do all six checks carry equal weight?
**Definition (learn):** No. Legality and conflict of interest are near-hard thresholds (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Hard: two.
- Weighted: four.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5142.
**Trap:** Equal weights.
**Mains hook:** Prioritise.
**Back:** *"Two walls, not six equals."*

### Flashcard ETH-5193 — Trap: avoiding weakness in own decision
**Front:** Does a good answer avoid mentioning weaknesses in its decision?
**Definition (learn):** No. Examiners reward residual-risk acknowledgment and steelmanning (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Name weakness.
- Address it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5148.
**Trap:** Claiming risk-free.
**Mains hook:** Acknowledge.
**Back:** *"Admit the risk, own the watch."*

### Flashcard ETH-5194 — Trap: over-decomposition
**Front:** Should every case be decomposed into many sub-dilemmas?
**Definition (learn):** No. Decompose only analytically separable dilemmas (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Separable: yes.
- Simple: no.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5146.
**Trap:** Over-splitting.
**Mains hook:** Proportionate decomposition.
**Back:** *"Split only what splits."*

### Flashcard ETH-5195 — Trap: good intention cures unlawful act
**Front:** Does good intention cure an act done without lawful authority or valid consent?
**Definition (learn):** No. Good intention bears on culpability and damages, not rightness (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.16).
**Breakdown:**
- Motive: culpability.
- Authority: rightness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5171.
**Trap:** Benevolence excuses.
**Mains hook:** Separate motive from act.
**Back:** *"Kind motive, unlicensed act."*

### Flashcard ETH-5196 — Trap: improved outcome validates technology
**Front:** Does outcome improvement validate a technology deployment?
**Definition (learn):** No. Means must be tested separately (✅ owner 22 Sec. 14.17).
**Breakdown:**
- Outcome.
- Means.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5173.
**Trap:** Outcome as vindication.
**Mains hook:** Test means.
**Back:** *"Results do not launder means."*

### Flashcard ETH-5197 — Trap: replaceability and "someone else will"
**Front:** Does replaceability justify a sale?
**Definition (learn):** No (see ETH-5186).
**Breakdown:**
- Complicity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5186.
**Trap:** Accepting.
**Mains hook:** Rebut.
**Back:** *"Not excused by substitute."*

### Flashcard ETH-5198 — PYQ routing: case-study bank 2018–2026
**Front:** How many GS-IV Section B cases are routed to the Ethics case-study method across the audited years?
**Definition (learn):** 2018–2023: 36 routed demands (2018 to 2023 each year). 2024–2025: 12 demands. 2026: Q7–Q12 fully worked in Sec. 14.16–14.21 (✅ owner 22 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- 2018–2023: 36.
- 2024–2025: 12.
- 2026: six cases.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner ledgers.
**Links:** ETH-5136, ETH-5199.
**Trap:** Assuming all cases are new families.
**Mains hook:** Practise against the families.
**Back:** *"Thirty-six, twelve, six: practise by family."*

### Flashcard ETH-5199 — Section B weight and subparts
**Front:** What weight and format does Section B carry in the audited papers?
**Definition (learn):** 120 of 250 marks in GS-IV for 2024–2026, six cases of 20 marks each (Q7–Q12). Each case carried two subparts (✅ owner 22; ⚠️ pattern from three years only).
**Breakdown:**
- 120 marks.
- Six cases.
- Two subparts each.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5200.
**Trap:** Answering (b) by restating (a).
**Mains hook:** Decode each subpart.
**Back:** *"Six cases, two subparts, one decode each."*

### Flashcard ETH-5200 — Subpart decode: restating is the costliest error
**Front:** What is the most expensive structural error in Section B?
**Definition (learn):** Answering subpart (b) with a restatement of (a) (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- (a): issues.
- (b): options or normative appraisal.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5199.
**Trap:** Continuous essay.
**Mains hook:** Separate the subparts.
**Back:** *"Each subpart, its own answer."*

### Flashcard ETH-5201 — PYQ: 2026 GS-IV Section B families
**Front:** Which six 2026 Section B families does the owner cover?
**Definition (learn):** Therapeutic consent (Q7); predictive policing (Q8); tout network (Q9); biometric PDS (Q10); ethnic conflict (Q11); arms export (Q12) (✅ owner 22).
**Breakdown:**
- Q7 to Q12.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5171 to ETH-5186.
**Trap:** Mixing families.
**Mains hook:** Map each to its module.
**Back:** *"Seven to twelve, six families, six modules."*

### Flashcard ETH-5202 — Mains: the directive-by-directive answer
**Front:** What is the Mains answer pattern for 20-mark case studies?
**Definition (learn):** Structured sections matching the architecture, a specific actionable step, and residual-risk close (✅ owner 22 Sec. 19).
**Breakdown:**
- Headers.
- Actionable step.
- Residual close.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5148.
**Trap:** Abstract balancing.
**Mains hook:** Signpost each element.
**Back:** *"Signpost, act, close."*

### Flashcard ETH-5203 — Case study: nested DC Vijay sub-dilemmas (advanced)
**Front:** What three sub-dilemmas does DC Vijay nest?
**Definition (learn):** Personal grief versus public duty; resource fairness in relief; transparency versus calm (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Three sub-dilemmas.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-IV Q7.
**Links:** ETH-5146, ETH-5163.
**Trap:** Blending them.
**Mains hook:** Solve each.
**Back:** *"Grief, relief, truth."*

### Flashcard ETH-5204 — Calibrated communication in active crisis
**Front:** What balances transparency against calm in an active crisis?
**Definition (learn):** Partial accurate disclosure with commitment to fuller disclosure once verified (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Avoid over-disclosure.
- Avoid under-disclosure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5203.
**Trap:** Full immediate or prolonged silence.
**Mains hook:** Calibrated.
**Back:** *"Partial now, full when verified."*

### Flashcard ETH-5205 — Mains: rebutting the replaceability argument (advanced)
**Front:** How does the advanced framework rebut "someone else will supply it"?
**Definition (learn):** Moral point: responsibility attaches to one's own act. Practical point: documented refusal builds reputation. Risk point: proxy weapons are liabilities (✅ owner 22 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Moral.
- Practical.
- Risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner.
**Links:** ETH-5186.
**Trap:** Using only the moral point.
**Mains hook:** Use all three.
**Back:** *"Moral, practical, risk: three locks."*

## ETHICS — Part 6: Comparative and Named Real Case Studies

### GROUP — 23 Comparative and Named Real Case Studies (basic and advanced)

### Flashcard ETH-6001 — Named cases as balanced evidence, not hero stories
**Front:** What is the analytical job of the named-case file, and why must each case carry its source, date and limitation?
**Definition (learn):** The owner treats named Indian cases and comparative anti-corruption models as evidence for a mechanism (what worked, why, and what stayed unresolved). The ARC deliberately pairs success cases with sobering ones; a model answer keeps both.
**Breakdown:**
- Source base: 2nd ARC 4th Report *Ethics in Governance* (2007): Box 3.1 (whistleblowers), paras 2.5.7.1-2.5.7.3 (Mudgal, cash-for-questions), 5.1.3-5.1.4 (MKSS, Parivartan), 6.4.2-6.4.4 (Gyandoot, Bhoomi, CARD), 5.1.2 and Colloquium Annexure I(2) (Hong Kong, Singapore, Korea, Finland, Thailand, Japan).
- Cross-domain named evidence (Sec. 4): Vishaka (1997), POSH Act 2013, ICMR 2017, NHRC v. Arunachal Pradesh (1996), Mohammad Salimullah (2021), Bhagalpur (1989), Nanavati, Anuradha Bhasin (2020), D.K. Basu (1997), Samira Kohli (2008).
- Tags: ✅ source-grounded, ⚠️ analytical inference, 📰 current anchor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner core proposition (✅ owner 23 basic, Sec. 1). Companion advanced owner (✅ owner 23 advanced).
**Links:** ETH-6002, ETH-6016, ETH-6025, ETH-6051, ETH-6052, ETH-6054.
**Trap:** "Civil society always wins" or "technology always reduces corruption". The ARC's own CARD citation (ETH-6015) is a counter-example in the same chapter.
**Mains hook:** Name the case, its source and date, its causal mechanism and its documented limit. Qualified conclusion: anti-corruption gains depend on process redesign and institutional capacity, not on a single lever.
**Back:** *"Keep the case, its date and its limit; drop the hero."*

### Flashcard ETH-6002 — Manjunath Shanmugam (IOC; petrol-pump adulteration)
**Front:** Who was Manjunath Shanmugam, what did he resist, and why is his death cited in the whistleblower debate?
**Definition (learn):** An IIM-Lucknow graduate and Indian Oil Corporation officer who resisted bribes and threats while fighting petrol-pump adulteration; shot dead on 19 November 2005.
**Breakdown:**
- Ethical issue: a public-sector officer's integrity against a consumer-harm racket.
- Principle illustrated: individual exposure of serious wrongdoing carries personal risk, which is why protection is needed.
- Attribution: the owner says the killing was "allegedly" at the behest of corrupt petrol-pump owners. Wikipedia names pump owner Pawan Kumar "Monu" Mittal as main accused, later convicted; the Supreme Court upheld the convictions in 2015.
- Use in a case-study answer: cite as the ARC's evidentiary basis for whistleblower protection (Box 3.1), then state that the 2014 Act is not in force (ETH-6005).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Died 19 November 2005 (✅ owner 23; ✅ Wikipedia). IIM-Lucknow MBA, IOC sales officer, Lakhimpur Kheri, Uttar Pradesh (✅ Wikipedia). ARC Box 3.1 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** ETH-6003, ETH-6004, ETH-6005.
**Trap:** Death did not produce immediate legal protection. The ARC-recommended statute was enacted in 2014 and remains not in force per owner.
**Mains hook:** Whistleblower courage exposes an institutional gap. Qualified: the case proves the need for protection, not that protection exists.
**Back:** *"Pump adulteration, IOC officer, 19 Nov 2005: a death that asked for a law."*

### Flashcard ETH-6003 — Satyendra Dubey (NHAI; road-construction corruption)
**Front:** Who was Satyendra Dubey, what did he expose, and what is the disputed status of his motive?
**Definition (learn):** An Indian Engineering Service officer on deputation to the National Highways Authority of India, found shot dead on 27 November 2003 after exposing corruption in road construction.
**Breakdown:**
- Posting: Project Director, NHAI, Koderma (Jharkhand) (✅ Wikipedia).
- Circumstances: found beside a road near Gaya on the night of his 30th birthday; the CBI presented a botched-robbery explanation; a Patna court convicted three men in 2010 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Principle: whistleblower vulnerability in infrastructure procurement.
- Answer discipline: state that the ARC cites his case as an exposure death, and that the public record marks the motive as disputed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 November 2003 (✅ owner 23; ✅ Wikipedia). "Killed for exposing corruption" is reportedly claimed but disputed (✅ Wikipedia). Owner-vs-source flag: the owner presents the exposure as the cause without qualification.
**Links:** ETH-6002, ETH-6004, ETH-6005.
**Trap:** Stating as settled fact that Dubey was killed for exposing corruption, or treating the 2010 conviction as proof of a non-corruption motive.
**Mains hook:** Qualified: the case supports protection for whistleblowers whether or not the motive can be proved in every instance.
**Back:** *"Highway engineer, NHAI, 27 Nov 2003: a cause the record still disputes."*

### Flashcard ETH-6004 — Manjunath vs Dubey: comparing the two whistleblower deaths
**Front:** How do the Manjunath Shanmugam and Satyendra Dubey cases compare, and what single lesson do they share?
**Definition (learn):** Two whistleblower deaths in different sectors (fuel quality; national highway construction) cited together by the ARC as the human cost behind its protection recommendation.
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: Dubey 27 November 2003; Manjunath 19 November 2005.
- Organisation: NHAI (central road project) vs IOC (fuel retail, petrol-pump network).
- Wrongdoing: procurement corruption in road construction vs adulterated petrol.
- Motive status: Dubey's motive disputed in public record; Manjunath's attribution to pump owners, with convictions upheld (✅ Wikipedia).
- Shared lesson: exposure without a statutory shield exposes the individual.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (Must-Know Facts); ✅ Wikipedia for motive and conviction details.
**Links:** ETH-6002, ETH-6003, ETH-6005.
**Trap:** Treating them as one story; or dating Dubey's death to 2005.
**Mains hook:** Two sectors, one institutional gap. Qualified: the cases show personal risk, not systemic failure alone.
**Back:** *"Two officers, two sectors, one missing shield: 2003 road, 2005 pump."*

### Flashcard ETH-6005 — Whistle Blowers Protection Act 2014: enacted but not in force
**Front:** Did the ARC's whistleblower recommendation become operative law, and what interim mechanism operates instead?
**Definition (learn):** The Whistle Blowers Protection Act, 2014 received assent on 9 May 2014 but has not been brought into force; the 2015 Amendment Bill lapsed; the operative interim mechanism is the CVC's PIDPI resolution, which is administrative.
**Breakdown:**
- Assent: 9 May 2014 (✅ owner 23 advanced).
- Commencement: not brought into force (owner, as of its own dated status).
- Interim: CVC PIDPI Resolution of 21 April 2004 (see ETH-6053).
- Legal weight: statutory protection (on paper) versus administrative (operative).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 May 2014; 2015 Amendment Bill lapsed (✅ owner 23 advanced cross-references to owners 10 and 19). 📰 Commencement status is time-varying: the advanced owner states it "as of 4 August 2026", which predates today (1 November 2026). ⚠️ Re-verify before quoting. Owner-vs-today flag.
**Links:** ETH-6002, ETH-6003, ETH-6053, ETH-6054.
**Trap:** "The Whistle Blowers Act immediately protected Manjunath and Dubey." Wrong: the Act came about a decade after their deaths and, per owner, has not been commenced.
**Mains hook:** A law without commencement is a protection in name. Qualified: check current commencement status on the day of use.
**Back:** *"Assent 2014, commencement pending: protection on paper, not yet in the field."*

### Flashcard ETH-6006 — H.G. Mudgal case (1951): first major parliamentary-expulsion precedent
**Front:** What was the H.G. Mudgal case, and why is it India's early parliamentary-expulsion precedent for corrupt conduct?
**Definition (learn):** An ad hoc Parliamentary Committee found H.G. Mudgal guilty of accepting money or benefits from the Bombay Bullion Association for parliamentary favours; Prime Minister Nehru argued the case was unambiguous and moved expulsion, which the House accepted.
**Breakdown:**
- Ethical issue: a Member using parliamentary office for private gain.
- Mechanism: ad hoc committee finding → House decision → expulsion.
- Doctrine (Nehru, as quoted in advanced owner): the case must be "as bad as it could well be", and the House's decision should be "clear, precise and definite".
- Significance: proven corrupt conduct is "unbecoming" of membership.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951 (✅ owner 23, ARC 2.5.7.1; ✅ owner 23 advanced for Nehru quotation). Owner calls it "independent India's first major parliamentary-expulsion precedent for corrupt conduct" (✅ owner 23 Must-Know).
**Links:** ETH-6007, ETH-6008.
**Trap:** Calling it the first expulsion of any kind. The precedent is specific to corrupt conduct, and the owner's word is "major".
**Mains hook:** Parliament can enforce its own standards on clear proof. Qualified: the case was triggered by a specific complaint (ETH-6008).
**Back:** *"1951: favours for money, expulsion for Mudgal; clear proof, clear House."*

### Flashcard ETH-6007 — Cash-for-questions (2005): ten Lok Sabha and one Rajya Sabha Member expelled
**Front:** What happened in the 2005 cash-for-questions case, and how many Members were expelled?
**Definition (learn):** After an "Aaj Tak" telecast on 12 December 2005 alleged cash for asking questions or raising matters, a Lok Sabha Enquiry Committee found ten Members' conduct unbecoming and unethical; the House accepted expulsion. The Rajya Sabha Chairman referred one Member to its Ethics Committee, which found a contravention of para 5 of the Rajya Sabha Code of Conduct and recommended expulsion, accepted by the House.
**Breakdown:**
- Ethical issue: Members taking money to ask questions or raise matters.
- Mechanism (Lok Sabha): TV exposé → enquiry committee → House expulsion.
- Mechanism (Rajya Sabha): chairman's referral → Ethics Committee → finding → expulsion.
- Total: eleven expulsions (10 Lok Sabha + 1 Rajya Sabha).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Telecast 12 December 2005; 10 LS + 1 RS = 11 (✅ owner 23, ARC 2.5.7.2-2.5.7.3; ✅ owner 23 Must-Know).
**Links:** ETH-6006, ETH-6008.
**Trap:** Saying "ten MPs" as the full total. The total is eleven.
**Mains hook:** The same self-discipline pattern as 1951, now triggered by media. Qualified: reactive, not preventive (ETH-6008).
**Back:** *"Aaj Tak, 12 Dec 2005: ten Lok Sabha, one Rajya Sabha, eleven in all."*

### Flashcard ETH-6008 — Parliamentary self-discipline: corrective, not preventive
**Front:** What evidentiary limit do the Mudgal and cash-for-questions cases reveal about parliamentary ethics enforcement?
**Definition (learn):** Parliament expels when wrongdoing is clearly and publicly proven, but both precedents were externally triggered (a complaint in 1951; a media sting in 2005). The mechanism is therefore corrective rather than preventive.
**Breakdown:**
- Strength: clear proof leads to House acceptance of expulsion.
- Limit: exposure-dependence; the owner shows no proactive detection by the Ethics Committees in these cases.
- Test to apply: does the mechanism detect conduct before exposure? If not, label it corrective.
- Inference: the cases do not show that undetected similar conduct was absent between episodes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 advanced Sec. 3 (Parliamentary conduct); ⚠️ the reactive/corrective label is analytical.
**Links:** ETH-6006, ETH-6007, ETH-6054.
**Trap:** "Parliament's ethics committees prevent corruption." The two cases do not show this.
**Mains hook:** Recommend pre-emptive disclosure and monitoring. Qualified: expulsion is strongest where proof is public.
**Back:** *"The cases prove a verdict, not a prevention: expulsion follows exposure."*

### Flashcard ETH-6009 — MKSS Jan Sunwai (Rajasthan): documents read aloud
**Front:** What did the Mazdoor Kisan Shakti Sangathan's Jan Sunwai do, and what did it achieve in Rajasthan?
**Definition (learn):** MKSS obtained employment rolls, vouchers, beneficiary lists and completion and utilisation certificates, then read them out in public hearings where villagers scrutinised them.
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: document access plus community-witnessed cross-checking, turning private suspicion into public, provable evidence.
- Exposed: false muster rolls, false bills and vouchers, false completion certificates.
- Outcome (ARC 5.1.4): Rajasthan created a ward sabha empowered to socially audit government programmes, approve public-works proposals and certify proper execution (see ETH-6012).
- Limit: depends on organisational capacity; not automatically replicable (ETH-6057).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 5.1.3-5.1.4). ✅ Wikipedia: Jan Sunwai reads official expenditure figures to villagers; MKSS campaigned for the RTI Act. ⚠️ Founding year is inconsistent on Wikipedia (1 May 1990 infobox vs 1987/1967 categories); do not cite a founding year without a primary source.
**Links:** ETH-6010, ETH-6011, ETH-6012, ETH-6057.
**Trap:** Describing MKSS as a protest group only. Its method was evidentiary, using the documents.
**Mains hook:** Civil society as an evidence-production mechanism. Qualified by capacity dependence.
**Back:** *"Papers read aloud, falsehood heard: Jan Sunwai turns files into evidence."*

### Flashcard ETH-6010 — Parivartan (Delhi): RTI and fair-price shop stock registers
**Front:** How did Parivartan use the Right to Information law to expose PDS diversion?
**Definition (learn):** A Delhi civil-society initiative that used RTI to insist on access to stock registers kept by fair-price shops, exposing diversion of rice, wheat and oil meant for the public into the open market.
**Breakdown:**
- Instrument: RTI application for stock registers.
- Method: reconcile recorded stock with actual distribution.
- Outcome: documented diversion to the open market.
- Limit: RTI gives access; enforcement of the findings is a separate step.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 5.1.4). No date given in the owner.
**Links:** ETH-6009, ETH-6011.
**Trap:** Treating Parivartan as a national scheme. The ARC cites it as a Delhi case.
**Mains hook:** Information rights as an anti-leakage tool in PDS. Qualified: depends on access being enforced and acted upon.
**Back:** *"Stock register in, diversion out: RTI made the PDS ledger speak."*

### Flashcard ETH-6011 — MKSS vs Parivartan: two civil-society instruments
**Front:** How do MKSS Jan Sunwai and Parivartan differ as civil-society anti-corruption mechanisms?
**Definition (learn):** Both convert suspicion into documented evidence, but by different devices: a public hearing (MKSS) and an RTI demand for registers (Parivartan).
**Breakdown:**
- Instrument: MKSS = public hearing and social audit; Parivartan = RTI application.
- Venue: MKSS = village-level public forum; Parivartan = fair-price shops in Delhi.
- Output: MKSS = a ward-sabha audit institution; Parivartan = exposure of diversion.
- Common sequence: information access → public verification → institutional audit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 5.1.3-5.1.4).
**Links:** ETH-6009, ETH-6010, ETH-6012.
**Trap:** "Both are RTI campaigns." The MKSS hearing is its distinctive device.
**Mains hook:** Use the sequence to explain how civil society moves from evidence to institutional reform. Qualified by capacity.
**Back:** *"Hearing versus register: one audits in public, one audits the ledger."*

### Flashcard ETH-6012 — Rajasthan ward sabha: social audit given an institution
**Front:** What powers did Rajasthan's ward sabha receive after the MKSS-era social-audit pressure?
**Definition (learn):** A ward sabha empowered to conduct social audit of government programmes, approve public-works proposals and certify proper execution.
**Breakdown:**
- Power 1: social audit of government programmes.
- Power 2: approval of public-works proposals.
- Power 3: certification of proper execution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 5.1.4). Owner phrasing is "led to"; the causal attribution to MKSS is the ARC's account (⚠️ not independently verified here).
**Links:** ETH-6009, ETH-6011.
**Trap:** Treating the ward sabha as a national statutory body. This is a Rajasthan institution.
**Mains hook:** Institutionalising social audit makes civil-society findings durable. Qualified: causation is the ARC's account.
**Back:** *"Ward sabha audits, approves, certifies: social audit given an institution."*

### Flashcard ETH-6013 — Gyandoot (Madhya Pradesh): youth-run village intranet kiosks
**Front:** What was Gyandoot, what services did it offer, and what did the evaluation show about corruption?
**Definition (learn):** A low-cost intranet linking market centres and villages, with local youth running kiosks on commercial lines, offering agricultural auction rates, land-record copies, online application registration, grievance redress and social-security beneficiary information.
**Breakdown:**
- Funding: entire project cost borne by panchayats and the local community, averaging about ₹75,000 per village.
- Usage: the three most-used services accounted for 95% of usage.
- Outcome: opportunities for corruption greatly reduced (evaluation as cited by the ARC).
- Mechanism: reduced discretionary human intermediation at the point of citizen contact.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 6.4.2). ⚠️ Launch year not stated in the owner and not verified here.
**Links:** ETH-6014, ETH-6016.
**Trap:** Reading ₹75,000 as a state outlay. It was community-borne, per village.
**Mains hook:** Local ownership and cheap intermediation can cut contact-point corruption. Qualified: the owner's three-service concentration shows usage was narrow.
**Back:** *"Youth kiosks, village purse, three services carry 95%: intermediaries thinned."*

### Flashcard ETH-6014 — Bhoomi (Karnataka): automated mutation with a 30-day window
**Front:** What did Bhoomi computerise, and how did it automate the mutation process?
**Definition (learn):** Karnataka's computerised land-ownership record system, which automated the mutation (transfer) process rather than only digitising records.
**Breakdown:**
- Scale: 20 million land-ownership records of 6.7 million farmers; RTC extract ₹15 at computerised kiosks in 177 offices.
- Mutation: the computer automatically generates notices; the field revenue inspector approves the change after thirty days if no objection is received.
- Evaluation: an independent evaluation in 2002 showed significant efficiency gains and reduced corruption (ARC citation).
- Mechanism: removal of the revenue official's discretionary gatekeeping in the mutation step.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 6.4.3). Wikipedia not checked; figures are owner-grounded.
**Links:** ETH-6015, ETH-6016, ETH-6017.
**Trap:** Describing Bhoomi as "mere digitisation". The owner's analysis stresses automated mutation and the removed gatekeeper.
**Mains hook:** Automation of discretion is the variable. Qualified: the evaluation is the ARC's citation.
**Back:** *"Twenty million records, thirty days, no gatekeeper: Bhoomi automated the decision."*

### Flashcard ETH-6015 — CARD (Andhra Pradesh): the ARC's counter-example
**Front:** What is CARD, and why is its evaluation the ARC's own counter-example to e-governance optimism?
**Definition (learn):** Computer-Aided Administration of Registration Department (Andhra Pradesh). A cited evaluation (Ramanathan and Balakrishnan, 2000) found no significant difference in corruption levels between computerised and non-computerised Sub-Registrar Offices.
**Breakdown:**
- Finding: null result on corruption.
- Analytical explanation (⚠️): record storage was computerised while the discretionary approval and valuation step at the transaction point was left largely intact.
- Lesson: computerising records is not automating discretion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC 6.4.4). Evaluation year 2000 (owner citation).
**Links:** ETH-6014, ETH-6016, ETH-6017.
**Trap:** Calling CARD a failed technology. It is a null result on corruption under the cited evaluation.
**Mains hook:** Digitisation without process redesign does not reduce corruption. Qualified: based on one cited evaluation.
**Back:** *"CARD computerised the register, not the discretion: no corruption change."*

### Flashcard ETH-6016 — Bhoomi, Gyandoot and CARD: why IT-enabled reforms diverged
**Front:** If Bhoomi, Gyandoot and CARD all used IT, why did two reduce corruption and one did not?
**Definition (learn):** The decisive variable is whether the discretionary decision step was removed or constrained, not whether technology was used.
**Breakdown:**
- Bhoomi: automated mutation, removed the gatekeeper, defined 30-day window.
- Gyandoot: kiosks reduced intermediation at citizen contact.
- CARD: digitised storage; discretion at the transaction left largely intact (⚠️ analytical).
- Design variable: redesign of the decision process.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 advanced Sec. 2 and 3 (Bhoomi vs CARD). The advanced owner explicitly rejects the "different technology" explanation.
**Links:** ETH-6013, ETH-6014, ETH-6015, ETH-6017, ETH-6058.
**Trap:** "Different technology explains different outcomes." Both Bhoomi and CARD were IT-enabled record reforms of the same period.
**Mains hook:** Ten-mark comparison answer. Technology is the instrument; process redesign is the variable. Qualified: the owner's causal reading is analytical.
**Back:** *"Same computers, different discretion: Bhoomi removed it, CARD kept it."*

### Flashcard ETH-6017 — Framework: digitising storage vs automating discretion
**Front:** What test separates digitising records from automating discretion when assessing an e-governance reform?
**Definition (learn):** A reform reliably reduces corruption only when it removes or constrains a human discretionary step (approval, valuation, gatekeeping). Electronic storage alone does not.
**Breakdown:**
- Test 1: which official step decides the outcome?
- Test 2: does the system decide, notify and time-bound that step, or only record it?
- Test 3: is there a defined objection window or deemed-approval rule?
- Test 4: is the redesigned process audited before the anti-corruption claim is certified?
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Analytical framework derived from the advanced owner (Sec. 3, 5 and 10).
**Links:** ETH-6014, ETH-6015, ETH-6016.
**Trap:** "Any e-governance project reduces corruption." The owner's CARD citation refutes the universal claim.
**Mains hook:** Audit whether discretion was removed before crediting an anti-corruption benefit (owner advanced Sec. 5). Qualified: not every corruption type is affected (ETH-6058).
**Back:** *"Scan the file and the discretion stays; automate the decision and it goes."*

### Flashcard ETH-6018 — Hong Kong ICAC: enforcement, prevention and education
**Front:** What is Hong Kong's Independent Commission Against Corruption, and what three-pronged strategy does it use?
**Definition (learn):** The ICAC, established 15 February 1974 under the Independent Commission Against Corruption Ordinance (Cap. 204), works through three prongs: law enforcement, corruption prevention and community education.
**Breakdown:**
- Law enforcement: Operations Department (investigation).
- Corruption prevention: Corruption Prevention Department, helping organisations build systems that resist graft.
- Community education: Community Relations Department, school programmes, community outreach and the television drama series *ICAC Investigators*.
- ARC credit: transformed a "passive social environment" that had condoned corruption (Box 5.1, Annexure I(2)).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 February 1974 and Cap. 204 (✅ Wikipedia). "February 1974" and the three-pronged strategy (✅ owner 23). Owner Box 5.1 and Annexure I(2) citation (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** ETH-6019, ETH-6025, ETH-6027.
**Trap:** "ICAC" is also the name of other bodies (Mauritius, New South Wales, South Australia, Northern Territory). Always say Hong Kong's ICAC.
**Mains hook:** The attitude-change model. Qualified: its public-education intensity presumes a compact city-state (ETH-6027).
**Back:** *"Enforce, prevent, educate: Hong Kong changed the norm, not just the penalty."*

### Flashcard ETH-6019 — Singapore: PCA 1960 penalties and the CPIB
**Front:** What are the current Prevention of Corruption Act 1960 penalty maxima in Singapore, and what does the CPIB do?
**Definition (learn):** Singapore's Prevention of Corruption Act was enacted 17 June 1960; the Corrupt Practices Investigation Bureau is its independent investigating and prosecuting agency for serious or complex fraud and corruption.
**Breakdown:**
- Each corruption count (sections 5-6): fine up to S$100,000, imprisonment up to five years, or both.
- Government or public-body contract matters (section 7): imprisonment up to seven years, or both.
- Forfeiture: the bribe amount is forfeited on conviction.
- CPIB: covers public and private sectors; holds arrest powers; reports to the Prime Minister's Office.
- The ARC's 2007 annexure (up to five years and S$10,000) is historical, not current law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 June 1960 (✅ Wikipedia). S$100,000 / five years / seven years for contracts (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 23 advanced Sec. 3). 2007 ARC figures (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** ETH-6018, ETH-6020, ETH-6025.
**Trap:** Quoting the ARC's S$10,000 figure as current penalty.
**Mains hook:** Deterrence plus an autonomous enforcement agency. Qualified: due-process trade-offs (owner advanced Sec. 6).
**Back:** *"S$100,000, five years, seven for contracts: the 2007 figures are history."*

### Flashcard ETH-6020 — Singapore confiscation: 1989 Act (ARC) vs 1992 CDSA (current)
**Front:** Which confiscation statute does Singapore now use, and what happened to the 1989 Act the ARC cites?
**Definition (learn):** The ARC cites the Corruption (Confiscation of Benefits) Act 1989. The owner states it was repealed on 13 September 1999, with confiscation now run under the Corruption, Drug Trafficking and Other Serious Crimes (Confiscation of Benefits) Act 1992.
**Breakdown:**
- Source-age: the 1989 Act is the ARC's 2007 description.
- Current regime: 1992 statute (CDSA).
- Model type: asset-confiscation as deterrent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1989 and 1992 statutes, and the 13 September 1999 repeal date are ⚠️ owner-only. Wikipedia's CPIB page does not mention them (not verified).
**Links:** ETH-6019, ETH-6025.
**Trap:** Citing the 1989 Act as current law. Also do not treat the repeal date as independently verified.
**Mains hook:** The confiscation model strips benefits from corruption. Qualified: confiscation regimes raise due-process questions.
**Back:** *"1989 in the ARC, 1992 in force: strip the gain, keep the dates straight."*

### Flashcard ETH-6021 — Korea's 1975 reform: extended-liability model
**Front:** What did Korea's 1975 General Administrative Reform Movement do according to the ARC, and what part is uncorroborated?
**Definition (learn):** The ARC's Colloquium annexure (I(2)) states the 1975 movement prohibited private-sector re-employment of civil servants dismissed for corruption and extended the prohibition to their sons, grandsons and whoever recommended them.
**Breakdown:**
- Corroborated: the 1975 movement and re-employment restrictions (IMF working paper, per owner).
- Not corroborated: the family and recommender extension. Present it as the ARC's account.
- Current framework (📰, owner): Act on the Prevention of Corruption and the Establishment and Management of the Anti-Corruption and Civil Rights Commission (ACRC); the Improper Solicitation and Graft Act, 2015 ("Kim Young-ran Act").
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1975 (✅ owner 23; IMF corroboration per owner). Family/recommender extension ⚠️ (owner). ACRC and 2015 Act (owner; not verified here).
**Links:** ETH-6025, ETH-6028.
**Trap:** Stating family liability as verified Korean law.
**Mains hook:** Extended liability as deterrent versus collective punishment. Qualified: the family extension rests on a single annexure.
**Back:** *"1975 barred re-hiring; the family and recommender part is the ARC's claim."*

### Flashcard ETH-6022 — Finland: least corrupt, with no special anti-corruption statute
**Front:** Why is Finland cited as a least-corrupt model, and what is the exact claim about its anti-corruption law?
**Definition (learn):** Finland is cited by the ARC (Annexure VIII, "Honesty in Finland") as least corrupt on the Transparency International index at the time. Its current CPI score is 88/100, rank 2 of 182 (CPI 2025).
**Breakdown:**
- CPI 2025: 88/100, 2nd of 182 (✅ Wikipedia; index published February 2026).
- Precise claim: no separate statute governing anti-corruption measures or defining the forms of corruption (⚠️ owner, citing its official portal).
- Bribery is criminalised through the Criminal Code (⚠️ owner).
- Lesson: institutional culture and rule of law matter more than definitional drafting.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 88/100, rank 2 (✅ Wikipedia). ARC Annexure VIII (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** ETH-6023, ETH-6026, ETH-6054.
**Trap:** "Finland has no anti-corruption law." Wrong: bribery offences exist in the Criminal Code.
**Mains hook:** Culture over statute. Qualified: a perception index measures perceived corruption, not its absence.
**Back:** *"Rank 2 of 182, 88 out of 100: no special statute, yet bribery is criminal."*

### Flashcard ETH-6023 — Thailand: severe penalties, high corruption
**Front:** Why is Thailand the ARC's counter-example to pure deterrence, and what penalty does the 2018 Organic Act actually prescribe?
**Definition (learn):** The ARC notes Thailand's very high corruption penalties yet its placement "among countries with the highest level of corruption". The Organic Act on Anti-Corruption B.E. 2561 (2018) prescribes five to twenty years' imprisonment or life, plus fine, for specified bribery offences.
**Breakdown:**
- Death penalty: appears as an alternative punishment in specified Penal Code provisions on official and judicial bribe-taking (ss.149, 201, 202), not under the Organic Act (⚠️ owner, not fetched).
- CPI 2025: 33/100, rank 116 of 182 (tied with Ecuador, Panama and Serbia) (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Organic Act and penalties (⚠️ owner; not verified by fetch). 33/100 and 116 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ETH-6022, ETH-6026.
**Trap:** Blanket claim that Thailand imposes the death penalty for corruption.
**Mains hook:** Severe penalties alone do not guarantee low corruption. Qualified: penalty and enforcement quality interact.
**Back:** *"Harsh statute, 33 out of 100: penalty alone is not enough."*

### Flashcard ETH-6024 — Japan: social disapproval as a regulator
**Front:** What informal mechanism does the ARC describe for regulating civil-servant conduct in Japan?
**Definition (learn):** Social disapproval, a cultural rather than purely legal sanction, is described as a principal historical mechanism. Senior officials and Ministers also bear reputational consequences for subordinates' misconduct (ARC National Colloquium Annexure I(2)).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: informal social disapproval.
- Accountability: senior reputational responsibility for subordinates.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (ARC Annexure I(2)). No independent verification in the owner.
**Links:** ETH-6025.
**Trap:** Presenting Japan as a legal-sanction model. The ARC stresses social disapproval.
**Mains hook:** Reputational accountability as a complement to penal sanction. Qualified: it rests on the ARC's account.
**Back:** *"Shame before statute: Japan's lesson is reputational."*

### Flashcard ETH-6025 — Three anti-corruption design philosophies
**Front:** What three distinct anti-corruption design philosophies do Hong Kong's ICAC, Singapore's CPIB and Korea's 1975 reform represent?
**Definition (learn):** Each model changes a different variable: social norms (Hong Kong), the price of corruption (Singapore), or the reach of consequences (Korea).
**Breakdown:**
- Hong Kong ICAC: attitude change and social norm; enforcement plus prevention plus education.
- Singapore CPIB: deterrent penalty plus asset confiscation.
- Korea 1975: extended liability and reputational consequence (family and recommender, per ARC account).
- Japan (ETH-6024) is an optional fourth informal model.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 basic Sec. 3 and Sec. 9; advanced Sec. 3.
**Links:** ETH-6018, ETH-6019, ETH-6021, ETH-6024, ETH-6027.
**Trap:** Ranking the three by "least corrupt" rather than naming the mechanism. Korea's family element is uncorroborated.
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark comparison: name the mechanism of each, then qualify adaptability to India. Qualified: each model carries context-specific trade-offs.
**Back:** *"Norm, price, kin: Hong Kong changes minds, Singapore prices risk, Korea spreads blame."*

### Flashcard ETH-6026 — Finland vs Thailand: culture and statute versus penalty
**Front:** Why do Finland and Thailand together undercut both "penalty severity alone" and "statute precision alone" theories?
**Definition (learn):** Finland combines a high CPI score with no special anti-corruption statute; Thailand combines severe penalties with a low score. The owner infers that institutional culture and rule-of-law strength matter more than either lever alone.
**Breakdown:**
- Finland: 88/100, rank 2; no special statute; bribery in Criminal Code (⚠️ owner).
- Thailand: 33/100, rank 116; severe penalties (⚠️ owner).
- Inference: culture, transparency and enforcement quality are decisive complements (⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia (CPI 2025 scores and ranks); ✅ owner 23 advanced Sec. 3.
**Links:** ETH-6022, ETH-6023, ETH-6025.
**Trap:** "Harsher penalties produce lower corruption" (refuted by Thailand). "No anti-corruption statute means no bribery offence" (refuted by Finland).
**Mains hook:** Qualified: a cross-country correlation does not establish causation.
**Back:** *"Finland: no special statute, strong culture; Thailand: strong penalty, weak result."*

### Flashcard ETH-6027 — Vivekananda's selective adaptation applied to ICAC
**Front:** How does the selective-adaptation principle from the Human Values owner apply when borrowing Hong Kong's ICAC model?
**Definition (learn):** "Learn... but do not become others" (owner 02): borrow the method, adapt the scale. The ICAC's public-education intensity presumes a compact city-state; India's continental scale and federal structure point to state- or district-level campaigns instead of one national campaign.
**Breakdown:**
- Adaptation required: the intensity and geography of public education.
- Institution to borrow: the three-pronged method.
- Caution: adaptation needs local capacity (⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 advanced Sec. 3 (Vivekananda caution) and link to `02`.
**Links:** ETH-6018, ETH-6025, ETH-6054.
**Trap:** Copying a city-state model verbatim, or rejecting it outright.
**Mains hook:** Name the specific India adaptation. Qualified: adaptation is itself a design choice.
**Back:** *"Learn the method, not the map: ICAC's education scaled down to districts."*

### Flashcard ETH-6028 — Korea's family-liability model: a boundary case
**Front:** What ethical concern does Korea's extended-family liability raise for any Indian adaptation?
**Definition (learn):** Collective or vicarious liability imposed on relatives who committed no wrongdoing. The owner suggests narrower mechanisms, such as enhanced scrutiny of family assets, rather than automatic employment bans on relatives.
**Breakdown:**
- Deterrence gain: a strong deterrent.
- Due-process cost: punishment of innocent kin.
- Narrower alternative: targeted asset scrutiny.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Owner analysis (advanced Sec. 5). The underlying Korean claim is uncorroborated (ETH-6021).
**Links:** ETH-6021, ETH-6025.
**Trap:** Presenting family liability as a model to copy without its due-process cost.
**Mains hook:** Deterrence versus collective liability. Qualified: any adaptation requires due-process safeguards.
**Back:** *"Target the wrongdoer, not the kin."*

### Flashcard ETH-6029 — Vishaka & Ors v. State of Rajasthan (1997): interim judicial guidelines
**Front:** What did Vishaka & Ors v. State of Rajasthan (1997) lay down on workplace sexual harassment?
**Definition (learn):** The first binding judicial guidelines requiring employers to prevent, prohibit and redress workplace sexual harassment, treating it as a violation of Articles 14, 15, 19(1)(g) and 21 (the "Vishaka Guidelines").
**Breakdown:**
- Citation: (1997) 6 SCC 241; AIR 1997 SC 3011.
- Date: decided 13 August 1997.
- Bench: CJI J.S. Verma, Justice Sujata V. Manohar and Justice B.N. Kirpal.
- Status: interim, judicially framed directions "until legislation is enacted"; operative for sixteen years until the 2013 Act.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 23 (Sec. 4.1). Wikipedia fetch for the case returned 404; date and bench are not fetch-verified (⚠️ on bench and date).
**Links:** ETH-6030, ETH-6031.
**Trap:** Calling the guidelines a permanent statutory code.
**Mains hook:** Judicial law-making filling a legislative gap; qualified by its interim status.
**Back:** *"1997 guidelines, sixteen interim years: court-made law until Parliament acted."*

### Flashcard ETH-6030 — Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace Act, 2013: the statutory process
**Front:** What does the Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013 require of employers?
**Definition (learn):** The Act converts the Vishaka Guidelines into statute and mandates an Internal Committee (IC) with prescribed composition and timelines.
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold: IC mandatory in any workplace with ten or more employees.
- Composition: at least half women; one external member from a women's-cause NGO or body.
- Complaint window: written complaint within three months, extendable by three more.
- Inquiry: completed within ninety days.
